# Progress Log

Running log of where this project stands. Update this whenever a milestone step lands or the plan changes — this is the first thing to read after a session restart or a day away, before re-deriving context from scratch.

## M5 Slice 3 — GPU performance validation: first real measurement against the PRD's ≤100ms budget (2026-09-03/04)

Third M5 slice, GPU rendering track. User's explicit choice among four candidates for "what's next" (perf validation vs. extending CPU-fallback feature parity vs. switching M5 tracks vs. Windows/WebView2 validation): measure the interactive Develop render path against MILESTONES.md's own M5 exit criterion, which Slices 1 (CPU fallback) and 2 (CPU/GPU parity harness) had both built safety nets around without ever actually timing. Full reasoning and real numbers in [ADR-0004](docs/adr/ADR-0004-rendering-and-color-management.md)'s new dated update section; no RFC drafted for this one (pure measurement against an existing architecture decision, no real design fork to resolve — matches the M5 RFC/ADR practice's own "use judgment" carve-out).

- **New `recordRenderLatency` instrumentation in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`**: times each reactive render effect's own fire to a real `device.queue.onSubmittedWorkDone()` completion (not a JS-return-time proxy, which would miss GPU-queue backpressure), rolling-buffered onto `window.__developRenderPerf` for e2e to read — the app's first persistent `window.__` debug surface (earlier `window.__gpuDebug` instrumentation, from the prior session's flaky-test investigation, was temporary and removed after use).
- **New `e2e/specs/develop-performance.e2e.js`**: drives five sliders chosen to span this shader's real cost range (Exposure/Contrast/HSL — global grade only; Sharpen/Luminance-NR — the separable H+V blur passes, this pipeline's heaviest per-pixel work) on one accumulating stack, asserts every sample stays under the PRD's 100ms budget. **Real measured numbers on this dev machine** (current-generation hardware, not the PRD's actual 5-year-old-mid-range target — a pass here is real evidence of headroom, not final confirmation of the PRD figure itself): Exposure 25ms, Contrast 22ms, HSL green-band saturation 20ms, Sharpen 3ms, Luminance NR 2ms. All comfortably under budget; the two blur passes measuring cheapest despite looking heaviest on paper is noted but not chased further (likely GPU/driver warm-up from scenario ordering, doesn't threaten the budget either direction).
- **A real, previously-undiscovered SQLite performance bug, found while validating the OTHER half of the exit criterion** ("...on a 50k-image catalog..."). The interactive render loop is architecturally decoupled from catalog size by ADR-0004's own design (never touches the catalog), so the real catalog-scale risk is the persistence path, not the shader — validated via a new `#[ignore]`-gated `catalog_scales_to_50k_images` Rust test (`cargo test --release -- --ignored --nocapture catalog_scales_to_50k_images`) seeding a real 50,000-row in-memory catalog. First run (before any fix): `list_images()` — the Library grid's own full-catalog query — took **37.4s** (release build), ~75x over a 500ms budget. Root cause, confirmed via `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` (`SCAN image_versions` in the query plan): `image_versions` had no index on `image_id`, the column `list_images()`'s per-row correlated subquery filters on — every one of the 50,000 outer rows triggered a full table scan of `image_versions`. Added `idx_image_versions_image_id` to `catalog.rs`'s `migrate()`; re-ran the identical test: **75ms**. Re-confirmed the fix mattered (not a fluke) by temporarily reverting the index and reproducing the 37.4s result again before re-applying it. `record_edit_stack`/`get_edit_stack` (both already indexed-primary-key lookups) were fast before and after (0ms at this scale) — included in the same test as a permanent three-way regression guard, not because they were ever actually at risk.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings across 364 files. 100/100 Vitest, unchanged (no new pure function — the perf instrumentation is orchestration, matching this file's own established no-dedicated-test convention for component-level glue). 255/255 Rust tests + 1 newly-added `#[ignore]`d test (run explicitly, confirmed passing at 75ms/0ms/0ms above — not part of the default fast suite since seeding 50k rows takes real wall time, ~1s in release, ~38s if it ever needs to reproduce the pre-fix regression). New e2e spec: 1/1 passing in isolation. Re-ran `golden-path.e2e.js` (3/3) and `develop-gpu-fallback.e2e.js` (3/3, including yesterday's flaky-fix test) afterward in isolation — both still passing, confirming no interaction with the new spec's shared fixture or the schema change.

## Fix: develop-gpu-fallback.e2e.js's deterministic "re-renders" failure (2026-09-03)

Follow-up to the bug M5 Slice 2's own entry below flagged and explicitly left unfixed: `develop-gpu-fallback.e2e.js`'s "re-renders the fallback preview (debounced) after an Exposure edit" test failed deterministically with `expect(received).not.toBeNull()`, reproduced on a clean `main` at Slice 1's own merge commit (fecd615) — unrelated to Slice 2.

- **Root cause, confirmed by temporarily instrumenting `+page.svelte`'s `gpuFallbackTimer` effect** (pushed a trace of every effect-run/timer-fire/resolve/reject into `window.__gpuDebug`, dumped from the spec on failure): same catalog-accumulation caveat `golden-path.e2e.js` and `develop-step-nudge.e2e.js` already document — this fixture's edit stack persists across local runs (content-hash dedupe on re-import), and the test always drove Exposure to the same hardcoded literal `1.5`. A repeat local run found Exposure already left at exactly `1.5` by the PRIOR run, so `upsertOp`'s filter-then-push produced a byte-identical `EditStack` (the op was already last in the array) — same JSON, same blake3 stack-hash, same cache key `ensure_graded_preview_for_hash` (`preview_cache.rs`) already had an `out_path` for. That function short-circuits on an existing `out_path` instead of re-rendering, so `previewEditStack` resolved with the exact same cached PNG path as before the edit — a real debounced round-trip happened, `.cpu-fallback-image`'s `src` just never changed because the output genuinely was identical.
- **Fix, in the test only** (no app-code change — the cache short-circuit is correct, desirable behavior, not a bug): compute the target Exposure value relative to whatever's currently there (`currentExposure >= 4 ? currentExposure - 2 : currentExposure + 2`), the exact pattern `golden-path.e2e.js`'s own Exposure test already uses for this identical reason, instead of a hardcoded literal.
- **Verification**: rebuilt the debug e2e binary, ran the spec 4x back-to-back against the same (deliberately re-accumulating) local catalog. The first run hit one `expect(...).not.toBeNull()` failure on the target test, immediately followed on its automatic retries by cascading `ECONNREFUSED`/"tauri-driver not found" errors — the exact signature of M4.5 Slice 7's own documented "WebDriver session degrades after repeated rapid local invocations" flakiness (this machine had already rebuilt/re-run this suite many times in the preceding investigation), not a repeat of the hash-collision bug. The next 3 runs (2, 3, 4) each passed clean, 3/3, zero retries, confirming the fix holds across genuinely accumulated catalog state.

## M5 Slice 2 — Automated CPU↔GPU Develop parity harness (2026-09-02)

Second M5 slice. Reviewing Slice 1, the user observed that this app's "apply an edit stack to pixels" math has two independent implementations — `develop_engine.rs` (Rust CPU, ~5,300 lines) and `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s WGSL shader (~1,600 lines) — with the only prior safety net being `m1-slice3-smoke/+page.svelte`, a manual M1-era dev page never run by any test suite, covering ~40 lines of a shader that's since grown 40x. Two research passes found the duplication currently disciplined (not silently diverged) but confirmed the automated-check gap was real; native `wgpu` unification (ADR-0004's deferred "option A") was set aside for now — its biggest open risk (CI GPU-adapter reliability) can't even be validated while this repo's CI is blocked on an unrelated billing issue. Chosen instead: an e2e-based parity harness reusing entirely existing infrastructure (no new Rust, no new dependency).

- **New `app/e2e/specs/develop-cpu-gpu-parity.e2e.js`**: an accumulating 6-scenario spec (Exposure, Contrast, White Balance, HSL green-band saturation, a Tone Curve point, Split Toning) that fetches the REAL live edit stack via `get_edit_stack`, renders it through `preview_edit_stack` (CPU) and reads a pixel back via `fetch`+`createImageBitmap`+`OffscreenCanvas`, and separately reads the same normalized coordinate off the live GPU canvas via the existing eyedropper/hover-pixel mechanism (`reportHoverPixel`, `.hover-rgb`) — comparing the two within a documented tolerance.
- **Three real test-harness bugs found and fixed while building this**, each initially producing a false failure that looked like a catastrophic rendering bug before being root-caused: (1) WebdriverIO's `expect()` doesn't accept a Vitest-style second message argument — switched to plain `if/throw`. (2) `get_edit_stack` read immediately after a slider edit raced `+page.svelte`'s 250ms-debounced catalog flush (the in-memory `editStack` driving the GPU render updates synchronously, persistence doesn't) — fixed by polling until the persisted stack actually changes. (3) The fixture's catalog row isn't reset between local e2e runs (an established pattern elsewhere in this suite) — an early version reset it via `set_edit_stack` in `before()`, but placed the call *after* `openDevelopFor`, so Develop had already loaded the stale pre-reset stack into memory; the next edit re-persisted all of it, silently clobbering the reset. Moving the reset before `openDevelopFor` fixed it.
- **A genuinely misleading dead end, resolved by direct pixel inspection rather than more code changes**: with the above three fixed, every scenario still showed CPU reading pure white and GPU reading pure black at every sampled coordinate — traced (via a WebDriver screenshot of the live canvas, not just programmatic pixel reads) to the harness bug above having left ~12 accumulated ops (including several this spec never touches) on the fixture, extreme enough that the live GPU canvas was **genuinely** rendering solid black — a real, if extreme-edge-case, finding, not a test artifact. Confirmed screenshot-for-screenshot once the reset-ordering fix landed.
- **A second, smaller and more interesting divergence, root-caused down to test-patch selection, not a rendering bug**: HSL/Split Toning failed by 30-40/255 at the originally-planned "flat green field" patch. Direct pixel-neighborhood inspection (an 11×11-window per-channel stdev check) showed that patch was actually individual corn stalks/leaves/shadow gaps (stdev ~30-40), not flat at all — `fs_lens_correct`/`fs_perspective` do genuine bilinear `textureSample` resampling on *every* render, even with zero lens/perspective correction dialed in (no "skip this pass" branch — `fs_grade` always reads `perspectiveCorrectedTex`, never the raw uploaded texture), so any fraction-of-a-texel difference from CPU's exact nearest-pixel read blends in whatever the neighborhood looks like. A genuinely flat, correctly-hued replacement coordinate (stdev ~3-6) cut the divergence by ~60-75%, and the small remainder (an inherent floating-point-precision property of exact-vs-bilinear sampling through two passthrough passes, already implicitly accepted elsewhere in `fs_perspective`'s own doc comment for a related reason) is covered by a separate, wider, explicitly-documented tolerance for just the two hue-reading ops — the other 4 scenarios keep the original tight ±4.
- **Verification-of-the-verifier, per this slice's own plan**: temporarily broke the WGSL contrast formula's divisor (100→50) and re-ran — 2 of 6 scenarios failed loudly with large margins (24 vs. tolerance 20, 49 vs. tolerance 20), confirming the harness actually catches a real injected divergence, not just green-by-construction. Reverted before committing.
- **A separate, unrelated pre-existing bug found and NOT fixed here** (out of this slice's scope, flagged as its own follow-up task): `develop-gpu-fallback.e2e.js`'s "re-renders the fallback preview (debounced) after an Exposure edit" test fails deterministically (`expect(received).not.toBeNull()`), reproduced on a completely clean `main` at Slice 1's own merge commit, unrelated to any change in this slice.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings (362 files, no production code touched). Vitest/Rust unchanged (no production code touched). New spec: 6/6 passing (one run needed its built-in retry due to an unrelated transient WebDriver flake, matching this suite's existing `specFileRetries: 3`). `golden-path.e2e.js` re-run afterward: still 3/3, confirming no interaction with this spec's catalog/state.

## M5 Slice 1 — Seamless CPU fallback for Develop's interactive canvas (2026-09-02)

First M5 slice. `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s `initGpu()` previously threw straight into a permanent `status = "error"` whenever `navigator.gpu` was absent or device/adapter acquisition failed — Develop became entirely unusable. This directly contradicted M5's own scope line ("...with correct, seamless CPU fallback") and a real, previously-undocumented-as-urgent risk ADR-0004 already names: WebView2 (Windows) WebGPU support has never actually been spike-tested, only WKWebView (macOS) has. Full design in [RFC-0002](docs/rfc/RFC-0002-develop-gpu-cpu-fallback.md); shipped-decision summary appended to [ADR-0004](docs/adr/ADR-0004-rendering-and-color-management.md).

- **No new Rust command** — reuses `preview_edit_stack` (M4.5 Slice 5's CPU graded-preview pipeline, `develop_engine.rs` under the hood) as the fallback's primary canvas content, the same command that already backs History/Snapshot/Preset hover-preview.
- **Detection is scoped narrowly**: only `initGpu`'s own three device-acquisition throw sites (no `navigator.gpu`, null adapter, `requestDevice()` rejection) route to the new `status = "cpu-fallback"`. A later shader-compile failure or `uncapturederror` — both meaning WebGPU itself works fine — keep surfacing as `status = "error"`, unchanged, so a real app/shader regression is never silently masked as an environment gap. New pure `gpuFallback.js`'s `classifyGpuFailure(error)` does this classification, independently Vitest-covered.
- **`+page.svelte` computes the fallback preview**, not `DevelopCanvas.svelte` itself — a new debounced (250ms, matching `flushEditStack`'s "settled after a drag" window) `$effect` calling `previewEditStack(path, contentHash, editStack)` whenever `gpuFallbackActive` is true, mirroring the exact shape already established for `softProofPreviewUrl`. `DevelopCanvas` only reports GPU availability upward via a new `onGpuFallback(active)` callback and separately fetches the source's true (unedited) pixel dimensions via `getDevelopPreview` for `onSourceDimensions`.
- **A distinct `status` value does most of the template gating for free**: introducing `"cpu-fallback"` (rather than reusing `"ready"` plus a side flag) means every existing `status === "ready"`-gated block — zoom badge, mask-overlay div (and every mask handle), committed-crop preview, histogram/eyedropper/before-after machinery — automatically doesn't render in fallback mode, with zero additional gating code. The real `<canvas>` (never WebGPU-configured in this mode) is hidden via CSS; a new template block shows the rendered `<img>` (or a "Rendering preview…" placeholder before the first debounced render resolves, avoiding a blank-canvas flash) plus a persistent on-canvas banner.
- **`MaskToolStrip.svelte`** gains a `gpuUnavailable` prop, OR'd into every tool button's (Crop + all 7 mask tools) existing `disabled` condition — same per-button pattern the "at mask cap" gate already uses. Masks/crop already baked into a stack from a prior GPU session still render correctly (the CPU engine applies them faithfully); only *creating/adjusting* new geometry is blocked, since that interactive geometry (brush rasterization, crop-handle drag math) is built against the WebGPU canvas's backing store and doesn't apply to a static `<img>`.
- **v1 non-goals, explicit**: native `wgpu`/CPU-GPU unification (ADR-0004's already-deferred option A), real Windows/WebView2 hardware validation (no Windows machine in this environment), mask/crop creation, 100%-zoom tier, live histogram, eyedropper, before/after toggle in fallback mode — all disabled with a stated reason, not silently broken.
- **Verified for real, not mocked**: this dev machine has working WebGPU, so `navigator.gpu` was monkeypatched out via `Object.defineProperty(navigator, "gpu", { value: undefined, configurable: true })` — confirmed empirically to work cleanly against the actual WKWebView build on the first attempt (no non-configurable-property obstacle here, unlike `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__`'s own precedent). New `app/e2e/specs/develop-gpu-fallback.e2e.js` run against a freshly rebuilt e2e-featured `.app`: 3/3 passing — fallback banner + rendered `<img>` with real pixel content appear, the real `<canvas>` is confirmed hidden (not just covered), every mask/crop tool button is disabled, and an Exposure edit produces a genuinely different rendered image after the debounce window. `golden-path.e2e.js` re-run afterward in isolation (no `navigator.gpu` patch) — still 3/3 passing, confirming the normal GPU path is completely unaffected by this slice.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings. 100/100 Vitest (4 new, `gpuFallback.test.js`). 255/255 Rust, unchanged (no Rust file touched — `preview_edit_stack` reused as-is).

## M4.5 Slice 7 — Step-nudge (up/down) slider controls (2026-08-31)

Seventh and final M4.5 slice: every Develop slider (all 45 in `DevelopPanel.svelte`, all 11 in `MaskEditorPanel.svelte`) gained a small ▲/▼ button pair next to its value display, for exact single-step adjustment beyond drag precision — completes M4.5's own exit criteria ("Every Develop slider supports fine step-nudge adjustment via up/down controls, not drag-only").

- **New `stepMath.js`**, alongside this project's existing `cropMath.js`/`histogramMath.js` pure-math-module convention: `nudgeValue(value, direction, step, min, max)` clamps and rounds to `step`'s own decimal precision. The rounding matters for real reasons, not just tidiness — Exposure's step is 0.05 and Perspective Rotate's is 0.1, and plain floating-point addition drifts (`0.1 + 0.05 === 0.15000000000000002`); repeated nudges without rounding would visibly accumulate garbage digits. 8 new Vitest cases, including that exact regression.
- **One `{#snippet stepButtons(...)}` per component** (`DevelopPanel.svelte` and `MaskEditorPanel.svelte` each define their own copy — they share no parent module, so duplicating ~20 lines was simpler than inventing a shared import just for this), rendered once per slider row via `{@render stepButtons(value, step, min, max, onChange)}`. Deliberately NOT a new generic `<Slider>` component wrapping all 56 rows — that would have meant rewriting every row's markup (each has small per-row differences: optional handlers, sign-prefixed value formatting, disabled-gated rows for Lens Corrections) for a single-slice feature; the snippet adds the new behavior without touching how any existing row already works. Buttons are `tabindex="-1"` (the input itself stays the tab stop) and individually `disabled` at each bound, matching `nudgeValue`'s own clamp so a maxed-out slider visibly can't be nudged further.
- **Lens Corrections' three profile-gated sliders (Distortion/Vignetting/CA) get an extra `disabledExtra` param**: their existing range inputs are already `disabled={!lensCorrection.profile_enabled}`, and the step buttons needed the same gate — otherwise a user could nudge a value hidden behind a disabled-looking control.
- **Verified via a real DOM click, not just existence**: `e2e/specs/develop-step-nudge.e2e.js` opens Develop on a fixture and calls `.step-btn[aria-label="Increase"|"Decrease"].click()` directly (a plain button click needed none of golden-path's own documented double-click/keyboard-focus workarounds), asserting the slider's real value after each click — covers a fractional-step slider (Exposure), a whole-number one (Contrast), and clamping-with-disabled at the max bound.
- **A real bug caught only by running the new spec together with the others, not in isolation**: the first version's "nudges Exposure" test read a `before` value and asserted `before + 0.05` without accounting for catalog accumulation across local runs (same class of caveat golden-path.e2e.js already documents) — a prior run's own "clamps at max" test had left the fixture's Exposure pinned at +5, so `before` was already 5, and the naive assertion expected an unclamped 5.05 instead of the correctly-clamped 5. Fixed by explicitly resetting Exposure/Contrast to 0 via the native property setter in the spec's own `before()` hook, rather than trusting whatever a previous run left behind.
- **A separate, NOT-fixed finding: whichever test attempt runs 3rd in a tight back-to-back sequence reliably times out**, reproduced 4+ times across two investigation passes -- `WebDriverError: Request timed out! ... when running "execute/sync"`, at a suspiciously consistent ~5m10s-5m27s (close to this config's `connectionRetryCount: 3` × `connectionRetryTimeout: 90000`, suggesting a broken connection being retried 3 times rather than a genuinely slow command). One correction to an assumption from the first investigation pass: `npm run test:e2e` launches a SINGLE app process for the entire run, not one per spec file (confirmed by polling `lsof -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN` every 20s through a full run -- the same PID/port persisted unchanged across all 3 specs; the `#0-0`/`#0-1`/`#0-2` reporter labels are per-spec-file labels within one session, not separate launches). Despite that, a completely fresh, isolated single-spec run of panel-resize ALSO failed identically when it happened to be the 3rd invocation in a rapid manual sequence that session -- so it isn't purely "one process degrading after N minutes of live use" either. Ruled out via direct testing: leftover/zombie processes (`ps`/`lsof` confirmed clean before each attempt), orphaned WebKit XPC helpers (`ps aux` showed none tied to recent runs), WebKit disk cache bloat (deleted `~/Library/Caches/{emulsion,dev.alfredwei.emulsion}/WebKit` entirely -- ~75MB/13MB of accumulated NetworkCache blobs from many repeated local runs -- and reran; identical failure), and SQLite catalog/WAL bloat (only 6 images / 167 history rows on disk, too small to explain a multi-minute stall). Root cause still unknown; matters going forward because CI's `e2e` job globs every spec file into one run, but this repo's CI is currently blocked on an unrelated GitHub Actions billing issue and has never actually run this suite on a genuinely fresh runner, so it's not yet known whether this is a real product/harness bug or an artifact of this one dev machine having run the debug binary 15-20+ times in rapid succession today. Flagged as a follow-up task with the full investigation trail rather than continuing to chase it here.
- **Third investigation pass on the above, still NOT fixed, but the failure mode is now much better localized.** CI re-checked first and is still blocked on the same GitHub Actions billing issue (`gh run view` on the open PR: every job fails in ~5s with "recent account payments have failed" — confirmed unchanged from the prior pass, so CI-based confirmation remains unavailable). Locally: temporarily dropped `connectionRetryCount` from 3 to 1 in `wdio.conf.js` to test the "one stuck command retried 3× accounts for the ~5min wall time" theory from the prior pass. It doesn't hold up — a first full 3-spec run with `count: 1` completed clean in under 4 minutes with **zero** timeouts of any kind (same spec order as always: develop-step-nudge, golden-path, panel-resize), directly contradicting a *deterministic* "3rd spec always times out." A second run immediately after, back-to-back, with the identical config and identical spec order, reproduced the timeout on panel-resize (the 3rd spec) again — so it's reproducible, but tied to repeated back-to-back invocations within a session rather than spec position alone, consistent with the prior pass's other data point (an isolated single-spec run failing only when it was 3rd in a rapid manual sequence). Timestamps from that second run pin down *where* it breaks: the normal `ensureActiveWindowFocus` polling WARN (documented above, fires every ~5.5s) ran regularly for the first ~78s of the panel-resize spec, then one command hung for exactly 90.0s (`connectionRetryTimeout`) before failing, and every command for the rest of that spec file failed the same way — so the session runs fine for a while and then goes permanently unresponsive mid-spec, not at spec start, and it isn't "one slow op retried" but "the whole session died and every subsequent command pays the full timeout once." Checked `log show` (`com.apple.xpc`/`launchd`/`jetsam`/`memorystatus` predicates, plus the app/WebKit process names directly) for the exact hang window (13:47:50–13:49:35) and `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports`: nothing logged, no crash report — rules out both the OS-level XPC-respawn-throttling theory and an app crash from the prior pass's follow-up list; whatever hangs isn't visible to the OS as a fault, memory-pressure event, or process death, which points more toward a stuck IPC/WebDriver transport (embedded `tauri-driver` or `wdio-webdriver` plugin) than an OS scheduling effect. `connectionRetryCount` reverted back to 3 (no net config change) since reducing it didn't prevent the failure, only changed how visible the retries were. **Separately, a real reproducible bug distinct from the timeout was found along the way**: golden-path's own "exports the develop image" test failed with a plain assertion failure (`expect(versionId).not.toBeNull()`, i.e. `list_images` had no match for `FIXTURE_PATH`) — not a timeout — in both back-to-back runs, specifically whenever golden-path ran as the 2nd spec (after develop-step-nudge) rather than 1st; likely catalog/localStorage cross-spec-file state leakage (same class of issue golden-path.e2e.js and develop-step-nudge.e2e.js already work around for *repeated runs* of the same spec, but apparently not yet for *other specs* running first in the same invocation). Not root-caused here — flagged as its own follow-up, separate from the timeout. Next directions if this gets picked up again: instrument the app side (the `wdio-webdriver` feature's Rust code) to log around the hang window instead of relying on the OS log, since the OS log came back empty; or try `driverProvider` options other than `embedded` to see if the failure is specific to that in-process driver.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` unchanged (same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors), 96/96 Vitest (8 new), 255/255 Rust (untouched — pure frontend feature). New spec passes 3/3 in isolation and paired with golden-path; full 3-spec suite currently cannot complete in one run for the infra reason above.

## M4.5 Slice 6 — Resizable Develop panels (2026-08-31)

Sixth M4.5 slice: the Develop module's History rail (left) and adjustments panel (right) had fixed pixel widths (200px / 240px). Both are now drag-resizable via a thin divider on each side, with the chosen widths persisted across restarts.

- **Persistence is plain `localStorage`, not the SQLite `settings` table**: this project's only prior key-value persistence precedent (`BackupSettings`) is scoped per-catalog-file, but panel width is a machine-level UI preference that should stay put across different catalogs — same reasoning already established for `shortcuts.js`'s keyboard-shortcut storage. New `panelLayout.js` holds the clamp bounds (History 160–400px, adjustments 200–480px, defaults 200/240 matching the old hardcoded values) and the get/save helpers, both try/catch-wrapped around `localStorage`.
- **Drag implementation reuses the existing pointer-drag skeleton** already established by `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s crop handles and `ToneCurveEditor.svelte`'s curve points: `pointerdown` records `{ which, startX, startWidth }` and best-effort `setPointerCapture`; `pointermove` computes a delta from the recorded start (sign flipped for the right-hand handle, since dragging it left grows the panel) and clamps into `panelWidths` state; `pointerup` best-effort releases capture and persists to `localStorage`. `HistoryPanel.svelte`/`DevelopPanel.svelte` each gained a `width` prop (defaulting to their old hardcoded values) driving an inline `style="width: {width}px"`, replacing the CSS rule that used to hardcode it.
- **Computer-use (screenshot + click-drag) could not reliably grab a 1px-wide handle**: several `left_click_drag` attempts at recalibrated x-coordinates either did nothing or landed on the canvas underneath (visible via an eyedropper readout appearing instead of a resize). Screenshot/zoom-based pixel calibration isn't precise enough for a target this thin, so verification for this slice shifted to a dedicated e2e spec instead, which can dispatch pointer events at a DOM element's own measured center with no coordinate guessing — first time this project has substituted e2e for computer-use as the primary verification method rather than a supplement to it.
- **Two new bugs, both caught only by the e2e spec and both in the test, not the app**: (1) reading `getBoundingClientRect()` in the same synchronous script tick as the pointer-event dispatch raced Svelte's reactive DOM update — a `$state` write is synchronous but the resulting DOM paint lands on the next frame, so the very first run of the assertion read the pre-drag width. Fixed with `await new Promise(resolve => requestAnimationFrame(resolve))` before reading. (2) `localStorage`, like the SQLite catalog, persists on disk across separate `npm run test:e2e` invocations in this WKWebView setup — an isolated re-run after the above fix still failed because the "before" width was already elevated from the previous run's leftover drag. Fixed by explicitly clearing the `emulsion_develop_panel_widths_v1` key in the spec's `before()` hook, before the page reload (state only reads from storage at component init).
- **`e2e/helpers.js`** (new): pulled `findCellByName`/`clickEl`/`openDevelopFor` out of `golden-path.e2e.js` into a shared module now used by both specs — no behavior change to golden-path, confirmed by re-running it after the extraction (still 3/3).
- **Verification**: `npm run check` unchanged (same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors), 88/88 Vitest, 255/255 Rust. New `e2e/specs/panel-resize.e2e.js` (drag both handles and assert exact resulting widths + persisted `localStorage` values; drag far past each bound and assert clamping) — 2/2 passing in isolation, and re-confirmed passing together with `golden-path.e2e.js` in one full from-scratch `npm run test:e2e` run (rebuilt debug binary, both specs via the `./specs/**/*.e2e.js` glob): 3 passing (golden-path) + 2 passing (panel-resize), no interaction between the two specs' catalog/localStorage state. Also visually sanity-checked via computer-use that the new dividers render correctly with no layout regression, before pivoting to e2e for the actual drag verification.

## E2E UI test infrastructure: WebdriverIO + embedded Tauri WebDriver (2026-08-31)

Every feature so far had been verified by hand (build a debug `.app`, drive it with computer-use, write up what was checked) — thorough but non-repeating; nothing stopped a later change from silently breaking something verified slices ago. This adds a real scripted equivalent that drives the actual packaged app (real Rust backend, real SQLite catalog, real file I/O), plus one golden-path test proving the whole pipeline: Import → Library → Develop → Export.

- **Tool choice**: `@wdio/tauri-service`'s **embedded** driver mode, not the classic `tauri-driver`. The classic driver only ever supported Windows (WebView2/msedgedriver) and Linux (webkit2gtk-driver) — no WebDriver for WKWebView exists, so it can never run on this macOS dev machine. Embedded mode runs a WebDriver HTTP server *inside* the app via a small Rust plugin (`tauri-plugin-wdio-webdriver`), so it works natively on macOS/Windows/Linux with no external driver and no paid service.
- **The plugin is gated behind a Cargo feature (`wdio-webdriver`), not `cfg(debug_assertions)`**: tried the debug-only approach the plugin's own docs suggest first (`[target.'cfg(debug_assertions)'.dependencies]`) — Cargo explicitly warns this selector isn't supported for dependency selection (it only understands real platform cfgs like `unix`/`windows`, not build-profile flags), so the dependency would have silently shipped in every build, debug and release alike. A normal optional dependency behind a Cargo feature (`--features wdio-webdriver`, only ever passed by `npm run test:e2e`) is the actual working equivalent — confirmed excluded from a real release build via `cargo tree --release`.
- **The matching capability + `withGlobalTauri` are e2e-only too, via a separate `tauri.e2e.conf.json` merged in with `--config`**: a capability file placed directly in `capabilities/` gets validated by Tauri's build script *unconditionally*, regardless of whether it's referenced in `security.capabilities` — so a stray `capabilities/e2e.json` broke the default build outright (`Permission wdio-webdriver:default not found`) even though nothing referenced it. Fixed by pinning the base `tauri.conf.json` to `"capabilities": ["default"]` and inlining the `e2e` capability (plus `"withGlobalTauri": true`) directly inside `tauri.e2e.conf.json`'s override instead of as a file in the `capabilities/` directory — it then only exists at all when that override is explicitly merged in.
- **Native OS file/folder pickers cannot be driven by WebDriver at all** (they're outside the webview), and the natural fix — mocking `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog`'s `open()` by patching `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke` — does not work: Tauri v2 defines that property (and the `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` object itself on `window`) as non-configurable, confirmed by both a direct reassignment and a `Proxy` substitution silently no-op'ing. The real fix requires the separate `tauri-plugin-wdio` plugin + its `@wdio/tauri-plugin` frontend import (explicitly out of scope for this slice — only the embedded-server plugin was planned). Instead, `golden-path.e2e.js` calls the exact same Tauri commands the dialog-gated buttons would eventually call (`import_files`, `export_images`) directly via `window.__TAURI__.core.invoke` (reachable because `withGlobalTauri` is on for e2e builds) — real backend commands, same catalog writes, same file I/O, just without the OS chrome. Every other step (opening Develop via the Loupe view's "Develop →" button, editing Exposure, the Export button's own enablement) drives the real UI.
- **Two other WebKit/embedded-WebDriver quirks, both worked around in the spec, not the app**: (1) a real `.doubleClick()` (two synthetic pointer actions) doesn't reliably land inside WKWebView's native double-click timing window, so `ondblclick` never fires even though both underlying clicks do — fixed by dispatching a real `dblclick` `MouseEvent` directly via `el.dispatchEvent()`. (2) keyboard events (`browser.keys()`) sent after clicking a range input don't reliably reach it as real OS-level focus in this environment — fixed by setting the value through the native `HTMLInputElement` property setter and dispatching a real `input` event, which is what Svelte's `oninput` listens for either way.
- **A real, non-hang per-command cost, not fixed**: `@wdio/tauri-service`'s own internal window-focus bookkeeping (`ensureActiveWindowFocus`) polls for `tauri-plugin-wdio` (the plugin this slice deliberately didn't install) before every single command, retrying for 5s each time it's absent, logged as a `WARN` rather than failing. Inflates a 3-step test to 2-3 minutes wall time but doesn't break anything; `wdio.conf.js`'s mocha timeout is raised to account for it, with a comment explaining why.
- **`app/e2e/`**: `wdio.conf.js` (embedded provider, points at the debug binary) + `specs/golden-path.e2e.js`. `npm run test:e2e` builds the e2e-featured debug binary then runs the suite. New CI job `e2e` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, same macOS/Windows matrix as `rust-core`.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` (2 pre-existing unrelated errors, confirmed via `git diff` untouched by this change), 88/88 Vitest, 255/255 Rust — all unaffected (only `lib.rs`'s plugin registration and two new config files touched on the Rust side). `cargo tree --release` confirms `tauri-plugin-wdio-webdriver` is absent from a release build. `npm run test:e2e` passes all 3 steps locally against a real rebuilt debug binary.

## M4.5 Slice 5 — History/Snapshot/Preset hover-preview + Presets relocated (2026-08-30)

Fifth M4.5 slice: hovering a History row, Snapshot, or Preset in the Develop left rail shows a small rendered thumbnail of that entry's resulting look, pinned at the top of the rail; History and Presets are now separate tabs (Snapshots stays grouped under the History tab).

### Follow-up: Apply Preset / Paste Settings weren't rejoining the Library thumbnail refresh pipeline (2026-08-30)

User feedback: "when dev settings update, the thumbnail should also join refresh pipeline." Auditing every immediate (non-slider-debounced) Develop commit path found `restoreTo` (History jump/undo/redo) and `handleRestoreSnapshot` already call `regenerateThumbnailFor` right after their write lands, but `handleApplyPreset` and `handlePasteSettings` -- the same class of "jump to a different look in one shot" commit, just via this slice's relocated Presets tab / the M4.5 Slice 1 Copy-Paste feature -- did not. Not a bug introduced by this slice (both predate it), but surfaced by it: previously, applying a preset only updated the Library grid/filmstrip thumbnail once the user later left Develop (`switchModule`'s own regen call catches up eventually), which reads as "nothing happened" if they're comparing looks without leaving. Fixed by making both `async` and awaiting their `flushEditStack(...)` before calling `regenerateThumbnailFor(versionId)` -- awaited, not fire-and-forget, matching `restoreTo`'s own documented reasoning (`regenerateThumbnailFor` re-reads the edit stack fresh from the catalog, so it must not race the write it's meant to reflect -- the same unawaited-dependent-IPC-calls hazard class this project has fixed once before, in `openDevelop`).
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings, 88/88 Vitest, 255/255 Rust, all unchanged (pure orchestration ordering fix, no new logic). **Verified interactively** against a rebuilt `.app`: applied "Classic B&W" to a real unedited RAW photo from the relocated Presets tab and confirmed BOTH the Develop filmstrip thumbnail and the Library grid thumbnail turned black & white immediately, without switching modules first (previously would have stayed the old color thumbnail until leaving Develop).

- **Redesigned mid-slice (user feedback) from an initial `editStack`-swap approach**: the first pass swapped the live `editStack` on hover, reusing `DevelopCanvas`'s existing reactive WebGPU render for a free live preview on the main canvas -- correct, but wrong on performance: every hover drove the SAME cost as a real edit, on the app's single most expensive render path, continuously, for as long as the pointer sat over a row. Replaced with a small STATIC thumbnail, rendered via the existing CPU "graded preview" tier (`preview_cache::ensure_graded_preview_for_hash` -- the same draft-resolution, content-hash-and-stack-hash-cached tier Library's own Loupe view already uses), and the main Develop canvas is never touched by a hover at all.
- **Three Rust commands, all `spawn_blocking` + cached like every other preview tier**: `preview_history_entry`/`preview_snapshot` (each merges a read-only stack lookup -- reusing the `Catalog::peek_history_entry`/`peek_snapshot` methods from this slice's first pass, kept as internal helpers -- with a render, into one round trip) and `preview_edit_stack` (renders an arbitrary caller-supplied stack, no catalog lookup -- backs Preset hover, where the frontend already has `PresetEntry.edit_stack` in memory and only needs Rust to merge-and-render it). The earlier JS-facing `peek_history_entry`/`peek_snapshot` commands (which returned a raw `EditStack`, not an image) are gone -- superseded, not kept alongside.
- **`+page.svelte`**: `previewUrl` (a plain `$state<string | null>`) is the ONLY thing hovering touches -- `editStack` itself is never written during a hover, so `restoreTo`/`handleRestoreSnapshot`/`handleApplyPreset` are back to their pre-hover-preview bodies apart from one new `clearPreview()` call at the top of each (clears the stale thumbnail the instant a click actually commits, rather than leaving it until mouseleave -- cosmetic now, not a correctness fix, since there's no `editStack` state left to accidentally leave dangling). All three peek sources (History, Snapshot, and now Preset too, since it also needs a real render) go through the same debounced (120ms) `schedulePreview`, with a `previewToken` to discard a still-in-flight render once the user has moved to a different row.
- **`HistoryPanel.svelte` layout**: a `.preview-box` (dark, `aspect-ratio: 4/3`, `object-fit: contain`) pinned at the very top, then a two-tab strip (History / Presets), then a `.rail-scroll` flex child that's the only part that actually scrolls -- same fixed-header/scrolling-body split `DevelopPanel.svelte`'s own pinned footer already established (M4.5 Slice 1's follow-up). History's tab holds both History and Snapshots (as before); Presets is its own tab, no longer sharing scroll space with a History list that can run up to 100 rows deep.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings across 258 files. 88/88 Vitest, 255/255 Rust (2 kept from the first pass, exercising the still-present `Catalog::peek_history_entry`/`peek_snapshot` methods), both unchanged. Real `cargo build` clean. **Verified interactively** against a rebuilt `.app`: hovered an early "Add Red Eye" History row on a real photo and watched the thumbnail update while the main canvas AND the right-hand Basic panel's live values stayed completely on the current committed state throughout; hovered "Cool Blue" (Presets tab) and watched the thumbnail render after a real few-second first-time decode+grade, then confirmed a second hover of the same preset was instant (cache hit); clicked it and confirmed a real "Apply Preset: Cool Blue" History row committed with the correct values, canvas updated for real, and the thumbnail cleared back to its placeholder; hovered a snapshot and confirmed its own distinct rendered preview.

## M4.5 Slice 4 — Native OS menu bar (2026-08-30)

Fourth M4.5 slice: a real native macOS/Windows menu bar (Emulsion had none before — `grep -rn "tauri::menu\|Menu::"` across `src-tauri/src` returned nothing), wired to the exact same handler functions the existing in-UI buttons already call.

- **`build_menu` in `lib.rs`** (Tauri v2's `tauri::menu` API, which ships in the core `tauri` crate — no separate plugin needed): App menu (macOS only: About, Preferences, Services/Hide/Quit), File (Import Folder…, Import Files…, Export…, Export Contact Sheet PDF…, plus Preferences/Quit folded in on non-macOS where there's no separate App menu), Edit (Undo, Redo, Copy Settings, Paste Settings, Select All, Deselect All), View (Library/Develop/Print module switch, Grid/Loupe/Compare/Survey, Toggle Clipping Overlay, Toggle Before/After), Window (Minimize/Maximize/Close, native predefined items). Every custom item just re-emits its id as a `"menu-action"` event (`.on_menu_event` + `app.emit`) — Rust never interprets the id, matching this file's existing "Rust never interprets `ops`" boundary for edit-stack data.
- **Frontend**: a single `listen("menu-action", ...)` in `+page.svelte`'s `onMount` (same try/catch-outside-Tauri precedent as the existing drag-drop listener) dispatches to `handleMenuAction`, a plain switch that calls the identical functions the in-UI controls already call (`handleImportFolder`, `handleExportClick`, `handleUndo`, `handleCopySettingsRequest`, `switchModule`, etc.) — no new behavior, only a new entry point. Module-scoped actions (Select All, the two Develop-only toggles) no-op outside their module rather than acting on invisible state.
- **Deliberately no accelerator on Undo/Redo/Copy Settings/Paste Settings/Select All/Deselect All**: these already have DOM-level keyboard shortcuts (`handleGlobalKeydown`) that explicitly skip firing while a text field has focus (`isTypingTarget`) — e.g. Cmd+A inside a Caption or Keyword input selects the field's text, not every photo. A native OS menu accelerator is checked by the OS's key-equivalent handling *before* the event ever reaches the webview's DOM (confirmed via macOS's own `performKeyEquivalent:` menu-bar-first dispatch order), bypassing that guard entirely — binding Cmd+A/Cmd+Z at the menu level would have silently broken normal text editing everywhere in the app. The accelerators that ARE set (Import Files ⇧⌘I, Export ⇧⌘E — both matching real Lightroom's own shortcuts, Preferences ⌘,, module switch ⌘1/2/3) don't collide with any text-editing convention.
- **Free native OS behavior, not hand-built**: macOS auto-relabels a "Preferences…"-titled item with the ⌘, accelerator to "Settings…" (Ventura+ convention) regardless of the source string; a menu titled "Edit" automatically gains Writing Tools/AutoFill/Start Dictation/Emoji & Symbols; a menu titled "View" automatically gains "Enter Full Screen". None of this is app code — confirms the menu is genuinely native, not a styled-to-look-native web menu.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings across 258 files (1 new). 88/88 Vitest, 253/253 Rust tests, both unchanged (menu wiring is orchestration, not a pure function — matches this file's own established no-dedicated-test convention for component-level glue). Real `cargo build` clean. **Verified interactively** against a rebuilt `.app` via computer-use, clicking every single item: App > Preferences opens Settings; File > Import Folder/Import Files open the real native picker (Export/Export PDF verified via the titlebar buttons' own precedent); File > Export opens the real ExportDialog; File > Export Contact Sheet PDF correctly no-ops outside Print with nothing loaded; Edit > Undo/Redo round-tripped a real edit stack's Basic-panel values; Edit > Copy Settings → Reset → Edit > Paste Settings correctly restored the exact copied values (Exposure -0.25, Contrast -8, Highlights -9, Shadows +11) on a different photo; Edit > Select All/Deselect All correctly changed the Library selection count; View > Library/Develop/Loupe switched modules/view-modes correctly; View > Toggle Clipping Overlay and Toggle Before/After both visibly changed the Develop canvas; App > Quit closed the app cleanly. **Noted, not fixed (pre-existing, unrelated to this slice)**: while either Import picker's native dialog is open, the titlebar's Import buttons briefly read "Importing…" and the Collections sidebar counts transiently show inflated numbers, self-correcting once the dialog is dismissed — reproduced identically by clicking the pre-existing titlebar "Import Files…" button directly (no menu involved), confirming it predates and is unrelated to this slice's own changes.

## M4.5 Slice 3 — Export dialog progress + reveal on completion (2026-08-30)

Third M4.5 slice: `ExportDialog.svelte` had no per-file feedback (a single opaque "Exporting…" label until the whole batch's one `exportImages(...)` call resolved) and no way to jump to the output folder afterward.

- **Real per-file progress without new backend infrastructure**: confirmed via research that there is zero event-emission plumbing anywhere in this app's Rust core (`app.emit` appears nowhere in `src-tauri/src`) — adding real Tauri events for this would have been new infrastructure just for one dialog. Instead, `handleExport` now calls `exportImages([item], options)` once per item in a sequential `for` loop (not `Promise.all` — matches this app's own established "deliberately sequential, not a worker pool" caution for full-resolution decode work) instead of one bulk call for the whole array. Each Rust round-trip already resolves/returns per image, so the loop gets real progress for free; no Rust changes needed for this part.
- **Progress bar UI**: a `<progress>` element plus "Exporting N of M — filename" label, replacing the static "Exporting…" button state during the run.
- **"Show in file manager when done"** checkbox (default checked): opens the destination folder via a new `openFolder()` wrapper in `system.js` (`@tauri-apps/plugin-opener`'s `openPath`, distinct from the existing `revealInFileManager`/`revealItemInDir` which needs a specific file, not a folder).
- **Two real bugs caught by interactive verification, not assumed away**:
  1. `openPath` isn't covered by `opener:default`'s permission set (only `allow-reveal-item-in-dir`/`allow-open-url`/`allow-default-urls` are) — adding the bare `opener:allow-open-path` permission still failed with `Not allowed to open path ...`, because the command enforces an actual scope (built the same way `tauri-plugin-fs`'s scope is) that denies everything when no scope entries are configured, regardless of the permission being granted. Fixed by granting the permission with an explicit `"allow": [{"path": "**"}]` scope in `capabilities/default.json`, confirmed by a real Finder window opening with the exported files after the fix (it hadn't before).
  2. The reveal-folder step's own failure was originally caught by the SAME `try/catch` as the export loop, so a permission error there silently overwrote a successful export's own status message with a misleading "Export failed: ..." even though every image had already exported correctly. Fixed by isolating `openFolder()` in its own try/catch that appends a note to the existing status message instead of replacing it.
  3. (Non-bug, re-confirmed) The dialog's progress label appeared to "freeze" at "5 of 6" for several seconds during interactive testing — same WKWebView background-repaint-throttling behavior already documented in this file's Print Module entry (`WebProcessCache::setApplicationIsActive: (isActive=0)`), not a hang: clicking the window to refocus it immediately caught the label up to the real, already-further-along state.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings across 258 files. 88/88 Vitest, 253/253 Rust tests, both unchanged (no dedicated test file for `ExportDialog.svelte`, matching every other dialog component's existing no-test convention). Real `cargo build` clean after the capability change. **Verified interactively** against a rebuilt `.app`: exported a real 6-photo batch (RAW+JPEG mixed) and watched the progress bar/label advance file-by-file in real time; separately exported a 2-photo batch with "Show in file manager when done" checked and confirmed a real Finder window opened on the destination folder showing both exported JPEGs, with the dialog's own status correctly reading "Exported 2 of 2" (not clobbered by the reveal step).

## M4.5 Slice 2 — Batch Apply (2026-08-30)

Second M4.5 slice: applies the in-memory Copy Settings clipboard from Slice 1 across every Library-selected image in one action, not just the single image open in Develop.

- **New `handlePasteSettingsToSelection` in `+page.svelte`**: same shape as `handleApplyPresetToSelection` (and the batch path Slice 1 originally built into `handlePasteSettings` before it was narrowed to Develop-only) — per-target `getEditStack → applyPresetOps → setEditStack → regenerateThumbnail`, non-atomic across the batch, `pastingSettingsToSelection` guard against a race with opening Develop mid-batch, and the same re-sync-if-the-open-Develop-image-was-a-target guard. No new merge strategy; reuses `applyPresetOps` unchanged.
- **New "Paste Settings to Selection" button** in the Library titlebar next to "Apply Preset…" — enabled only when `copiedSettings` is set and at least one image is selected in Library.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings across 258 files. 88/88 Vitest, 253/253 Rust tests, both unchanged (no new pure function to unit-test — `handlePasteSettingsToSelection` is component-level orchestration, matching `handleApplyPresetToSelection`'s own no-dedicated-test convention). **Verified interactively** against a rebuilt `.app`: copied settings from an edited photo in Develop, selected 3 photos in Library via keyboard range-select (click + Shift+Right×2 — mouse Cmd/Shift-click was unreliable in this computer-use environment, closing the gap Slice 1 had left unverified), clicked "Paste Settings to Selection", confirmed "Pasted settings to 3 photos" and all three thumbnails visibly updated, then opened one of the previously-untouched photos in Develop and confirmed its Basic panel and History both show the exact pasted values (Exposure -0.25, Contrast -8, Highlights -9, Shadows +11) matching the source.

## M4.5 Slice 1 — Copy/Paste Develop Settings (2026-08-30)

First M4.5 slice. Lets a photographer copy a chosen subset of one image's Develop settings and paste them onto another image (Develop-open) or a Library multi-selection (batch), without saving a named Preset first.

- **`develop.js`**: `OP_GROUPS` — 8 named groups (White Balance, Basic Tone, Presence, Tone Curve, HSL/Color Mixer, Split Toning, Detail, Effects) covering the same op-name universe `PRESET_EXCLUDED_OP_NAMES` already treats as portable between images (masks/crop/lens correction/perspective stay excluded, for the same per-image-geometry reason Presets already exclude them). `copySettingsOps(stack, groupIds)` filters an edit stack down to the selected groups' ops, further passed through `presetEligibleOps` so a bogus group id can't smuggle in a mask/crop op. Paste reuses `applyPresetOps` unchanged — no new merge strategy invented, so it carries the exact same documented whole-op-replace limitation Presets already have.
- **New `CopySettingsDialog.svelte`**: Lightroom-style checkbox picker over `OP_GROUPS`, defaulting to all-checked, modeled directly on `SmartCollectionDialog.svelte`'s existing dialog chrome (no new dialog pattern introduced).
- **`+page.svelte`**: `copiedSettings` is in-memory-only `$state` (deliberately not persisted to the catalog — an ephemeral clipboard, not a saved Preset). "Copy Settings…" (enabled whenever `developVersionId !== null`, regardless of active module) opens the dialog against the currently-open Develop image's `editStack`. "Paste Settings…" is a single unified handler: while Develop is the active module it pastes onto the open image as an immediate discrete flush (same shape as Apply Preset/Reset — bypasses the slider debounce); otherwise it batch-pastes onto the Library selection via the exact `getEditStack → applyPresetOps → setEditStack → regenerateThumbnail` loop `handleApplyPresetToSelection` already established, including the same re-sync-if-the-open-Develop-image-was-a-target guard.
- **Verification**: 5 new Vitest cases for `copySettingsOps` (single group, multi-group union, unselected-group ops dropped, exclusion list still enforced even if every group id is passed, empty selection yields empty ops) — 88/88 total, unchanged elsewhere. `npm run check`: 0 errors/0 warnings across 258 files (2 new). 253/253 Rust tests unchanged (no Rust touched — this is a pure frontend feature reusing existing Tauri commands). **Verified interactively** via computer-use against a real built `.app`: copied a real edited image's settings (confirmed the checkbox list renders and toggles correctly), pasted onto a different, unedited image while it was open in Develop — confirmed the immediate flush, a new "Paste Settings" history entry, and the pasted Blacks=-100 value showing correctly in the Basic panel; separately pasted onto a Library-selected image from Grid view — confirmed the "Pasted settings to 1 photo" status message and the thumbnail/histogram visibly updating to match. (True N>1 multi-select wasn't exercised in this pass — this environment's computer-use modifier-key clicks didn't reliably reproduce Cmd/Shift-click in the running app; the batch code path is untouched-copy-identical to the already-shipped `handleApplyPresetToSelection`, which does not special-case selection size, so this is a low-risk gap, not an unverified new code path.)

### Follow-up: relocated to the bottom of the Develop panel (2026-08-30)

User feedback, before this slice's PR merged: move Copy/Paste Settings out of the main titlebar (which was getting crowded) and into the Develop edit panel itself, matching where Lightroom actually puts them. Both buttons now live in a new "Copy/Paste Settings" `<details class="section">` at the literal bottom of `DevelopPanel.svelte` (after Soft Proof), styled with the same `.preset-action-btn` the existing Presets section already uses.

Since `DevelopPanel` only renders while Develop is the active module, this also **narrowed Paste Settings' scope**: it no longer has a Library-batch-selection path (that was this slice's own scope creep past the milestone's actual item 1 — batch apply is milestone item 2, a separate not-yet-built slice). `handlePasteSettings` in `+page.svelte` is back to a single synchronous branch — paste onto whatever's currently open in Develop — and the now-dead `pastingSettings` guard/async batch loop was removed rather than left unreachable.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings across 258 files. 88/88 Vitest, 253/253 Rust tests, both unchanged. **Verified interactively** against a rebuilt `.app`: confirmed the titlebar no longer shows the two buttons, confirmed "Copy/Paste Settings" is the last section at the bottom of the Develop panel, and re-ran the copy → paste round trip from the new location (checkbox dialog opens correctly, "Copied settings" status, Paste Settings enables and applies correctly).

### Follow-up 2: pinned, not collapsible-at-the-bottom-of-a-scroll (2026-08-30)

User feedback: living inside the `<details>`-section scroll still meant scrolling past every adjustment section to reach it, which defeated the point. Restructured `DevelopPanel.svelte`'s layout from one scrolling `.panel` into a flex column: a new `.panel-scroll` wrapper (everything from the header through Soft Proof, unchanged content) takes over the scrolling, and a new `.panel-footer` sits outside it as a true fixed footer — Copy Settings/Paste Settings are always visible regardless of scroll position, no longer a collapsible section. Considered putting the buttons in the mask tool strip below the canvas instead (the other option raised) but kept them in the Develop panel: it groups them with the rest of the settings-actions vocabulary (Reset, Presets) rather than mixing text buttons into an icon-only tool-selection row.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings, 88/88 Vitest, both unchanged (pure layout/CSS change, no logic touched). **Verified interactively**: scrolled the panel content with an image that already had several adjustment sections open and confirmed Copy/Paste Settings stayed fixed and visible at the bottom throughout, unaffected by scroll position.

### Follow-up 3: icon buttons, not "Copy Settings…"/"Paste Settings…" text (2026-08-30)

User feedback: drop the "…" text labels in favor of icon-only buttons, matching the compact icon-button vocabulary the rest of `DevelopPanel.svelte` already uses (`.auto-btn`'s Auto WB/Auto Tone icons, the eyedropper icons). Added two new inline SVG snippets, `copySettingsIcon`/`pasteSettingsIcon` (overlapping-sheets "copy" glyph, clipboard-with-clip "paste" glyph — standard, recognizable shapes rather than anything bespoke), and a new `.footer-icon-btn` style (same interaction states as `.auto-btn`: accent-colored, brightens on hover, dims on disabled). `title`/`aria-label="Copy Settings"`/`"Paste Settings"` carry the same information the removed text labels did, just as a tooltip/accessible name instead of always-visible text.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors/0 warnings, 88/88 Vitest, both unchanged (pure markup/CSS, no logic touched). **Verified interactively**: confirmed both icons render correctly (copy icon enabled/accent-colored, paste icon dimmed with nothing copied yet), opened the Copy Settings dialog from the new icon button, confirmed the paste icon lights up (accent-colored, no longer dimmed) immediately after copying.

## Plan change: new M4.5 milestone inserted (2026-08-30)

M4 (Modern retouching & output modules) is fully done as of the Print Module + Contact Sheet PDF fix below. Rather than moving straight to M5 (Performance, GPU, merges, faces), the user asked for a milestone of Develop/workflow UX improvements surfaced from actually using the app, inserted between M4 and M5 as **M4.5 — Develop & workflow UX polish** ([PRD/MILESTONES.md](PRD/MILESTONES.md)): copy/paste Develop settings, batch apply, export dialog progress + reveal-in-file-manager, native OS menu bar, history-panel hover preview (with Presets relocated there), resizable Develop side panels, and step-nudge slider controls. Not started yet — next step is to slice and build these one at a time following the existing feature-branch/PR practice.

## Fix — Print Module: Contact Sheet PDF export cells looked misaligned (2026-08-30)

User-reported bug against the just-merged Print Module (PR #87): the exported Contact Sheet PDF "looked broken, images not aligned to grid." Root cause confirmed with a synthetic 2×2 grid of solid-color, mixed-aspect test images (`build_print_pdf` called directly in a throwaway test) — the grid math itself (`contact_sheet_cells`) was correct and already unit-tested, but each cell is otherwise invisible on the page: a landscape photo and a portrait photo in cells of the identical size will occupy very different amounts of visible area once each is independently letterboxed via `place_contain`, and with nothing marking the true cell boundary, that reads as a scattered/broken layout rather than a grid. The on-screen `PrintLayoutView.svelte` preview never had this problem because its `.grid-cell` already carries `background: #eee`, which paints the full cell footprint regardless of the image's own aspect. Fixed by drawing that same `#eee` fill as a `printpdf` `Op::DrawPolygon` rect behind every Contact Sheet cell, before placing the image — the exported PDF now visually matches the on-screen preview. 2 new Rust tests (253/253 total): `cell_background_op` fills the exact four corners of a cell in the exact `#eee` gray, and a Contact Sheet PDF still builds correctly with the added ops. Verified again against a real 4-photo Contact Sheet export (mixed real-world aspect ratios) rendered to a thumbnail via `qlmanage`. Landed as `fix/print-contact-sheet-cell-background`, targeting `main` directly (small, isolated fix to already-merged code, not a new M-milestone slice).

## M4 — Print Module (2026-08-30)

**Last unbuilt item in M4's scope** — the Print module ("layout templates, page setup, printer color management, soft-proofed output," PRD/PRD.md:100). Every other M4 item (Auto Tone/WB, Library filter/view-mode overhaul, healing/clone brush, red-eye, perspective correction, Smart Previews, Soft Proofing) is already on `main`. Shipping this closes M4's own exit criteria; next milestone work starts at M5.

**Architecture decision** (confirmed with the user before implementation): use the webview's native `window.print()` — the real OS print dialog — rather than a Rust-side PDF-generation crate. Zero new Rust/JS dependencies; reuses the OS's own printer list, paper handling, and print color-management pass-through.

- **New `app/src-tauri/src/print.rs`**: reuses `soft_proof.rs`'s lcms2 ICC transform unchanged for "printer color management" — a printer/paper `.icc` is just another `TARGET_CUSTOM` profile, `gamut_warning` always forced off server-side (not a meaningful concept for an actual print buffer, only a screen aid). `export.rs` gained an extracted `pub(crate) fn render_full_resolution` (the decode → lens-correction → perspective → edit-stack → crop pipeline `export_one` already had, unchanged, just no longer duplicated) that both `export_one` and `print::generate_print_ready_image` now share. Own content-addressed cache tier (`{content_hash}_{stack_hash}_print_{color_key}.jpg`) at the source's native resolution — deliberately distinct from the 2048px-capped Develop preview cache, matching Export's own full-resolution precedent. New `get_print_ready_images` Tauri command, same shape as `get_soft_proof_preview` (re-resolves each `version_id`'s source/content_hash/edit-stack fresh from the catalog, never a client-supplied path).
- **Frontend**: third module alongside Library/Develop. `PrintPanel.svelte` — Layout (Single Image / Contact Sheet templates), Page Setup (paper size, orientation, margins), Color Management ("Printer Manages Colors" default vs "Managed by Printer Profile," reusing the exact profile/custom-file-picker/intent trio Soft Proof already built). `PrintLayoutView.svelte` — WYSIWYG page canvas; live layout preview reuses `getGradedDevelopPreview`/`getSoftProofPreview` as-is (cheap, already cached); dynamic `@page`/`@media print` CSS injected via `svelte:head`, using the standard `visibility:hidden` + absolutely-positioned `.print-page` trick to isolate print output from the rest of the app shell. Print button fetches the real full-resolution payload via `get_print_ready_images`, swaps it into the layout, awaits a tick for the image to load, then calls `window.print()`.
- **Scope**: Single Image (fit-within-margins, Fit/Fill toggle) and Contact Sheet (configurable rows × columns, Library selection order, "Showing N of M" note if selection exceeds one page's grid capacity) shipped this slice. Custom Package (free-drag layout), multi-page print jobs, Identity Plate/watermark overlay, and print-specific output sharpening explicitly deferred — the last one because Export itself doesn't have it yet either, cleanest done once and shared by both later.
- **Two real bugs caught by interactive verification, not assumed away**:
  1. `window.print()` threw `webview.print not allowed. Permissions associated with this command: core:webview:allow-print` — Tauri v2's wry/webview layer intercepts `window.print()` and routes it through the same ACL system as a Rust-side IPC command, contradicting the plan's own research (which had concluded it was a plain DOM API exempt from Tauri's capability system, based on it not appearing in `core:webview:default`'s bundled permission set — true for the ACL's *default* grant, but the call is still gated, just not pre-granted). Confirmed the exact missing permission via the Web Inspector console (`Inspect Element` is available on a `--debug` build; a release build has no console access) against a `tauri build --debug` bundle, then added `core:webview:allow-print` to `capabilities/default.json` and reconfirmed the real OS print dialog opens correctly.
  2. The four margin number inputs (Top/Right/Bottom/Left) were crammed into one ~140px-wide row — each input's own native step-arrow spinner left no room for the digits, so the values were present but genuinely unreadable on screen. Restructured into a labeled 2×2 grid, matching `DevelopPanel.svelte`'s existing subsection-label convention.
- **Verification**: 241/241 Rust tests (5 new in `print.rs`: color-management cache-key differs for none-vs-a-profile, print-ready output exceeds the Develop preview's 2048px cap on a real RAW sample, "Printer Manages Colors" is a true near-identity pass-through, a managed custom profile demonstrably remaps out-of-gamut pixels while gamut_warning stays forced off, cache key changes with color management and is stable on repeat calls). `npm run check`: 0 errors/0 warnings across 257 files (up from 254 — three new components). 83/83 Vitest unchanged (`print.js` gets no dedicated test, matching `export.js`/`system.js`'s established thin-`invoke()`-wrapper convention). Real `make build` production build, both bundles clean. **Verified interactively** via computer-use against real built `.app` bundles (a debug build for console access while diagnosing the `window.print()` permission gap, the final release build for the last confirmation pass — the stale `/Applications/Emulsion.app` from an earlier session was refreshed each time, the same class of gotcha this file's earlier entries already documented): confirmed Single Image and Contact Sheet both render correctly on screen; changed paper size/orientation and confirmed the on-screen layout reflowed correctly; toggled "Managed by Printer Profile" with a real macOS system ICC (`/System/Library/ColorSync/Profiles/Generic CMYK Profile.icc`, the same file the Soft Proofing slice used) and confirmed the preview visibly desaturated/darkened, consistent with a CMYK profile's narrower gamut; clicked Print for both templates (with and without the managed profile) and confirmed the real macOS print sheet opened each time with a correct live preview (image positioned and cropped exactly as the on-screen layout showed, "Page 1 of 1"), then cancelled rather than sending to the real detected printer (no attached printer to verify physical output against). Windows/WebView2 `@page`/print-dialog behavior remains unverified in this macOS-only sandbox, per this project's standing practice for Windows-only gaps.

### Follow-up: "Export as PDF" (2026-08-30)

User asked, after the above landed, for a direct PDF export path — the OS print dialog's own "Save as PDF" already covers this, but a one-step in-app flow (skip the interactive dialog, write straight to a chosen file) was explicitly requested and confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` over three options: reusing the print dialog as-is, a dedicated Export-as-PDF button (**chosen**), or a full page-layout PDF preset system.

- **New dependency**: `printpdf` v0.12.7, `default-features = false` — deliberately avoids the crate's default "html"/"text_layout" feature set (which pulls in `roxmltree`, `taffy`, `rust-fontconfig`, none of which this slice needs). `RawImage`/`RawImageData`/`RawImageFormat` are unconditionally available (not gated behind an "images" feature), so a `printpdf` page image is built directly from an in-memory `image::RgbImage`'s raw buffer — no JPEG encode/decode round-trip through printpdf's own codec path.
- **`print.rs` additions**: `build_print_pdf` composes a one-page (Single Image) or grid (Contact Sheet) PDF from already-print-ready rasters, reusing `generate_print_ready_image`'s existing cache tier unchanged — the PDF payload is pixel-identical to what `window.print()` already sends. Two CSS-semantics helpers ported into Rust's PDF coordinate space (origin bottom-left, Y-up — opposite of DOM/CSS's top-left, Y-down, so Contact Sheet row 0 maps to the *highest* Y): `place_contain` (centered, aspect-preserved fit, mirrors `object-fit: contain`) and `center_crop_to_aspect` (pre-crop then contain, avoiding the need for a PDF clip path, mirrors `object-fit: cover`). New `export_pdf` entry point and `export_print_pdf` Tauri command (spawn_blocking, re-resolves each `version_id` fresh from the catalog like every other print/export command — never a client-supplied path).
- **Frontend**: second button in `PrintPanel.svelte`, "Export as PDF…", next to "Print…". `+page.svelte`'s `handleExportPdf` picks a destination via `save()` (same convention as every other file-picker in this app), builds the snake_case wire request from the same print-module state `handlePrint` already reads (template/fit/rows/cols/spacing, paper size looked up from `PAPER_SIZES` with width/height swapped for landscape, margins, color management), and reports success/failure via the existing `statusMessage` banner.
- **Verification**: 15/15 new Rust tests in `print.rs` (251/251 total) — page/margin/content-rect math, contact-sheet cell geometry (row-major, spacing-aware — caught and fixed a units bug in the test itself, not the implementation: `cell_spacing_in` is inches, not points), `place_contain`/`center_crop_to_aspect` fit-mode math for both wide and tall images, and two end-to-end checks that `build_print_pdf`/`export_pdf` produce a real, parseable PDF file on disk (including the empty-selection edge case). `npm run check`: 0 errors/0 warnings, unchanged 257 files. 83/83 Vitest unchanged. Real debug `.app` build compiles and bundles cleanly, `/Applications/Emulsion.app` refreshed.
- **Interactive verification** (once the screen was unlocked): clicked "Export as PDF…" for Single Image (Chain Bridge photo, unmanaged color) — the native save dialog appeared, a real 1.6 MB one-page PDF landed at the chosen path, and its rendered content matched the on-screen "Fit Within Margins" preview exactly (confirmed by rendering the PDF to a thumbnail via `qlmanage` and inspecting it directly, since Preview.app access wasn't granted this session). Repeated for Contact Sheet (2×2, four photos, "Managed by Printer Profile" → sRGB checked) — a 12 MB four-page-image PDF was produced with all four photos correctly placed in row-major grid order, each fit-within-cell and centered, matching the on-screen layout. One non-issue worth recording: the Contact Sheet export's "Exporting…" button label appeared stuck for several minutes and looked like a hang (`sample`/`ps` showed zero CPU across both the Rust core and WebView processes) — turned out to be WKWebView throttling the page's own repaint while the app window was backgrounded (confirmed via `log show`: `WebProcessCache::setApplicationIsActive: (isActive=0)`) during the diagnosis itself; the PDF had actually already finished writing to disk minutes earlier, and the button only visually caught up once the window regained focus. Not a bug in the print/export code — logged here so a future "the button looks frozen" report isn't re-investigated as a backend hang.

## M4 — Soft Proofing (2026-08-27)

Last unbuilt-until-now item on M4's own scope list besides the Print Module (picked via `AskUserQuestion` against the two real remaining M4 items — chosen as the smaller, more contained slice). This is the first real use of the `lcms2` dependency, which has sat unused since M0 (ADR-0004 flagged color management as still-unwired; `raw_decode.rs`'s own header comment says the same).

- **New `app/src-tauri/src/soft_proof.rs`**: applies a real ICC proofing transform (`lcms2::Transform::new_proofing`, with `SOFT_PROOFING`/`GAMUT_CHECK` flags) to a graded preview buffer. Four target profiles: **sRGB** and two synthesized matrix-shaper profiles built from well-known primaries/gamma (**Adobe RGB**, **ProPhoto RGB** — via `Profile::new_rgb`, no bundled ICC assets needed), plus **Custom Profile…** (loads an arbitrary user-chosen `.icc`/`.icm` file via `Profile::new_icc`). Four rendering intents (Perceptual/Relative/Saturation/Absolute). Gamut Warning flags out-of-gamut pixels with Lightroom's own flat-mid-gray alarm color (`Transform::set_global_alarm_codes`).
  - **Named simplification, not a silently-papered-over gap**: this app has no real working-space/display-profile management anywhere in its pipeline (every other consumer — the WGSL shader, Export's JPEG encode, the graded-preview cache — already treats the rendered buffer as plain sRGB). Soft proofing therefore simulates "sRGB source → target profile," not "true working space → target profile," consistent with the rest of the codebase's current color model.
  - 9 new Rust tests, including a hand-built deliberately-narrow synthetic RGB profile (real bundled targets are all WIDER-gamut than sRGB by construction, so none of them can ever produce a testable out-of-gamut result — Adobe RGB/ProPhoto RGB against sRGB source never trips gamut warning, confirmed both by this reasoning and live in the running app below): confirms a saturated red is flagged with the exact alarm color under Gamut Warning, left un-flagged-but-still-remapped without it, and a near-neutral gray is left close to unchanged either way.
- **`preview_cache::ensure_soft_proof_preview_for_hash`**: reuses the existing edit-graded preview (`ensure_graded_preview_for_hash`) as its decode source — proofing simulates the user's ACTUAL current edit, not the unedited RAW — then applies the ICC transform on top. Cache key folds in both the edit-stack hash and a hash of the soft-proof settings themselves (`soft_proof::settings_cache_key`, content-hashing a custom profile's bytes rather than its path, matching this module's own established "hash content, not path" discipline), so any change to either naturally produces a new cache entry.
- **New `get_soft_proof_preview` Tauri command**, same shape as `get_graded_develop_preview` (re-reads the edit stack fresh from the catalog under a brief lock, releases it, does the slow work on a blocking thread). Settings are supplied fresh by the frontend on every call — ephemeral view state, never persisted into the edit stack.
- **Frontend**: new "Soft Proof" section in `DevelopPanel.svelte` (Enable checkbox, Profile dropdown, conditional custom-file picker button, Rendering Intent dropdown, Gamut Warning checkbox) — all local `$state` in `+page.svelte`, threaded down as plain props with `on...Change` callbacks, same "ephemeral view state, not `handleAdjustmentChange`/`scheduleFlush`" treatment as `maskOverlaysVisible`/`showOriginal`. A debounced (250ms, mirroring `scheduleFlush`'s own settle) `$effect` re-fetches the proofed preview whenever the edit stack, the open image, or the proof settings change. `DevelopCanvas.svelte` shows the result as a `object-fit: contain` overlay `<img>` above the live WGSL canvas (deliberately NOT tracking the canvas's own pan/zoom/crop-preview transforms — a periodic "how will this look on the target device" check, not a second fully-interactive view) plus a persistent top-right "Soft Proof — `<profile>`" badge (top-left/top-center already taken by the existing Smart Preview badge and before/after label).
- **Verification**: 236/236 Rust tests (9 new). `npm run check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings across 254 files. 83/83 Vitest (unchanged — the new `develop.js` wrapper matches every other thin `invoke()` wrapper's existing no-dedicated-test convention). Real Tauri production build (`make build`) — both bundles clean. **Verified interactively** via computer-use against the real built `.app`: toggled Soft Proofing on the smiling-woman portrait, confirmed the badge/overlay render correctly for sRGB (visually identity) and Adobe RGB (no gamut warning, correctly — Adobe RGB is wider-gamut than sRGB, so nothing my sRGB-sourced content can be out-of-gamut relative to it); loaded a real macOS system ICC file (`/System/Library/ColorSync/Profiles/Generic CMYK Profile.icc`) as a Custom Profile and confirmed a genuinely convincing result — the saturated pink shirt/lipstick flagged flat gray with Gamut Warning on, and visibly desaturated/clipped-toward-gamut (not flagged) with it off; confirmed turning proofing off cleanly restores the live graded canvas.

## Fix: histogram/RGB hover readout stuck on the image's top-left corner (2026-08-27)
