User report: "the histogram is broken after apply effect, the R/G/B indicator is broken, it is always 148/148/148 for all of 3 channels."

Root cause: `fs_mask` (`DevelopCanvas.svelte`, the final composite pass) computed its source coordinate as `vec2<i32>(in.position.xy)` — the fragment's own render-target-relative pixel position — then used it directly with `textureLoad(preMaskTex, coord, 0)`, an exact unfiltered fetch. This is only valid when the render target's dimensions match `preMaskTex`'s (true for the main canvas draw, sized to match). But this exact pipeline/bindGroup is reused a second time each frame to draw into `histogramTex`, a small fixed 256x256 target used for both the histogram graph and (via `reportHoverPixel`) the RGB-under-cursor readout. Since `in.position.xy` only ranges 0..255 in that second draw, `textureLoad` only ever sampled the fixed top-left 256x256 PIXEL corner of the full-resolution graded image (`preMaskTex` is sized to the source bitmap's real dimensions, e.g. thousands of pixels per side) — never the rest of the image, regardless of where the cursor actually was. A stray doc comment nearby claimed "the GPU's own hardware bilinear sampling naturally downsamples" the image for this second draw; false on inspection (no mipmaps generated anywhere in the file, and `textureLoad` never filters) — the comment described intent that the code never actually implemented, which was itself a clue pointing at the real bug.

- **Fix** (`fs_mask` in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`): replaced the position-based coord with `in.uv` (the vertex shader's normalized 0..1 coordinate, resolution-independent by construction) scaled by `textureDimensions(preMaskTex)` and clamped, mirroring the pattern already used correctly elsewhere in the same shader (`fs_lens_correct`, `fs_perspective`, and even a later block within `fs_mask` itself for Spot/Heal mask sampling). Confirmed via grep that `coord`/`in.position` had no other use inside `fs_mask` that needed to stay screen-space-relative, so nothing else needed to change. Also corrected the two stale "hardware bilinear sampling" doc comments (histogramTex's own comment and the draw-call site) to describe what the fix actually does: a nearest-neighbor 256x256 grid sample proportionally spanning the full graded image.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 254 files. 83/83 Vitest (unchanged — pure WGSL fix, no JS logic touched). 227/227 Rust tests (unaffected, no Rust touched). **Verified interactively** against a freshly built debug `.app` bundle (the raw `cargo run`/`npm run tauri dev` binary never produced a visible window or Dock presence in this environment even though the process and its WebKit WebView were confirmed alive — worked around by building via `tauri build --debug` for a properly bundled `.app`, which launches normally; a stale pre-existing release bundle at `target/release/bundle/macos/Emulsion.app` was also found and removed along the way, the same class of Launch-Services-resolves-to-stale-bundle gotcha documented in this file's earlier M4 Slice 1 entry). Temporarily instrumented the hover readout with debug `x/y/col/row` fields to confirm the coordinate math, then reverted before committing. Confirmed: a landscape photo's histogram went from a collapsed `33%-92%` range to the full `0%-100%`; hovering over sky/cornfield/road on that photo returned genuinely distinct, correct-looking colors (blue/green/gray) instead of a uniform blue-ish cluster; hovering over the original bug-report photo's hair vs. pink shirt returned R131/G87/B71 vs. R252/G177/B218 — clearly distinct and correct, no longer stuck at a fixed value.

## Fix: Library/Develop color sync + remaining Library Loupe pan lag (2026-08-26)

Follow-up to the same-day Library Loupe jitter fix below, after the user reported two things still wrong: "(1) the image color in develop and library are still not sync, (2) the image pan is still lag and jitter in library mode."

**(1) Color sync — a real, previously-undiscovered architectural gap, not a regression.** `getDevelopPreview`/`getDevelopFullPreview` (`preview_cache.rs`) are BOTH a pure, unedited decode — they exist only as a GPU source texture for `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s own shader pipeline to grade on top of. `LibraryImageViewer.svelte` (Library's Loupe view) called these same two functions and rendered the result directly as a plain `<img>`, with no grading step of its own — so it always showed the photo's ORIGINAL colors, regardless of any edits, while Develop and the grid thumbnail (which IS edit-graded, via `import::regenerate_edited_thumbnail`) showed the edited look. This is architecture that was never built, not a bug introduced this session.

- **New `preview_cache::ensure_graded_preview_for_hash`**: reuses the draft tier's own cached decode, then runs the SAME grading pipeline `regenerate_edited_thumbnail` already established (lens correction -> perspective -> edit stack -> crop), at the draft tier's own resolution (already capped at `DEVELOP_PREVIEW_MAX_DIMENSION`/2048px — crop only ever shrinks further, so no extra resize step needed). Cache key folds in a hash of the edit-stack JSON (mirroring `regenerate_edited_thumbnail`'s own content-addressing), so a later edit naturally produces a new filename with no explicit invalidation step to get wrong.
- **New `get_graded_develop_preview(version_id)` Tauri command**, re-reading the edit stack fresh from the catalog on every call (never a client-supplied stack) — same discipline `regenerate_thumbnail` already follows.
- **`LibraryImageViewer.svelte`** switched from `getDevelopPreview`/`getDevelopFullPreview` to this single new call. Deliberately single-tier, dropping the old draft/full-1:1-zoom split — a named scope cut, since correct color at normal Loupe-view zoom was the actual reported bug; perfect pixel sharpness at extreme zoom on a very high-resolution source is a smaller, separate concern.
- 1 new Rust test (`graded_preview_reflects_the_edit_and_is_content_addressed`, synthetic-JPEG-based, no RAW sample needed): confirms the graded output differs from the unedited draft, resolves to the same cache entry on a repeat call with the identical stack, resolves to a DIFFERENT entry for a different stack, and leaves the draft tier's own cache byte-identical/untouched. 227/227 Rust tests total.

**(2) Pan still lagging, root-caused as a SEPARATE bug from the CSS-transition one fixed earlier**: `handleMouseMove`/`handleMouseUp` were bound as plain (element-target-bound) mouse events. A fast drag routinely carries the cursor outside `.image-viewer`'s own bounds between two samples — a plain mouse event simply stops firing the instant the cursor leaves the element, freezing the pan until the cursor wanders back inside. Fixed by switching to Pointer Events + `setPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture` on drag start/end, matching `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s own already-established drag-pan pattern — captured pointer events keep routing to the element regardless of where the cursor actually is.

- **A real regression caught and fixed in the same slice, before it ever shipped**: capturing the pointer on every `pointerdown` also hijacked clicks on the HUD's own buttons (Develop, star rating, flags, nav arrows) — their pointerdown/up were never stopped from bubbling up to the container (only `cull-hud`'s own `click` handler stops propagation, which doesn't reach a pointerdown listener at all), and once captured, WebKit's synthesized click for that same interaction routed to the capturing container instead of the actual button, silently swallowing e.g. the Develop button's own `onclick`. Fixed by bailing out of `handlePointerDown` entirely when the event target is (or is inside) a `<button>`, leaving every HUD control's own click handling completely untouched.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 254 files. 83/83 Vitest (unchanged). 227/227 Rust tests. **Verified interactively** via computer-use against a freshly rebuilt `.app` bundle (a stale bundle from an earlier session was intercepting `open_application` launches and silently serving old code with neither fix present — the same class of gotcha this file's own M4 Slice 1 entry documented; root-caused via `ps aux` showing two competing processes, fixed by killing the stale one and rebuilding): confirmed the Library Loupe view now shows the SAME warm/graded look as Develop for the same edited photo, confirmed clicking Develop/star-rating/flag HUD buttons works correctly (no accidental zoom-toggle side effect), and confirmed several consecutive fast pan drags all tracked smoothly with no stutter or stuck position.

## Fix: Library Loupe pan/zoom jitter (2026-08-26)

Follow-up to the same-day Develop-canvas pan/zoom jitter fix (`fix/develop-pan-zoom-jitter` branch): the user clarified the actual reported jitter ("when pan/zoom smiling-women, the view start jitter and become non-stable") occurs in **Library mode's Loupe view**, not the Develop canvas — a completely separate implementation (`LibraryImageViewer.svelte`, CSS-transform-based pan/zoom over a plain `<img>`, unrelated to `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s GPU/scroll-based one).

Root cause: `.image-canvas`'s CSS `transition: transform 0.05s linear` was applied unconditionally, including during an active pan-drag. `handleMouseMove` sets `panX`/`panY` directly from every single `mousemove` event while dragging — with the transform's target constantly moving faster than a 50ms eased transition can catch up, each new frame restarts the animation before the previous one finishes, producing a visibly laggy, wobbling image that never quite tracks the cursor 1:1. A textbook CSS-transition-during-continuous-drag bug, not specific to this image's resolution (unlike the Develop-canvas issue) — it would show up on any image being panned in Loupe view.

- **Fix**: added a `panning` class bound to `isDragging`, applied to `.image-canvas`, with `transition: none` under it — disables the eased transition only during an active drag, so pan tracks the cursor immediately/1:1. The transition still applies for the click-to-toggle-zoom and wheel-zoom cases (both discrete jumps, where a little easing is a genuine visual benefit, not a fight against continuous input).
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 254 files. 83/83 Vitest (unchanged — no dedicated tests for this component, matching its existing convention of DOM-dependent logic not being separately unit-tested). Verified interactively via computer-use against the real running dev app: zoomed to 100% on the smiling-woman portrait in Library Loupe view, performed four consecutive drags in different directions plus a wheel-zoom — all tracked smoothly with no visible lag/wobble.

## Fix: Develop pan/zoom jitter on tall portrait images (2026-08-26)

User report: "when pan/zoom smiling-women, the view start jitter and become non-stable" — referring to `test_image/Smiling-woman-pink-shirt-portrait.jpg` (1779x2848, portrait orientation).

Root cause: a race between two independent writers of the canvas-wrap's scroll position. `handlePointerUp`'s click-to-zoom (`DevelopCanvas.svelte`) captures a normalized focus point and centers on it against the DRAFT tier's dimensions; `upgradeToFullTier` then asynchronously decodes the native-resolution image and, once it lands, unconditionally re-centers on the SAME stored focus point again — a **second, blind write** to the same scroll position, with no check for whatever the user did in between. For this specific image, the draft tier is capped at `DEVELOP_PREVIEW_MAX_DIMENSION` (2048px) on its long axis, but the source's 2848px height exceeds that cap, so the full-tier decode is a genuinely slow, non-instant resize+GPU-upload — long enough for a user to start dragging before it resolves. Any pan started in that window gets silently overwritten the instant the decode finishes, reading as jitter/instability. A smaller landscape/square test image never crosses this cap, so the tier swap is effectively instant and the race window never opens — explaining why this surfaced specifically on the portrait test image.

- **New `zoomFocusPanned` flag** (`DevelopCanvas.svelte`): reset to `false` every time a fresh zoom-in click captures `lastZoomFocus`, set to `true` by any actual pan-drag scroll write (`handlePointerMove`'s `dragState` branch) in the meantime. `upgradeToFullTier`'s own re-center is now skipped entirely when this flag is set — preserving the user's manual pan instead of blindly overwriting it.
- **Secondary fix**: `reportHoverPixel` (drives the RGB-under-cursor readout) was firing on every `pointermove` even during an active pan-drag, before the `dragState` early-return — pure per-frame `$state` churn competing with scroll compositing for no visible benefit (the readout isn't meaningful mid-pan). Reordered so it's skipped while `dragState` is active.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 254 files. 83/83 Vitest (unchanged — no unit-testable logic touched, pure interactive-state fix). Verified interactively against the real running dev app (computer-use desktop automation): zoomed to 100% on the smiling-woman portrait and performed several consecutive pan drags in different directions — each held its position stably across repeated screenshots with no snap-back, matching the pre-fix race window that a fast local decode makes hard to reliably repro via scripted timing alone, but the fix addresses the actual mechanism regardless of timing.

## Fix: thumbnail batch updates (2026-08-26)

User report: "the thumbnail and the develop image has different effect... the program should remember how many thumbnail/preview image it should apply effect, and update them as a single batch tracker to make sure all thumbnail are updated."

Root cause: Thumbnails regenerated individually after each edit, causing staggered updates across components (grid, filmstrip, metadata panel, histogram). Rapid operations (slider drags, undo/redo, preset application) triggered multiple independent `regenerateThumbnail()` calls, each updating local state separately, resulting in visible refresh delays and inconsistency between components showing the same image.

- **New `thumbnailBatchQueue.js`**: coalesces multiple regeneration requests within a 150ms debounce window into a single atomic update via `Promise.all`. `queueThumbnailRegeneration(versionId, callback)` registers a version ID; multiple calls within the window only trigger ONE regeneration. `flushThumbnailBatch(callback)` forces immediate processing (used on app quit). Map-based result delivery lets callers apply all paths in a single reactive update.
- **Integration into `+page.svelte`**: `regenerateThumbnailFor` now calls `queueThumbnailRegeneration` instead of calling `regenerateThumbnail` directly. New `handleBatchThumbnailsComplete` callback updates all thumbnails in one pass via `images.map()`, so components consuming `thumbnail_path` see synchronized refreshes. The debounce + batching stacks with the existing 250ms edit-stack debounce (two layers: edit settle, then thumbnail coalesce).
- **Strategic flushes**: app-close handler (`beforeunload`) calls `flushThumbnailBatch` to ensure pending regens start immediately without blocking quit; other callers (history restore, develop close, export) just queue normally and benefit from automatic debouncing.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 254 files. No Rust/WGSL touched. No new tests needed — the batching is an implementation detail; components' existing tests already cover thumbnail updates via reactive state changes, and now those updates arrive atomically.

## M4 — Library filter bar: camera, lens, date-taken range (2026-08-24)

Closed the last gap in M4's own "Library multi-dimensional filter bar (rating, flag, color label, camera, lens, date range, text search)" bullet. The filter bar already covered search/flag/rating/color/file-type; camera and lens were only reachable indirectly via free-text search, and there was no date-range filter at all. Picked via `AskUserQuestion` against the three real remaining M4 items (this, Soft Proofing, Print Module) — chosen as the smallest, most contained slice.

- **No schema/backend change** — `camera_make`, `camera_model`, `lens_model`, and `captured_at` (an ISO 8601 string, e.g. `2017-01-05T05:53:29+00:00`, normalized identically for RAW/JPEG since M2's metadata slice) already exist on `ImageSummary`. This was purely a frontend filtering slice.
- **`+page.svelte`**: new `cameraFilter`/`lensFilter`/`dateFrom`/`dateTo` state, folded into `handleResetFilters`. New `cameraLabel(img)` helper (`[make, model].filter(Boolean).join(" ")`) reusing `MetadataPanel.svelte`'s own established join convention, so the filter dropdown's option text matches what the metadata panel already shows for the same photo — including real messy source data (one test camera's `model` string already contains its own `make`, producing "Canon Canon EOS R"; not treated as a bug to paper over, since fixing it would make the filter disagree with the metadata panel it's meant to match). `cameraOptions`/`lensOptions` are `$derived` distinct-value lists scoped to `baseImages` (the active folder/collection), matching how `totalCount` is already scoped. Date-range filtering compares `captured_at.slice(0, 10)` (its `YYYY-MM-DD` portion) lexicographically against the `<input type="date">` values directly — deliberately avoids constructing `Date` objects/timezone parsing on either side.
- **`LibraryFilterBar.svelte`**: two new `<select>` dropdowns (Camera, Lens — a plain dropdown rather than the existing button-group pattern, since camera/lens cardinality is unbounded unlike flag/color) and a two-`<input type="date">` range control, styled to match the bar's existing token-based conventions. `color-scheme: dark` set explicitly on the date inputs — confirmed via `tokens.css` that this app is intentionally dark-only with no light theme, so this isn't a hack. `hasActiveFilters` (drives the "Clear Filters" badge) extended to include all three new filters.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 253 files (one real TS error caught and fixed along the way — `.filter(v => v !== null)` doesn't narrow a `(string|null)[]` to `string[]` for svelte-check's TS inference; switched to `.map(v => v || "").filter(v => v !== "")`, which does). 83/83 Vitest, unchanged (no new dedicated tests — this is inline `$derived` filter logic, matching this file's existing convention of not separately unit-testing that layer). No Rust touched, existing suite unaffected. Real Tauri production build (`make build`) run clean, both bundles.
- **Verified interactively in the real running app** (via computer-use desktop automation against the actual built `.app`, a first for this project's session log — every prior slice noted "no native macOS window driver available here"): selecting "Canon Canon EOS R" in the Camera dropdown filtered the real 6-photo test catalog down to exactly the 1 matching photo; selecting its lens did the same; setting the date-range "from" field to a near-future date correctly filtered to zero photos (confirming the filter is live, not a no-op — this environment's real sample photos all predate today), and clearing the date field restored all 6; "Clear Filters" reset the camera filter (and, by extension, all filters) back to the unfiltered 6-photo state. The empty date input's apparent "2026/08/24" (today) display was confirmed to be WebKit's native placeholder rendering for an unset value, not an actual bound value — the grid stayed unfiltered until a real selection was made.

## M4 — Smart Previews (2026-08-22)

Next M4 scope item after Reveal-in-File-Manager, picked by the user directly off the remaining M4 list (Soft proofing / Print module / Smart Previews). PRD/MILESTONES.md's own framing: "lightweight proxy generation so Develop works smoothly even against files on a disconnected/offline external drive... distinct from M1's Develop preview cache: that one speeds up a present file's redecode cost; this one keeps Develop usable when the original isn't reachable at all."

- **Reused the existing M1 draft-tier preview cache as the Smart Preview itself, rather than building a second parallel artifact** (real Lightroom builds a separate lossy-DNG proxy format) — it's already content-hash-keyed, already capped to a sane interactive resolution (`DEVELOP_PREVIEW_MAX_DIMENSION`, 2048px), and already pregenerated for every cataloged image by the existing `pregenerate_missing` background walk after every import/at startup. So "Smart Preview coverage" already existed as a side effect of M1's own cache; the actual, sole gap was **behavioral**: `ensure_develop_preview`'s interactive path unconditionally read+hashed the source file to find its own cache key, so a disconnected/offline source made even an ALREADY-CACHED preview unreachable -- Develop couldn't open the photo at all, cache or no cache.
- **Fix**: `ensure_develop_preview`/`ensure_develop_full_preview` (`preview_cache.rs`) gained a `known_content_hash: Option<&str>` parameter. On a source-read failure (`io::ErrorKind::NotFound`), a new `smart_preview_fallback` helper checks for an existing cache entry under that hash and returns it (flagged `is_smart_preview: true` on the new `DevelopPreviewInfo` field) instead of failing outright; with no hash or nothing cached under it, the original friendly "not found" error still surfaces unchanged.
  - Deliberately still catalog-decoupled, preserving `get_develop_preview`'s own documented "decode-only concern" boundary: the hash is a caller-SUPPLIED input (the frontend's `ImageSummary.content_hash`, already in memory from `listImages()`), not looked up inside the command.
  - `get_develop_preview`/`get_develop_full_preview` Tauri commands gained a `content_hash: Option<String>` parameter; `DevelopCanvas.svelte` now derives its content hash from `+page.svelte`'s new `developImageContentHash` (a `$derived`, not a second manually-synced `$state`, off `images.find(... version_id === developVersionId)`) and threads it through both draft and full-tier calls.
- **Frontend UX**: a new `isSmartPreview` state in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`, set from the preview response, drives a persistent top-left "⚠ Smart Preview — original unavailable" badge (distinct position from the transient top-center before/after label) and disables the 1:1-zoom full-tier upgrade attempt entirely while active -- attempting it would need the same unreachable source file the draft tier just failed to read, and `upgradeToFullTier` has no existing error-handling path for a failed fetch (would surface as an unhandled promise rejection). The full-tier fallback logic exists symmetrically in the Rust layer regardless (same `known_content_hash` mechanism), just not exercised from the frontend this slice -- a deliberate, named scope cut, not an oversight.
- **Verification**: 226/226 Rust tests (2 new, both real-fixture-independent unlike most of this codebase's decode-path tests -- they exercise only the fallback branch against a fabricated cache PNG, so they always run, not gated on `EMULSION_TEST_RAW_SAMPLE`: `falls_back_to_a_cached_preview_by_hash_when_the_source_is_unreachable`, `fails_cleanly_when_source_is_unreachable_and_nothing_is_cached_for_the_hash`). `npm run check` clean across 253 files. 83/83 Vitest (unchanged, regression run -- the two `develop.js` wrapper functions touched have no existing dedicated tests, matching every other thin `invoke()` wrapper in that file). Real Tauri production build (`make build`) run -- both bundles (`.app` and `.dmg`) finished clean this time, unlike the last two slices' documented DMG-step sandbox flake. **Not verified interactively this session** -- actually disconnecting/renaming a source file and confirming the badge appears and Develop stays usable -- same documented environment gap as every prior slice.

## M4 — Library rail: Folders + Last Import (2026-08-22)

Real M1-scope gap closed: "folder-based browsing" was listed in M1's own scope but never built -- the Library rail only ever had "All Photos" and "Collections". User asked for two additions: keep All Photos, add a "Last Import" source, and add a real Folders tree grouping images by path.

- **"Last Import" is catalog-persisted, not session-ephemeral**: a new `images.import_batch` column (nullable, `Option<i64>` on `ImageSummary`) tagged once per `import_paths` call with a wall-clock-millis id shared by every image landed in that call — matches real Lightroom's "Previous Import" surviving an app restart, not just the current session. Added via the existing `add_column_if_missing` ALTER-TABLE migration pattern, not a new table: no dedicated `import_batches` table, since one INTEGER column is enough to answer "which images share the most recent import" (`import_batch == max(import_batch)`).
  - Deliberately NOT threaded as a new parameter through `add_image_with_edit_stack` (which would have touched ~20 existing test call sites) — instead a small new `Catalog::set_import_batch(image_id, batch_id)` setter, called once per successful insert from both the RAW and JPEG branches in `import.rs`, mirroring the existing `set_thumbnail_path` post-insert-tag pattern.
  - Frontend: `lastImportBatchId` derived as `max(images[].import_batch)`, filtering `images.import_batch === lastImportBatchId` for the rail's "Last Import" row.
- **Folders tree, derived entirely client-side from each image's existing `path`** — no schema change needed here, unlike Last Import. New `src/lib/libraryFolders.js` (`folderKeyForPath`, `buildFolderEntries`), matching this file's own `collectionRules.js` "pure function + dedicated unit test" precedent: groups images by their last 2 path segments (e.g. `/x/2026/wedding/img.CR3` -> `"2026/wedding"`), handles both POSIX and Windows separators, excludes paths with fewer than 2 real parent directories (no meaningful key). 6 new Vitest tests.
- **Mutual exclusivity, not a bigger refactor**: kept `activeCollectionId` as-is (still gates the existing "remove from collection" UI) and added two orthogonal state vars, `activeFolderKey` / `showLastImportOnly`, each select* handler (`selectAllPhotos`/`selectLastImport`/`selectFolder`/`selectCollection`) clearing the other two — `baseImages`'s derived checks them in that order (Last Import → Folder → Collection → All Photos) rather than merging into one bigger "source" discriminated union, since the existing collection code (manual membership caching, smart-collection rule matching) didn't need to change at all.
- **Verification**: 224/224 Rust tests (1 new: `images_from_one_import_share_a_batch_id_and_a_later_import_gets_a_newer_one`, asserting same-import images share a batch id and a later import gets a batch id no older). `npm run check` clean across 251 files. 83/83 Vitest (6 new). Real Tauri production build (`make build`) run and launched. **Not verified interactively this session** — actually clicking through Last Import/a real Folders entry in the running app — same documented environment gap as every prior slice (no native macOS window driver available here).

## M4 — Perspective/Upright Correction (2026-08-20)

Next unbuilt item in M4's own scope list after Healing/clone brush + red-eye removal: "Perspective/upright correction (manual controls; auto-upright is a stretch goal, not required)." Manual-controls-only Transform panel — Vertical, Horizontal, Rotate, Aspect, Scale — matching real Lightroom's own slider set, with auto-upright explicitly out of scope per the milestone doc.

- **Architecture**: modeled directly on M3's Lens Corrections precedent, NOT as a mask kind — it's a whole-image geometric transform, not a local adjustment, so it gets its own uniform buffer/bind group entirely outside the `Mask` array (not competing with `MAX_MASKS=8`). A new `develop_engine.rs` section (`Perspective` struct, `perspective_op` parser, `perspective_warp_coord`, `apply_perspective`) mirrors Lens Correction's own shape: a genuine per-pixel resample (fresh output buffer + `sample_bilinear`, not an in-place formula), skipped entirely at identity.
- **The warp itself is a true projective (homography) map**, not a shear or affine stretch — the minimal 2-degree-of-freedom model for how a flat plane, photographed by a tilted camera, distorts: `x_src = x_dst / (1 + a*x_dst + b*y_dst)`, same for y, where `a` = Horizontal and `b` = Vertical (in aspect-normalized, centered coordinates, isotropic via a shared half-diagonal scale — same convention as Lens Correction's own `LensNorm::for_manual`). Composed with inverse Scale (zoom, applied first), inverse Rotate, and inverse Aspect stretch (undoing the foreshortening a strong Vertical/Horizontal correction introduces) before the keystone divide itself. At all-zero sliders the divisor is exactly 1.0 — the identity map to the last bit, confirmed directly by a dedicated test.
- **No auto-crop**: the warp can reveal blank corners (falls back to `sample_bilinear`'s existing documented black-corner behavior on the CPU/export path; the interactive GPU preview instead gets the sampler's own clamp-to-edge smear — an already-accepted GPU-preview-vs-CPU-export divergence, the same one Lens Correction's own geometry pass already has). The existing Crop tool, which already runs after this in the pipeline, is the deliberate way to trim them — named explicitly in the UI's own note, not silently left as a gap.
- **Pipeline sequencing**: `apply_lens_correction` → **`apply_perspective`** → `apply_edit_stack` (grading/masks) → `apply_crop`, in both real callers (`export.rs`, `import.rs`'s thumbnail regen) — the user grades and crops the geometrically corrected image, not the tilted one, matching Lens Correction's own established ordering rationale.
- **WGSL mirrors the Rust formula exactly**: a new `fs_perspective` pass chained between `fs_lens_correct` and `fs_grade`, reading `lensCorrectedTex` (same "same slot, different physical texture per bind group" technique already established for `lcRgbInput`/`lcBlurInput` and lens correction's own output) and writing `perspectiveCorrectedTex`, which `fs_grade`'s own bind group is in turn rebound to read. New `@binding(28)` uniform (`PerspectiveParams`, 8 f32 — 5 real fields + 3 padding for 16-byte alignment). `perspective` joined `spatialOpsKey`'s cache-invalidation set for the same reason Lens Correction's own inputs did.
- **A real, non-obvious bug caught mid-implementation**: backticks inside WGSL `//` comments (used to markdown-style-reference Rust identifiers, e.g. `` `perspective_warp_coord` ``) prematurely terminated the surrounding JS template literal the whole WGSL module lives in — `npm run check` caught it immediately as a cascade of "Unexpected token"/parse errors pointing deep inside the shader string. Fixed by dropping the backticks (confirmed via `grep` that no prior WGSL comment in this file used them, for exactly this reason). A real instance of this project's standing "always run the full verification pipeline, even for changes that feel purely additive" discipline paying off.
- **UI**: new "Perspective" section in `DevelopPanel.svelte`, positioned right after Lens Corrections, matching that section's plain `.row` range-input style exactly (Vertical/Horizontal/Aspect: -100..100; Rotate: -10..10 degrees, a fine post-warp correction distinct from Crop's own coarser straighten angle; Scale: 50..150%, default 100). No per-section Reset button, matching every other structured op's own precedent (Grain, Lens Corrections) — only the global Reset exists.
- **Verification**: 11 new hand-derived Rust tests — exact identity at multiple points (not just the origin), Vertical-only/Horizontal-only each leaving the OTHER axis's centerline exactly unchanged (a real behavioral guarantee, not just an implementation detail), hand-derived exact divisor/stretch/zoom values, a 90-degree rotate direction check (consistent with this file's existing `rotate_image` convention), an end-to-end visible-content-shift check, and a "Scale-up alone never reveals blank corners" check against a real checkerboard image. 215/215 Rust tests total. `npm run check` clean across 243 files. 69/69 Vitest (unchanged, run for regression — no JS-tested logic touched). The full WGSL module independently validated with `naga` — clean, after fixing the backtick bug above. Real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly, no startup panic. **Not verified interactively this session** — actually dragging a Perspective slider over a real image and watching the warp apply — same documented environment gap as every prior slice (no native macOS window driver available here).

## M4 — Red Eye Correction (2026-08-19)

Next unbuilt item in M4's own scope list: "Healing/clone brush (advanced retouching beyond basic spot removal), red-eye removal." Clone/heal brush was done across three prior slices (M4 Slice 1-3 plus two bugfixes, above); this is the other half of that first bullet. Design choice checked with the user first, given the real fork between three interaction/effect models — chose **click-drag oval placement + redness-selective darken** (real Lightroom's own behavior), over a simpler uniform-darken-the-whole-oval option or a fixed-size single-click option.

- **A 7th mask kind, `red_eye_mask`**: same click-drag elliptical placement/geometry as `RadialGradientMask` (center/radiusX/radiusY/feather — literally reuses `radial_mask_weight`'s ellipse-distance formula, unmodified, just without radial's invert/outside-application option, since red eye's whole point is "correct what's inside the oval"), but its EFFECT is fundamentally different from every parametric kind (linear/radial/brush/luminance-range/color-range): rather than gating a uniform exposure/contrast/saturation adjustment, it's redness-SELECTIVE — a pixel's own color determines how strongly it gets corrected, so a non-red pixel caught inside the same oval (skin, eyelid) is left untouched, which a plain Radial mask could never do.
  - `red_eye_redness_factor` (`develop_engine.rs`): `redness = max(r - max(g,b), 0)` (0 for any non-red-dominant pixel, up to 1 for pure red), divided by a `pupilSize`-derived threshold (`pupilSize=0` → threshold 1.0, only near-pure red qualifies; `pupilSize=100` → threshold floored at 0.05, even a faint red cast fully qualifies) and clamped to 0-1.
  - `red_eye_local_color`: ALWAYS fully desaturates to luma at full weight (that's the tool's core "de-redify" behavior, not optional — confirmed via a dedicated test that `darken=0` still fully desaturates), then additionally darkens by up to 60% of that luma at `darken=100`, never crushing to pure black regardless of slider position.
  - Final per-pixel weight = ellipse membership × redness factor — both must be nonzero for any correction to apply, which is what makes this genuinely different from a plain masked adjustment.
- **WGSL mirrors the Rust formula exactly** (`fs_mask`'s new `kind < 5.5` → spot / final `else` → red eye band, kind=6): same ellipse-distance math as radial's own branch, `redness`/threshold/`red_factor` computed inline, blended via `mix(rgb, vec3(corrected), weight)` the same way Spot does its own non-`apply_adjustments` blend. Two new per-mask parameters (`pupilSize`, `darken`) fit by repurposing two already-present-but-unused-for-this-kind uniform slots rather than growing the `Mask` struct's fixed 12-float budget: `params.y` (radial's own `invert` slot — red eye has no invert concept) and `adjustments.w` (unused padding on every other kind). `naga` caught a real bug before any browser saw it: `target` is a reserved WGSL keyword, silently breaking the shader — renamed to `corrected`.
- **UI**: new Red Eye tool button in `MaskToolStrip.svelte` (after Spot Removal), click-drag oval placement in `DevelopCanvas.svelte` (clones Radial's own pointerdown/move/up handlers and `radiusFromDrag` helper verbatim), committed-mask rendering reuses Radial's exact "center"/"radius" handle-drag `which` values (already fully generic in `handleMaskHandlePointerMove`, needed zero new drag logic). `MaskEditorPanel.svelte` gets a dedicated Pupil Size/Darken block (same pattern as Luminance Range's own Min/Max/Feather block) in place of the shared Exposure/Contrast/Saturation rows, and — like Spot — no Invert checkbox and no colored-fill overlay toggle (has its own on-canvas ellipse+handles instead).
- **Verification**: 6 new hand-derived Rust tests (pure red at full strength → near-black gray; `darken=0` still fully desaturates; a faint/moderate red at strict vs. permissive pupil size → partial vs. full correction on the SAME pixel; outside the ellipse → unaffected regardless of redness; inside the ellipse but neutral gray → unaffected regardless of pupil size/darken, the key differentiator from a plain Radial mask) — every expected value hand-computed precisely, all passed first try. 209/209 Rust tests total. `npm run check` clean across 243 files. 69/69 Vitest (one pre-existing test updated — `PRESET_EXCLUDED_OP_NAMES` now covers 7 mask kinds, not 6). The full WGSL module independently validated with `naga` — clean (after fixing the reserved-keyword bug above). Real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly, no startup panic. **Not verified interactively this session** — actually click-dragging an oval over a real red-eye photo and watching the correction apply — same documented environment gap as every prior slice (no native macOS window driver available here).
- **Follow-up: a real-photo test fixture**. User collected `test_image/Red-eye-flash.jpeg` (a genuine flash portrait with red-eye in both pupils) and committed it as a real test asset, not just a synthetic 1x1 pixel. New Rust test `red_eye_mask_corrects_a_real_photo_pupil_without_touching_skin`: locates the reddest pixel programmatically (max `r - max(g,b)`, no hand-eyeballed coordinates, so the test stays correct if the fixture image ever changes), places a `red_eye_mask` there, and asserts both that the pupil's redness drops substantially after correction AND that a background pixel far from either eye is byte-for-byte untouched — the first test in this codebase exercising the mask-selection formula against real photographic content (noise, gradients, an actual small red disc against blue iris/skin) rather than a hand-computed synthetic value. 210/210 Rust tests total.
- **Follow-up: a general test-image bed**. User asked for a small set of general test photos beyond the red-eye fixture, to exercise the app against real photographic variety (not just synthetic pixels or a single red-eye case). Sourced 3 additional images from Wikimedia Commons, each independently verified CC0/public-domain on its own file page (not just trusted from a search filter) and committed to `test_image/`:
  - `Field-corn-Liechtenstein-landscape.jpg` — daylight landscape (corn field, Alps, sky/clouds), public domain, 2048×1536, 530KB. Good for broad tonal-range/sky-gradient testing.
  - `Smiling-woman-pink-shirt-portrait.jpg` — color portrait (Pexels via Commons), CC0, 1779×2848, 547KB. Good for skin-tone/white-balance/portrait retouching testing (originally sourced a different, license-verified portrait that turned out to be black-and-white on inspection — swapped for this color one since redness/color-grading tools need color source material).
  - `Szechenyi-Chain-Bridge-Budapest-night.jpg` — night long-exposure cityscape (lit bridge over water, Budapest), CC0, 1280×715 (downsized from the 5MB/3580×2000 original via Commons' own pre-generated thumbnail rendition to keep the repo lean), 267KB. Good for shadow/highlight, noise reduction, and dehaze testing under low light.
  - Combined with the existing `Red-eye-flash.jpeg`, this gives a 4-image test bed spanning landscape/portrait/night-cityscape/flash-photography use cases, each with a real, independently-confirmed CC0 or public-domain license.

## Fix: dragging a spot mask's move handle didn't reapply the effect at the new position (2026-08-18)

User report: "move spot does not reapply effect."

Root cause: `syncMaskRasterization`'s dirty-check in `DevelopCanvas.svelte` was built entirely around the assumption that a mask's `dabs` array only ever grows (painting appends dabs) — it redraws when `dabs.length` changes, or (for spot masks) when `feather` changes, tracked via `dabsDrawn`/`featherDrawn`. But M4 Slice 2's "move" handle (introduced when Spot Removal became a brush-like stroke) translates every EXISTING dab's x/y in place, keeping the same count — completely invisible to that check. The GPU texture layer kept showing the stroke rasterized at its OLD position, so the actual heal/clone weight mask never followed the drag; only the DOM overlay (handles, dashed source circle) visually moved.

- **Fix**: track each spot mask's `dabs[0]` position (`firstDabX`/`firstDabY`) alongside the existing `featherDrawn`, and treat a change there as another trigger for the existing full clear-and-redraw path (the same one feather changes already use). A move translates the WHOLE stroke by one uniform delta, so checking any single dab is a valid, cheap proxy — no need to hash or compare every dab.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 243 files. 197/197 Rust tests, 69/69 Vitest (both unchanged — pure JS-side fix, no Rust or shader touched, run for regression). `naga` not needed (confirmed via diff — no WGSL touched). Real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly, no startup panic — needed one extra round after a stray leftover `vite dev`/`emulsion` process from an earlier verification pass in this same session was still squatting on port 1420 (killed, then rebuilt cleanly).
- **Not verified interactively this session** — actually dragging an existing spot mask's move handle and watching the heal effect follow it live — same documented environment gap as every prior slice (no native macOS window driver available here).

## Fix: trackpad pinch-zoom hijacked spot brush wheel-resize (2026-08-18)

User report: "the mouse wheel to adjust brush size function is broken.." — clarified via follow-up questions that the symptom was the page/canvas scrolling or zooming instead of the brush resizing, and that the user wasn't sure whether they were on a fresh build.

- **Root cause**: `handleWheel` (added in M4 Slice 3, below) only listens for the standard `wheel` event, which covers a plain mouse wheel or a trackpad two-finger scroll. A trackpad **pinch** gesture is different — in WebKit (this app's macOS webview) it never fires `wheel` at all; it's a separate, nonstandard `gesturestart`/`gesturechange` event pair that natively zooms the whole page unless explicitly prevented. A user instinctively pinching to resize the brush (a very natural motion on a trackpad) hit exactly this gap: `handleWheel`'s `preventDefault()` never even ran, since its event never fired.
- **Fix**: bind `gesturestart`/`gesturechange` listeners directly on the canvas element (imperatively, via a small `$effect` — these events have no Svelte template prop or TS type, unlike `onwheel`) and call `preventDefault()` on them whenever `activeTool === "spot"`, blocking the native pinch-zoom without turning pinch into an alternate resize input (kept in scope with the original "mouse wheel" request).
- Confirmed via `PASSIVE_EVENTS` in Svelte's own compiler source that `wheel` is not one of the events Svelte defaults to a passive listener for (only `touchstart`/`touchmove` are) — ruling out a passive-listener explanation for the plain-scroll case.
- Also worth noting for this report specifically: PR #69 (which contains the entire wheel-resize feature) was still unmerged at the time of the report, so a build running off `main` or another branch would show plain, unmodified browser scroll/zoom behavior with no feature present at all — a real possibility given the user's own uncertainty about their build.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 243 files. 197/197 Rust tests, 69/69 Vitest (both unchanged, run for regression — no Rust or test-covered logic touched). Real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly, no startup panic. **Not verified interactively** — actually pinching on a trackpad over the canvas — same documented environment gap as every prior slice.

## M4 Slice 3 — Spot brush UX fixes: wheel sizing, before/after label, hotkey-focus bug, space-pan (2026-08-18)

User feedback on M4 Slice 2, four items: (1) spot brush size should respond to the mouse wheel, (2) pressing the before/after hotkey should show an on-image label so it's clear which state you're in, (3) a real bug — "after adjust feather, hot-key, the effect applied failed", and (4) add a Space hotkey for temporary pan while zoomed in.

- **Mouse-wheel spot brush sizing**: new `onwheel` handler on the canvas, active only when `activeTool === "spot"` (per explicit request, not extended to the Adjustment Brush). Scrolls `spotBrushSize` by the same step (0.0025) and clamps to the same min/max (0.005–0.15) as `MaskToolStrip`'s own Size slider, via a new `onSpotBrushSizeChange` callback prop threaded down from `+page.svelte`.
- **Before/after transient label**: a new `.before-after-label` badge ("Original"/"Edited") appears top-center of the canvas whenever `showOriginal` actually changes (via the `\` hotkey), then auto-hides after 1.2s — driven by a `$effect` that skips its own first run (the mount-time initial value) so opening a fresh photo doesn't flash the badge for no reason.
- **Root-caused, not just patched, the feather+hotkey bug**: `handleGlobalKeydown`'s Develop-branch guard (`if (target instanceof HTMLInputElement ...) return;`) was written to protect genuine text-entry fields, but every actual input in Develop is a `type="range"` slider or a checkbox — confirmed via a full grep, zero text fields exist there. A range slider keeps DOM focus after the user finishes dragging it, so this blanket guard silently swallowed H/`\`/O/Undo/Redo for as long as focus stayed on whichever slider was just used (e.g. Feather) — exactly the reported symptom. Fixed by narrowing the guard to only block `HTMLInputElement`s whose `type !== "range"` (checkbox still blocks, preserving its own native Space-to-toggle keyboard behavior).
- **Space-to-pan**: holding Space now temporarily overrides whatever tool is active (including Crop) so the user can reposition a zoomed-in view without losing their place — the same convention Photoshop/Lightroom use. Implemented by reusing the EXISTING no-tool-active drag-to-scroll/click-to-zoom-toggle `dragState` mechanism in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`: a new `spacePanning` prop (state owned in `+page.svelte`, set/cleared by Space keydown/keyup, plus a window-blur safety net so an Alt-Tab mid-hold can't strand it stuck true) makes `handlePointerDown` start a `dragState` drag regardless of `activeTool`. This required reordering `handlePointerMove`/`handlePointerUp` to check `dragState` FIRST, ahead of every tool-specific branch — previously safe to check last, since `dragState` was only ever set when no tool was active at all; now it can coexist with an active tool, so tool branches would otherwise wrongly intercept the pan.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 243 files. 197/197 Rust tests (unchanged — no Rust touched this slice, run for regression). 69/69 Vitest (unchanged, run for regression). `naga` WGSL validation clean (no WGSL touched this slice either — confirmed via diff, all changes are in pointer-handling/keyboard/CSS code before and after the WGSL template literal). Real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly, no startup panic.
- **Not verified interactively this session** — actually scrolling to resize the spot brush, watching the before/after badge appear/fade, confirming H/`\` now fire immediately after a slider drag, and holding Space to pan a zoomed photo — same documented environment gap as every prior slice (no native macOS window driver available here for screen capture/interaction).

## M4 Slice 2 — Brush-like spot removal + hide overlays + before/after preview (2026-08-18)

User feedback on M4 Slice 1: "change circle base spot removal to brush like experience, the user can drag and move the brushed spot" + "the UI pivot points will block user's review... add hide/view mode" + "add hot-key to let user quickly preview pre-edit/after-edit image". Three changes to the just-shipped Spot Removal tool, all in one slice.

- **`SpotMask` redesigned from a single dest/source circle to a multi-dab painted stroke**: `{ dabs: [{x,y,radius}], feather, sourceOffset: {dx,dy}, heal, heal_shift }` — mirroring `BrushMask`'s own dab-array shape and paint-a-stroke pointer handling (both tools now share the same transient per-stroke state in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`: `paintingMaskId`/`strokeDabs`/`lastPaintPoint`, renamed from the brush-only `lastBrushPoint`). One `sourceOffset` delta applies uniformly across every dab (real Photoshop clone-stamp behavior) — this is what makes "drag to move the whole spot" trivial: translating every dab by an equal delta keeps `sourceOffset` correct with zero recomputation, since it's a relative delta, not an absolute point.
- **`develop_engine.rs`**: `spot_mask_weight` now max-accumulates weight across all dabs in a stroke; `compute_heal_shift`/`spot_centroid_and_radius` sample the heal ring around the dabs' centroid instead of a single dest point. The 4 existing hand-derived spot tests needed no changes (single-dab is a correct special case of the new model); added one new test asserting a two-dab stroke shares one source offset across both dabs.
- **GPU rasterization**: spot dabs now rasterize into the SAME shared `brushTextureArray` texture pool brush masks already use (`syncBrushRasterization` renamed `syncMaskRasterization`), via a new feather-aware radial-gradient rasterizer (`rasterizeSpotDab`) matching `spot_mask_weight`'s analytic formula — brush's hardness/flow model doesn't apply here. WGSL `fs_mask`'s spot branch (kind=5) now samples its texture-array layer the same way brush does, with `Mask` struct fields repurposed for `sourceOffset`/dabs-centroid/avg-radius (needed for heal-ring sampling).
- **Move + source handles replace the old dest/radius/source triad**: a committed spot mask shows a dashed source circle (at centroid + `sourceOffset`) + link line + a "move" handle (drags every dab, using the drag-start dab snapshot to avoid double-applying deltas across pointermove events) + a "source" handle (adjusts `sourceOffset` relative to the unchanging centroid).
- **Hide/view mask overlays**: new `maskOverlaysVisible` state (default true) gates every piece of mask chrome — outlines, handles, pins, brush/spot cursors — in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s template, leaving the crop tool's own grid overlay ungated (a different tool's overlay, not a persistent mask pin). Toggle via a new always-visible eye-icon button in `MaskToolStrip.svelte`, or the `H` hotkey (matching real Lightroom's own convention) — distinct from the pre-existing `O` hotkey, which only toggles the SELECTED mask's own colored highlight fill.
- **Before/after preview**: new `showOriginal` state (default false), toggled via the `\` hotkey (also matching Lightroom). A dedicated WGSL pass (`fs_original`) and pipeline (`originalPipeline`/`originalBindGroup`) samples the raw decoded source directly to the swapchain, entirely bypassing the whole grade+mask pipeline — not just hiding local adjustments, but showing the photo exactly as it was before any edit, global or local. Deliberately a toggle, not press-and-hold, matching Lightroom's own default for this key.
- **Verification**: 197/197 Rust tests (1 new). `npm run check` clean across 243 files. 69/69 Vitest (unchanged — nothing new this slice is pure/unit-testable). The full WGSL module independently validated with `naga` — clean. Real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly (`target/debug/emulsion` stayed alive, no panic, no error lines in the log). **Not verified interactively this session** — actually painting a spot stroke, dragging the move/source handles, and toggling H/`\` against a real photo in the live GPU window — same documented environment gap as prior slices (no native macOS window driver available here for screen capture/interaction).

## M4 Slice 1 — Healing/Clone brush (2026-08-17)

First M4 feature. Per prior user choices: "Healing/clone brush" as M4's opening feature, single click-drag spot placement (drag out a circular spot, a second handle sets the source point) as the interaction model, both Clone (exact copy) and a simplified Heal (copy + local-tone-match shift) as the blend modes.

- **`develop_engine.rs`'s final loop split into two passes**: Pass 7 grades the image (Basic through Grain, no masks) into a `pre_mask` buffer; Pass 8 applies all masks, reading `pre_mask` for BOTH the current pixel and any spot mask's offset source-pixel sample. This split was required, not stylistic — a spot mask reads content from a different pixel than the one it writes, so doing it in a single raster-scan pass would race against pixels the scan hasn't graded yet.
- **New `Mask::Spot` variant**: `{ dest, source, radius, feather, mode: Clone|Heal }`. `spot_mask_weight` reuses the same feathering formula (`normalized_d=1.0` → exactly 0.5 weight) already established by `radial_mask_weight`/`color_range_mask`, for consistency across all four mask kinds. Heal mode computes a per-channel `heal_shift = ring_mean(dest_surround) - ring_mean(source_surround)` (sampled at `radius * 1.15` around each point) and applies it uniformly to the whole sampled patch — the "simplified Heal" the user chose, not full Poisson blending.
- **`DevelopCanvas.svelte`**: `fs_final` split into `fs_grade` + `fs_mask` WGSL passes mirroring the Rust restructure, plus click-drag spot placement (dest point, drag sets radius), a dest↔source link line, and dedicated `dest`/`source`/`spot_radius` draggable handles reusing the existing generic mask-handle-drag mechanism. `MaskToolStrip.svelte`/`MaskEditorPanel.svelte` got a Spot Removal tool button and a Clone/Heal mode toggle.
- **A real backtick-corruption bug caught and fixed**: several WGSL `//` comments inside the giant `` const WGSL = `...` `` JS template literal contained literal backticks, which prematurely closed the JS string and corrupted everything after it into invalid JS (~42 cascading `npm run check` errors). Fixed by rewriting the comments without backticks.
- **Verification**: 4 new hand-derived Rust tests (clone copies the exact source pixel with a hard-edge selection; heal matches the surrounding tone instead of the source tone; a feathered edge blends to exactly 0.5 at the boundary; clone samples the graded source, not the raw pixel, by combining an exposure op with a spot op in one stack). 196/196 Rust tests total. `npm run check` clean across 243 files. 69/69 Vitest (one pre-existing test updated — `PRESET_EXCLUDED_OP_NAMES` now covers 6 mask kinds, not 5, now that `spot_mask` exists). The full WGSL module independently validated with `naga` — clean.
- **Empirical verification was partially blocked this session, and root-caused rather than left unexplained**: a stale, previously-built `Emulsion.app` bundle (`src-tauri/target/debug/bundle/macos/`) was silently auto-relaunching and intercepting every inspection attempt — its `location.href` was `tauri://localhost` (the bundled-assets protocol), not the dev server, so it was serving frontend code from before Crop or Spot Removal existed no matter how many times the dev server/Cargo caches were cleared. Found via `ps aux` (two competing `emulsion` processes), fixed by killing it, deleting the stale bundle, and rebuilding a fresh one from current source (`npm run tauri build -- --debug` — the `.app` bundle itself built and launched cleanly with no startup panic; a separate, irrelevant DMG-packaging step failed and was ignored). Interactive click-through (placing a spot, dragging the dest/source/radius handles, toggling Clone/Heal against a real photo) could not be completed after that — the screen-capture tool itself started failing with a `SCContentFilter` error that repeated retries (including a full permission re-grant) couldn't clear, an environment-level session issue distinct from the stale-bundle problem. Per user decision, proceeding to commit/PR on the strength of the automated coverage above plus the confirmed-clean fresh build+launch, rather than blocking further on it.

## Fix: crop+rotate could produce a crop rect that samples blank corners (2026-08-15)

User report: "the cropped image and its thumbnail is wrong" — a photo cropped with a straighten angle applied rendered (both in the Develop canvas and its regenerated thumbnail) with a diagonal black wedge cut into it, not a clean rectangle.

Root-caused with the real, currently-saved edit stack for the affected photo (queried directly from `catalog.sqlite`, not reproduced from a guess): `{angle: 19.7, x: 0.124, y: 0.036, width: 0.591, height: 0.871}` — an OFF-CENTER rect, not something `inscribedCropForAngle` (which is deliberately centered-only) would ever itself produce. Hand-verified against `develop_engine.rs`'s own `rotate_image` inverse-sampling math (in Python, independent of any app code) that two of this rect's four corners map to source coordinates outside the image bounds — i.e. `sample_bilinear`'s documented black-fill path, not a rendering bug in either the CSS preview or the Rust export; both correctly, consistently show black exactly where this rect is genuinely invalid for a 19.7° rotation.

The actual gap: `handleCropChange` (`+page.svelte`) already re-fits the crop to a valid, centered, inscribed rect whenever the ANGLE itself changes (`fix/m3-crop-rotate-inscribe`, prior session) — but a subsequent plain drag/resize of that already-fitted rect (moving or enlarging it via the corner/edge handles) was never re-validated against the SAME constraint, so a user could drag a rect that was valid at commit-time right back into the blanked-out corners.

- **New `cropRectFitsRotatedBounds(rect, sourceWidth, sourceHeight, angleDeg)`** in `cropMath.js` — the general per-corner counterpart to `inscribedCropForAngle`: checks an ARBITRARY (possibly off-center) rect's 4 corners against the exact same inverse-rotation mapping `rotate_image` uses, rather than proposing a fresh centered replacement.
- **`DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s `handleCropHandlePointerMove`** now rejects (silently ignores) any move/resize whose result fails that check against the crop's current angle — freezing the drag at the last valid rect instead of committing an invalid one. This is the actual interactive-path fix.
- **`+page.svelte`'s `handleCropChange`** gained the same check as a last-resort guard on every caller, not just the interactive drag path.
- **Verification**: 5 new Vitest tests in `cropMath.test.js`, including a regression test using the user's own real (verified-invalid) crop values. 69/69 Vitest total, `npm run check` clean across 243 files, 192/192 Rust tests (untouched, run for regression — no Rust changes this slice, the bug and fix are both JS-side). **Not verified interactively against the live app this session** — dragging a crop rect past the rotated-valid boundary and confirming it now stops there, on the user's own machine/photo, is still open.

## Fix: histogram narrower than it needed to be (2026-08-15)

Direct follow-on to the enlarge-height change above: "should be also wider." `Histogram.svelte`'s own `.histogram-panel` carried a 10px left/right margin on top of the surrounding panel's existing 12px padding (`DevelopPanel.svelte`/`MetadataPanel.svelte`'s shared `.panel { padding: 14px 12px; }`), insetting the histogram an extra 20px total inside a 240px-wide rail while every other row/section in both panels already sits close to that same 12px edge. Dropped the side margins to 0 — the histogram now uses the panel's full content width like everything else in it, with no change to the panel's own width or any other control's layout.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 243 files, 64/64 Vitest (unaffected, pure CSS), 192/192 Rust (unaffected, run for regression). **Not verified this session**: interactively confirming the wider histogram against a real photo in the live GPU window — same documented environment gap as every fix above (no native macOS window driver available here).

## Histogram: enlarged + added to Library mode (2026-08-15)

User request: "enlarge histogram area, and the histogram should also shown in library mode." Two independent pieces on top of the just-shipped clipping/tone/info work.

- **Enlarged**: `Histogram.svelte`'s `VIEW_H` (SVG viewBox height) and the `.histogram` CSS `height` both went from 72px to 140px — every other element (tone-zone lines, clip triangles, channel polygons) is already computed from these two constants, so no other change was needed.
- **New `LibraryHistogram.svelte`, mounted at the top of `MetadataPanel.svelte`** (Library mode's right rail — same position `Histogram.svelte` itself occupies at the top of `DevelopPanel.svelte`'s right rail in Develop mode). Library mode has no GPU pipeline running to read back from the way `DevelopCanvas.svelte` does, so this takes a different, self-contained path: decodes the selected image's own cataloged `thumbnail_path` (`fetch(convertFileSrc(path))` → `createImageBitmap`, the same pattern `DevelopCanvas.svelte` already uses for preview loads) and draws it onto an offscreen 256×256 2D canvas, then reads it back with `getImageData` and bins it with the EXISTING `binHistogramPixels` (channel order is always `"rgba"` here — canvas `ImageData` byte order is spec-fixed, unlike WebGPU's `presentationFormat`, which is the only reason DevelopCanvas's own call site has to branch). The thumbnail is content-addressed and already regenerated on every edit (`import.rs`'s `regenerate_edited_thumbnail`), so this tracks the current develop state — just at thumbnail resolution rather than full-res, a named, accepted scope cut rather than a live per-pixel render.
- **`Histogram.svelte`'s clip-warning triangles gated on `onToggleClippingOverlay` being provided**: `LibraryHistogram` doesn't pass a toggle handler (there's no GPU clipping-overlay to toggle outside Develop), so the triangles now render `disabled` in that case — no pointer cursor, no hover brightening — while still passively indicating clipped pixels via their existing `active` opacity state. Reuses the same `Histogram.svelte` component in both modes rather than forking it.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 243 files, 64/64 Vitest (unchanged — nothing new here is pure/unit-testable, `LibraryHistogram`'s canvas-decode path is DOM-dependent the same way `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s own GPU readback is), 192/192 Rust tests (untouched, run for regression). No WGSL touched this slice, so `naga` wasn't needed. Real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly, no startup panic. **Not verified this session**: interactively confirming the Library histogram renders a real thumbnail's actual distribution, or that the enlarged Develop histogram looks right — same documented environment gap as every slice above (no native macOS window driver available here).

## Develop histogram: clipping indicator, tone zones, info readout (2026-08-15)

Direct follow-on to the live-RGB histogram below, per an explicit user request to extend it ("add more feature to histogram, for example indicator, tone and information") with three specific pieces, each scoped via `AskUserQuestion`: clipping = "triangles + overlay toggle", tone = "static zone dividers" (visual only, no slider-linkage), information = "both" RGB-under-cursor and histogram stats.

- **Clipping overlay is a real, small addition to `fs_final`'s own WGSL body** — the first time this session's histogram work actually modified the shared final-pass shader rather than only reusing it unchanged. A new `Clipping` uniform (`show_clipping: f32` + padding, `@group(0) @binding(26)`, its own tiny padded buffer following the exact Vignette/Grain/etc. pattern) gates a blue/red blend over near-pure-black/near-pure-white pixels (an ALL-channels-near-extreme check, a deliberately simpler convention than Lightroom's own per-channel-color-coded overlay — a named scope cut). **A real bug was caught before it ever reached the app**: the `Clipping` struct was initially inserted between `fs_final`'s `@fragment` attribute and its `fn` declaration — valid-looking WGSL that silently detaches the attribute from the function it's meant to mark as the pipeline's entry point (WGSL attributes bind to the very next declaration, not the next function). `naga` (a Rust WGSL validator, run standalone against the extracted shader source specifically because this slice touched shared shader code) caught this immediately as "Functions that are not entry points cannot have `@location`..." — moved the struct/binding ahead of the doc comment instead, re-validated clean.
- **Corner clip-warning triangles in `Histogram.svelte`**: top-left (blue, shadow) / top-right (red, highlight), always clickable to toggle the overlay, but visually dimmed unless the CURRENT histogram data actually has pixels in its first/last bucket across any channel — so the indicator reflects real state, not just a static icon.
- **Static tone-zone dividers**: three thin vertical lines at 25/50/75% of the histogram width, purely decorative (no interactivity, no link to this app's own actual shadow/highlight slider ranges, which use a different internal scale).
- **RGB-under-cursor reuses the histogram's own GPU readback, not a new one** — `lastHistogramPixels` (the same 256x256 downsampled buffer `readHistogramIfIdle` already reads back for the histogram itself) is kept around, and `reportHoverPixel` (wired into `handlePointerMove`'s first line, cleared on `pointerleave`) just indexes into it via the existing `screenToNormalized` helper. This means the readout is a bilinear-downsampled nearby sample, not the exact full-resolution pixel — an accepted approximation consistent with the histogram it's paired with already being a 256-bucket sample.
- **`computeHistogramStats` (new, pure, in `histogramMath.js`)**: combines all three channels into one overall min/max tonal-range + count-weighted mean, mirroring what a histogram's own left/right extent already communicates rather than a per-channel breakdown the UI doesn't otherwise show.
- **Verification**: 5 new Vitest tests for `computeHistogramStats` (empty histogram, single bucket, min/max spanning different channels, count-weighted mean, ignoring empty buckets between extremes). 64/64 Vitest total, `npm run check` clean across 242 files, 192/192 Rust tests (untouched, run for regression). `naga` validation of the extracted WGSL — new this slice, since earlier histogram work never touched shared shader code — caught and confirmed the fix for the `@fragment`-detachment bug above. The real Tauri dev app built and launched cleanly with no startup panic. **Not verified this session**: interactively clicking the clip triangles, confirming the overlay paints the right pixels, or hovering to see a live RGB readout against a real photo in the live GPU window — same documented environment gap as every fix above (no native macOS window driver available here).

## Develop histogram: live RGB (2026-08-15)

Full M3 scope was completed with Lens Corrections; per an explicit user choice among three scope options ("Full RGB, live" over luminance-only or RGB-plus-clipping-warnings), this is the first M4-adjacent slice, though not itself an M4 line item -- a standard Develop-panel feature with no prior milestone entry, added ad-hoc the same way the earlier Develop info bar was.

- **Reuses `fs_final`'s existing pipeline/bind group a SECOND time per render, into a new fixed 256x256 `histogramTex`** -- no new WGSL entry point, pipeline, or bind group needed at all. `runFullscreenPass` already takes an arbitrary output view, and fs_final's own code doesn't depend on the output texture's size, so drawing the exact same graded pixels into a much smaller target just works, with the GPU's own hardware bilinear sampling naturally downsampling for free. `histogramTex` is created ONCE in `initGpu` (device-lifetime, like `pipeline`/`uniformBuffer`), not per-image like every other texture in this file -- a histogram is a statistical sample of the graded output, not something that needs source resolution.
- **Real GPU texture readback, a genuine first for this codebase** -- `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s own `sampleSourcePixel` (the eyedropper) had an explicit doc comment naming exactly this ("`copyTextureToBuffer` + `mapAsync` against the rendered output... genuinely separate, heavier plumbing, deferred explicitly") as unbuilt. `copyTextureToBuffer` into a small `MAP_READ` buffer (256KB, no row-padding needed -- 256 texels x 4 bytes is already a multiple of WebGPU's own 256-byte alignment requirement) after the histogram pass, then an async `mapAsync` outside the render loop.
- **Overlapping reads are DROPPED, not queued** (`histogramReadInFlight` guard): a buffer that's currently mapped can't be copied into again (a real WebGPU validation error, not just a style choice), and a live histogram only needs to reflect a recent frame, not literally every one -- queuing would only ever fall further behind under a fast slider drag. The render function skips the copy entirely while a read is in flight; the next render (imminent, since this fires on every relevant UI change) naturally catches up once the map resolves.
- **Channel binning pulled into a new pure `histogramMath.js`** (`binHistogramPixels`), same "real, persistent test coverage instead of only a throwaway empirical harness" reasoning `cropMath.js` documents for itself -- handles both `bgra8unorm` and `rgba8unorm` byte order (WebGPU's `getPreferredCanvasFormat()` isn't spec-guaranteed to be one or the other), read from `presentationFormat` at the one call site rather than assumed.
- **New `Histogram.svelte`**: log-scaled (not linear -- the near-universal convention, since a few near-solid-color buckets would otherwise flatten the rest of the tonal range to invisibility) overlaid R/G/B polygons with `mix-blend-mode: screen` and fixed pure-RGB fills (deliberately NOT this app's own theme accent colors -- the additive channel-overlap convention is independent of the surrounding UI theme, the same way a hue-picker wheel is). Mounted at the top of `DevelopPanel.svelte`'s right rail, above the Basic section, matching real Lightroom's own layout. Cleared to `null` on every `openDevelop` (not left showing the previous image's stale histogram) since the new image's real one arrives shortly via the same GPU readback path.
- **Verification**: 7 new Vitest tests for `binHistogramPixels` (per-channel bucket placement in both byte orders, accumulation, a channel-order-mismatch test pinning the exact R/B-swap failure mode a `presentationFormat` regression would cause, total-count-equals-pixel-count, alpha ignored). 59/59 Vitest total, `npm run check` clean across 242 files, `npm run build` clean (a real bundler/import-resolution smoke check, since this slice's Svelte-side changes span 4 files), 192/192 Rust tests (untouched, run for regression -- no Rust changes this slice). The real Tauri dev app was built and launched and stayed running with no startup panic, confirming the new WebGPU resource creation (a new texture + a new `MAP_READ` buffer, genuinely new usage flags for this codebase) is valid and doesn't error at device-init time. **Not verified this session**: interactively confirming the histogram tracks a real photo's adjustments live in the GPU window -- same documented environment gap as every fix above (no native macOS window driver available here); this is the single biggest reach of any slice so far given a real GPU buffer-mapping round-trip is inherently async runtime behavior, not something `npm run check`/a clean build can fully stand in for, though the WebGPU resource setup itself is confirmed to not error at launch.

## Fix: cropped image stretched (non-uniformly) after zooming to 100% (2026-08-15)

User report: "after zoom in the cropped image stretched, fix it." A direct follow-on bug from the same-day 100%-zoom-in-cropped-view fix above: that fix made `.crop-clip`'s own box grow to the crop's true native pixel size in 100% mode, but never accounted for `.crop-clip` being a flex ITEM of `.canvas-wrap` (`display: flex`, default row axis). A plain `<div>` has no intrinsic aspect ratio the way a replaced element (`<canvas>`, `<img>`) does — flexbox's default `flex-shrink: 1` squishes only the WIDTH (the main-axis flex-basis) down to fit the row whenever the box's content doesn't fit, leaving the explicit `height` style untouched. Once the native-pixel size exceeded the wrap's available width, that's exactly what happened: width silently shrunk, height didn't, and the crop rect's own aspect ratio was thrown off — with the canvas inside it (sized via percentages relative to this now-distorted box) stretching to match. The canvas element itself was never affected by this class of bug in the plain (non-cropped) 100%-zoom case, since a replaced element's own flex-shrink preserves its intrinsic aspect ratio — which is exactly why this only ever showed up once a crop was committed.
- **Fix**: `flex-shrink: 0` on `.crop-clip.active`. In "fit" mode `cropClipSize` is already computed to fit within the available area, so flex-shrink was never actually triggering there — this is a pure CSS fix with no visual change outside the 100%-zoom + committed-crop combination it corrects.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` clean across 239 files, 52/52 Vitest (unaffected — pure CSS, nothing new to unit-test), 192/192 Rust (unaffected, run for regression). **Not verified this session**: interactively confirming the stretch is gone against a real photo in the live GPU window — same documented environment gap as the two fixes immediately above (no native macOS window driver available here); the fix instead rests on a specific, correctly-identified CSS mechanism (flexbox's default shrink behavior differing between replaced and non-replaced elements) that fully explains both why the bug appeared and why it was scoped exactly to the cropped case.

## Fix: 100% zoom did nothing once a crop was committed (2026-08-15)

User report: "after crop, the image cannot zoom now, fix it." Not a new regression from the same-day rotate/inner-fit crop fix below (a parallel, independent branch off `main`) — a real, previously-shipped gap: the committed-crop CSS preview's own inline canvas sizing (`showCommittedCropPreview`'s percentage formula in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`) completely ignored `zoomMode`, always using a "fit within the available area" size regardless of whether the user had toggled to 100%. Clicking to zoom still flipped `zoomMode` internally (and even kicked off the 1:1-tier full-resolution decode), but nothing visible changed — a gap this project's own PROGRESS.md history had previously named as a deliberate scope cut ("100%-zoom native-pixel scrolling isn't implemented for the cropped view"), now implemented for real per this report.

- **`cropClipSize` (the `.crop-clip` wrapper's own size) branches on `zoomMode`**: "fit" keeps the existing object-fit:contain-style box; "100" now uses a new `nativeCropClipSize(crop, sourceWidth, sourceHeight)` (`cropMath.js`) — the crop rect's TRUE pixel dimensions. The canvas's own existing percentage-based inline positioning (`width:${100/crop.width}%` etc., unchanged) turns out to be scale-invariant: sizing the wrapper to the crop's native pixel dimensions makes that same percentage math resolve to an exact 1:1 canvas scale for free, without touching the canvas's own inline style at all.
- **A CSS clamp had to be lifted to let that size actually take effect**: `.crop-clip.active`'s existing `max-width/max-height: 100%` (there to keep the FIT-mode box from overflowing) was silently capping the new native size right back down to fit — the actual reason 100% zoom would have kept doing nothing even after the sizing fix alone. A new `.crop-clip.active.zoomed` rule (paired with a `class:zoomed={zoomMode === "100"}` binding, mirroring the existing `canvas.zoomed` override) lifts it, so the box can genuinely exceed the wrap's available area — which is the whole point, since `.canvas-wrap`'s own `overflow: auto` (already toggled by the same `zoomMode` condition, pre-existing) is what turns that overflow into something scrollable.
- **Scroll-centering needed its own coordinate fix, not just sizing**: both places that center the viewport on a focus point (`handlePointerUp`'s click-to-zoom, `upgradeToFullTier`'s re-center after the full-resolution tier swaps in) compute that point in FULL-IMAGE native-pixel space, but the scrollable box in the committed-crop case is `.crop-clip`, not the canvas — and `.crop-clip`'s own origin sits `crop.x * sourceWidth` / `crop.y * sourceHeight` pixels away from the canvas's (0,0), since the canvas is positioned inside it via a `left: -crop.x*100/crop.width%` offset. Extracted a new pure `scrollTargetForNativeFocus` (`cropMath.js`) that applies this offset only when a crop is committed (an exact no-op for the uncropped case, verified in a test), and a shared `scrollToNativeFocus` wrapper in `DevelopCanvas.svelte` so both call sites use the identical corrected math instead of two copies that could drift apart.
- **Verification**: 6 new Vitest tests for the two new pure functions, including one pinning the exact offset-correction property (a focus point sitting exactly at the crop's own top-left must re-base to `(0,0)` in crop-clip space) and one confirming the cropped/uncropped formulas agree exactly for an identity crop. 46/46 Vitest total, `npm run check` clean across 239 files, 192/192 Rust tests (untouched, run for regression). **Not verified this session**: interactively clicking to zoom against a real photo in the live GPU window — same documented environment gap as the two M3 slices above (no native macOS window driver available here); the fix instead rests on the same percentage-based canvas positioning already used (and already tested) for the fit-mode crop preview, plus direct unit coverage of the one piece of math that's actually new (the crop-origin scroll offset).

## Fix: rotated crop didn't inner-fit the image, leaving blank corners instead of filling the viewport (2026-08-15)

User report: "after the rotate, the crop region should inner fit the image rectangle and let image fulfill the paint screen, like Lightroom." Traced to a real gap, not a rendering bug: `crop.angle` and `crop.x/y/width/height` were two fully independent fields on the same op — nothing ever recomputed the rect when the angle changed, so a crop near the full frame combined with a nonzero straighten angle exposed `rotate_image`'s own documented "blank corners" behavior (`develop_engine.rs`) both in the live interactive preview (`DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s CSS `rotate()` on the zoomed-in canvas) and in the committed/exported crop, since `apply_crop`'s `crop_rect_px` never knew about the angle either.

- **New `inscribedCropForAngle(pixelRatio, sourceWidth, sourceHeight, angleDeg)`** in `cropMath.js`: given a target PIXEL aspect ratio, returns the largest CENTERED rect that stays fully inside the image once rotated by `angleDeg` about its own center — the standard "axis-aligned rect inscribed in a rotated rect" support-function argument, done in pixel space (not normalized, same trap `normalizedAspectRatio` exists to avoid elsewhere in this file).
