- **Design reviewed before implementation, and it changed the plan materially.** The review re-derived the linear feather-boundary math independently and found my first draft (a "generous fixed half-length" for the perpendicular guide lines) was **structurally wrong, not just under-tuned**: a completely ordinary case (`start=(0.2,0.5)`, `end=(0.8,0.5)`, `feather=50`) puts the weight=1 boundary at `x=1.1` — outside the frame — with the perpendicular direction being purely vertical, meaning no length, however large, can ever bring the line back inside `x∈[0,1]` (the perpendicular offset is orthogonal to the axis and can't correct a coordinate along it). Fixed with proper line-vs-box clipping (Liang–Barsky) instead of a length constant — verified by hand against the review's own counterexamples, including a fully off-frame diagonal case that correctly clips to nothing.
- The review also steered the brush overlay away from my first design (a downscaled `OffscreenCanvas` + a JS luminance-to-alpha pixel loop, redrawn on a cache-key match) — it found the proposed cache key omitted `mask.invert` (toggling Invert would silently leave the overlay showing stale shading), found the destination canvas's resolution was never wired to reset per newly-opened image, and flagged real per-move performance cost stacking on top of an already-expensive per-move operation. Its alternative — extend the WGSL shader itself to sample the *already-bound* brush texture array and `mix` in a translucent color as one more step in the existing render pass — eliminates all three problems at once and adds **zero new cross-platform risk**, since brush masks already require WebGPU to work at all (unlike a CSS `filter: url(#...)` SVG-filter alternative, genuinely new browser-feature surface on WebView2, a platform ADR-0004 already flags as unverified even for the *existing* WebGPU path). Adopted.
- **Brush overlay**: `Adjustments` WGSL uniform struct grew from 4 to 8 `f32` fields (padded to a full 2×vec4, mirroring the `Mask` struct's own established "pack into vec4 multiples" discipline) — `overlay_layer` (-1 = disabled) and `overlay_invert`. The shader samples the selected brush mask's own texture-array layer (already bound, no new GPU resource) and mixes in a soft red at up to 55% opacity, right before the final clamp. `showBrushOverlay` (default `true`, since brush masks are otherwise invisible) lives in `+page.svelte` grouped with the brush *tool options* (`brushSize`/`brushHardness`/`brushFlow`/`eraseMode`) rather than with `activeTool`/`selectedMaskId` — deliberately never force-reset on image/module switch, matching those siblings' own already-documented "a user's preferred setting should survive within one session" reasoning (a real inconsistency the review caught in my first draft, which grouped it with the reset-on-switch state instead). The toggle checkbox lives in `MaskEditorPanel.svelte` (not `MaskToolStrip.svelte`): that panel is already only shown when a mask is selected and already branches on `mask.op === "brush_mask"` for hiding the Feather row — the exact same gating this checkbox needs, for free, whereas `MaskToolStrip`'s brush-options row gates on `activeTool === "brush"`, the wrong condition (a mask stays selected, and the overlay stays relevant, after switching away from the Brush tool).
- **Hotkey**: `"O"` (matching real Lightroom), added to `+page.svelte`'s single keydown handler — renamed from `handleLibraryKeydown` to `handleGlobalKeydown` since it's no longer Library-only, with a new early Develop branch (only the dialog guards actually reachable from Develop, not the Library-only ones copied blindly).
- **Feather range indicators**: radial masks show two ellipses (inner = fully-inside boundary at `radiusX/radiusY·(1-softness)`, outer = fully-outside boundary at `·(1+softness)`) when `feather > 0`, re-derived and confirmed correct against `radial_mask_weight`'s real formula; `feather === 0` deliberately keeps the single-ellipse rendering (two coincident stroked shapes would alpha-composite to a visibly heavier line — a real regression the review caught, not just redundant markup). Linear masks gain two new perpendicular guide lines at the weight=0/weight=1 boundary points (purely additive — the existing draggable axis line/handles are unchanged), clipped against the visible frame via a new `clipLineToUnitBox` helper (Liang–Barsky), each independently omitted if that boundary is genuinely off-frame.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app** (same throwaway-harness-via-`report_spike_result` technique used for the brush slice, removed before commit): constructed a linear mask (`start=(0.2,0.5)`, `end=(0.6,0.5)`, `feather=40`) and read the real rendered SVG line coordinates back via `document.querySelectorAll` — matched the hand-computed expected boundaries (`x=4%`/`x=76%`, spanning the full frame height) exactly; did the same for a radial mask (`radiusX=0.2`, `radiusY=0.15`, `feather=50`) — matched hand-computed inner/outer ellipse radii (`10%`/`7.5%` and `30%`/`22.5%`) exactly; confirmed the WGSL pipeline never errors (`canvas.width`/`height` correctly set, no `.overlay.error`) across every overlay-uniform state this change introduces (no masks, overlay on/not-inverted, on/inverted, off, on again); confirmed a real dispatched `"o"` `KeyboardEvent` correctly flips `showBrushOverlay`. The one thing this couldn't verify is the overlay's actual rendered *color* (WebGPU-only output, no screenshot tool available in this environment) — the shader code was reviewed directly and reuses the same `textureSampleLevel` pattern already proven correct for brush weight sampling, so this is a named, honest limitation, not a claimed-but-unverified success.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings. `cargo test --lib`: 80/80 passing, unchanged (no Rust logic touched — this is a pure preview-rendering/UI slice; the overlay is deliberately not part of the exported/CPU-rendered output).

## M3 Slice 7 — Local adjustments: adjustment brush (2026-08-02)

Third and last named local-adjustment mask type, reusing the masking infrastructure the two gradient mask slices built. The biggest technical departure of the three: a brush stroke is a variable-length sequence of paint "dabs," not a single small parametric shape, so it can't reuse the fixed-size uniform-array-of-analytic-formulas approach linear/radial share. Auto-mask (edge-aware painting, a real image-segmentation feature) is deliberately deferred to a future slice — consistent with how this same MILESTONES.md bullet was already split once before (linear shipped before radial).

- **New `brush_mask` op**: `{id, dabs: [{x, y, radius, hardness, flow, mode: "add"|"erase"}], invert, exposure, contrast, saturation}` — no mask-level `feather` (unlike linear/radial): softness is baked per-dab at paint time from the brush tool's own Size/Feather/Flow settings, matching real Lightroom's own brush-options model (they apply to whatever gets painted *next*, not editable globally after the fact). `radius` is a normalized fraction of image WIDTH only (a single scalar) — deliberately, since rasterization happens directly in an offscreen canvas sized to the image's own native pixel resolution, where a single `radius * nativeWidth` used for both dimensions of `ctx.arc()` is inherently a true circle with no separate axis scaling needed (simpler than radial's `radiusX`/`radiusY`, which has to correct for aspect ratio because that geometry is evaluated analytically in normalized UV space).
- **A real, deliberate design divergence from linear/radial, named explicitly**: a brush mask can span *multiple separate strokes* (paint, lift the pointer, paint again, all adding to the same mask), matching real Lightroom's actual model, rather than linear/radial's "one placement = one mask." Implemented with zero new persistent cross-stroke state: every `pointerdown` re-derives the paint target fresh from `selectedMaskId` — if it currently points at a brush mask, the stroke appends to it; otherwise a new mask is created and selected. A "New Brush" tool-strip button achieves "start fresh" simply by deselecting (`selectedMaskId = null`), reusing the exact same re-derivation logic rather than a parallel reset signal.
- **Rasterization**: each active brush mask owns a persistent `OffscreenCanvas` (sized to the image's native resolution, opaque-black-initialized — not the canvas default transparent, which is required for `"multiply"` erase compositing to correctly no-op over never-painted areas, see below) that is **never cleared** — only newly-added dabs are drawn onto it each `pointermove`, keeping per-move cost bound by texture resolution/upload cost, not stroke length (a long stroke's full dab list is never re-rendered from scratch). Uploaded via `device.queue.writeTexture` into a `texture_2d_array` (one layer per active brush mask, sized to the same combined `MAX_MASKS`=8 budget every mask kind shares — a single shared texture would have silently broken true op-order interleaving and independent per-mask adjustments the moment there's more than one brush mask), indexed via the WGSL `Mask` struct's previously-unused `params.w` slot. Sampled via `textureSampleLevel` (not `textureSample`) since it sits inside a per-mask branch and has no implicit-derivative uniformity restriction to worry about.
- **Add vs. erase compositing, and a real correctness bug caught and fixed before it shipped**: add dabs use `"lighter"` (additive, clamped at full weight — matches real flow/density build-up, confirmed as the deliberately *additive*, not max-union, model, since two overlapping partial-flow dabs should build toward full coverage); erase dabs use `"multiply"` with a gradient from `(1-flow)` at the dab's center to `1.0` at its edge. The bug: a fresh `OffscreenCanvas` defaults to fully *transparent*, and Porter-Duff `"multiply"` against a transparent destination lets the erase gradient's own color show through directly (no destination alpha to constrain it) — meaning an erase stroke over an area that was never painted would incorrectly *paint* weight into it instead of correctly staying a no-op. Fixed by initializing every new canvas to opaque black before any dab is drawn, so `multiply`'s simplified opaque-vs-opaque formula (`Co = Cs × Cd`) correctly yields 0 regardless of the erase color wherever nothing has been painted yet. Caught during implementation by reasoning through the exact Porter-Duff composite math, not discovered empirically.
- **CPU parity (`develop_engine.rs`)**: a `Mask::Brush` variant with the identical per-dab accumulation formula — `add` dabs: `weight = (weight + falloff).min(1.0)`; `erase` dabs: `weight *= 1.0 - falloff` — the exact multiplicative reduction the canvas's own `"multiply"` composite produces, confirmed matching so both renderers agree. `aspect` (image height/width) threads through `Mask::weight` to reconstruct the same true-circle-in-pixel-space behavior the offscreen canvas gets "for free," since this CPU path has no canvas to rasterize into.
- **UI**: `MaskToolStrip.svelte` gained a Brush tool button, Size/Feather/Flow sliders (Feather shown is `100 - hardness`, matching the app's existing gradient-mask "Feather" convention even though the underlying `Dab` field is named `hardness`), an Erase toggle, and a "New Brush" button, plus a third independent per-kind chip counter ("Brush N"). `MaskEditorPanel.svelte` got a **real** third branch (not a free ride, per design review before implementation) — brush masks show Exposure/Contrast/Saturation/Invert/Delete but no Feather row, since softness is baked per-dab, not mask-level. `DevelopCanvas.svelte` shows a live circular brush-size cursor (aspect-corrected the same way the radial mask's on-screen-circle drag already is) that follows the pointer on hover, not just while painting. A fast drag interpolates intermediate dabs (spaced ~25% of the brush radius apart) so a stroke doesn't come out gappy/dotted.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app** (throwaway test harness temporarily added to `+page.svelte`'s `onMount`, removed before commit — real dispatched `PointerEvent`s against the real rendered canvas, real IPC round-trips through `flushEditStack`/`getEditStack`/`export_images` against the real persisted catalog; reported back via the existing `report_spike_result` Tauri command printing to the terminal running `cargo run`, since this app has no way to screenshot its native window and WKWebView's own console isn't forwarded there): a multi-point stroke produced correctly-interpolated, correctly-ordered dabs in the real catalog; a second stroke with no deselect appended to the *same* mask (multi-stroke-per-mask confirmed); clicking "New Brush" then painting created a genuinely separate second mask; toggling Erase and painting appended a dab with `mode:"erase"`; `canvas.width`/`canvas.height` correctly reflected the decoded image's real dimensions after load with no error overlay present, confirming the WGSL pipeline (including the new texture array and its bind-group entry) initialized without throwing — and again after switching to a second, different image and back, confirming the brush texture array and its rasterization state correctly reset and re-populated per image; a real export (via `develop_engine.rs`'s CPU path, the same code the new unit tests cover) with a +3EV boost on the painted mask produced `srgb(163,211,249)` → `srgb(255,255,255)` at a painted point and a byte-identical `srgb(166,140,113)` → `srgb(166,140,113)` at an untouched corner — real, visible, correctly-scoped proof of the effect, not just a claim that the command succeeded.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings. `cargo test --lib`: 80/80 passing (5 new brush parity cases: center dab, exact-radius-boundary exclusion, outside-every-dab, overlapping add dabs union additively not via max, and an erase dab reducing weight multiplicatively — each hand-computed before writing the assertion, same discipline as every prior mask slice).

## M3 Slice 6 — Local adjustments: radial gradient masks (2026-07-31)

Second local-adjustment mask type, reusing the multi-instance masking infrastructure linear gradient masks (Slice 5) built — the WGSL `Mask` struct's `params.z` "kind" slot, reserved specifically for this, is now used (0=linear, 1=radial). The adjustment brush stays a separate future slice (freehand painting is a genuinely different, heavier UI/data model).

- **New `radial_gradient_mask` op**: `{center, radiusX, radiusY, feather, invert, exposure, contrast, saturation}`, defaults `feather: 50` and `invert: false` (effect applies OUTSIDE the ellipse by default) — deliberately matching real Adobe Lightroom's own Radial Filter convention (its classic vignette use case; checking Invert flips it to spotlight/subject-enhancement inside application), confirmed against real Lightroom behavior by design review before implementation, not assumed.
- **WGSL + `develop_engine.rs`**: same ellipse-distance parametrization in both (`d` = 0 at center, 1 at the boundary), same feather-widens-the-band-around-the-boundary model as linear's midpoint-band model, same invert-flip approach (`invert ? insideWeight : 1-insideWeight`). Fixed a **real ordering bug found by design review before implementation**, not caught by testing: the Rust side had been about to parse linear and radial masks into two separate `Vec`s and apply "all linear, then all radial," which would silently diverge from the edit stack's TRUE op order (and from what the WGSL side already does correctly, since its packed array preserves order from one unfiltered pass) the moment a user interleaves the two kinds. Fixed with a unified `Mask` enum (`Mask::Linear`/`Mask::Radial`) parsed in one ordered pass.
- **UI**: dragging with the Radial Gradient tool active places a circular mask by applying the SAME native-pixel radius to both axes (not equal normalized fractions) — a true on-screen circle regardless of the image's own aspect ratio, with the underlying data model (independent `radiusX`/`radiusY`) already supporting true ellipse-dragging "for free" if a later slice wants it. Explicitly documented as a deliberate deviation from real Lightroom's actual free-form bounding-box ellipse drag, not left to read as an oversight. A minimum-radius guard (mirroring linear's minimum-drag-size guard) rejects an accidental near-zero placement — radius is a divisor in both WGSL and Rust, so a zero value would corrupt the frame with Inf/NaN. `MaskToolStrip.svelte` gained a second tool button and independent per-kind chip numbering ("Gradient N"/"Radial N"); `MaskEditorPanel.svelte`'s title became kind-aware (the sliders/checkbox/delete body is unchanged, confirmed already kind-agnostic).
- **Real bug found and fixed during empirical verification, not caught by any unit test or design review**: dragging an existing mask's center or radius handle silently did nothing — the persisted mask never updated. Root-caused via temporary instrumentation to a code-ordering bug: `handleMaskHandlePointerDown` called `e.currentTarget.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId)` *before* setting `handleDragState`, and `setPointerCapture` on that button element (unlike the canvas's own capture calls elsewhere in this file, which have never been observed to throw) threw — silently aborting the rest of the function before `handleDragState` was ever set, so the subsequent `pointermove` handler saw no drag in progress and did nothing. Confirmed precisely via a debug trace (`down` fired, `move` saw `hasState=false`) before concluding the diagnosis, not guessed. Fixed by reordering (`handleDragState` set first) and wrapping the capture/release calls in try/catch, since pointer capture is what keeps a drag working if the pointer exits the small handle's hit area mid-drag, not a strict requirement — applied consistently to the canvas-level pan/zoom/placement handlers too, closing off the same latent risk class there even though it had never been observed to fail.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app** (throwaway query-param-gated test driver, removed before commit; an earlier pass produced an ambiguous result once the dev catalog had accumulated masks across several test runs, so the final pass resets the target image's edit stack first and reads back through the real `getEditStack` IPC command at each step, not DOM-position diffing): placement lands correctly-shaped ops in the real catalog; the minimum-radius guard rejects a tiny drag while correctly leaving the tool engaged for an immediate retry; dragging the center handle moved the persisted center to exactly the intended target; dragging the radius handle grew the persisted radius proportionally (same aspect ratio as placement); **export inspected via real pixel values** confirmed default (outside) leaves the center unaffected while brightening the far pixel, and invert (inside) does the exact opposite; the aspect-ratio/distortion check from Slice 5 (`ratioDiff: 0`) held with a radial mask active, confirming no regression in the shared canvas/overlay rendering path.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings. `cargo test --lib`: 75/75 passing (5 new: `develop_engine.rs`'s radial parity cases).

## M3 Slice 5 — Local adjustments: linear gradient masks (2026-07-30)

First slice of M3's "real" local-adjustment scope (PRD/MILESTONES.md: "Local adjustments: linear gradient, radial gradient, adjustment brush... all as composable masks feeding the edit stack"). Deliberately scoped to linear gradient only — radial gradient and the (much heavier UI/data-model) adjustment brush are separate follow-up slices, matching this project's "prove the hard technical path with the smallest real instance first" precedent (M1 Slice 3). Comparable in size/risk to that original Develop-pipeline build: a new WGSL uniform-array layout, new Rust CPU parity code, a new multi-instance edit-stack op shape, and an entirely new UI paradigm.

- **New op shape, id-keyed, not name-based**: `{"op": "linear_gradient_mask", "id": "<uuid>", "start": {x,y}, "end": {x,y}, "feather": 0-100, "invert": bool, "exposure": ..., "contrast": ..., "saturation": ...}` — the existing `opValue`/`upsertOp` global-slider helpers assume one instance per name, which doesn't hold once several gradients can exist on one image; `develop.js` gained a separate `createLinearGradientMask`/`listMasks`/`addMask`/`updateMask`/`removeMask` CRUD surface. Coordinates normalized (0..1) so they stay valid across preview resolutions/zoom.
- **WGSL**: a bounded `array<Mask, 8>` at a new `@binding(3)`, each `Mask` packed entirely into `vec4`-multiples (48 bytes) specifically to sidestep WGSL's vec2/vec3-in-array uniform-alignment footguns — confirmed correct by design review before implementation, including that `layout:"auto"` handles the new binding the same way it already handles the existing `Adjustments` uniform. Masks apply after the existing global exposure/contrast/saturation pass (matches real Lightroom's own local-adjustments-on-top-of-global-grade ordering). Feather widens the transition band symmetrically around the midpoint (at feather=50 the pins themselves move to weight 0.25/0.75, not just the corners softening) — a deliberate choice confirmed to match real Lightroom's own gradient-feather model, documented so it doesn't read as a bug later.
- **`develop_engine.rs` CPU parity**: `apply_edit_stack` now also parses `linear_gradient_mask` ops and applies the identical projection-onto-segment/feather/mix math per pixel, in the same order as the WGSL shader. Parity test table expanded with four hand-computed mask cases (before-start, after-end, midpoint, and a feathered case pinning the "anchor moves off 0/1" behavior) — each value computed via script, not eyeballed, matching this module's own established discipline from the Slice 4 extraction.
- **UI**: `activeTool` on `DevelopCanvas` hard-branches pointer routing (mask placement vs. pan/zoom are mutually exclusive, confirmed by design review as a real requirement, not a nice-to-have) — dragging with a mask tool active never pans, even in 100% zoom mode, and vice versa. Existing masks get draggable HTML handles overlaid on the canvas (easier to hit-test/drag as real DOM nodes than GPU-rendered geometry); a new bottom `MaskToolStrip.svelte` (matching UX-DESIGN.md's documented-but-previously-unbuilt "contextual tool strip... for crop/mask/heal tools") holds the tool toggle and a click-to-select mask list; a new floating `MaskEditorPanel.svelte` (not a `DevelopPanel.svelte` section — masks are multi-instance and geometrically anchored, no existing static-section pattern fits) shows the selected mask's sliders, feather, invert, and delete. The 8-mask cap is enforced in the UI, matching the WGSL array's fixed size.
- **Real regression found AFTER initial empirical verification passed — reported directly by the user watching the app, not caught by any automated check**: the first implementation wrapped canvas in a plain `<div class="canvas-frame">` sized via a CSS `aspect-ratio` hint (needed to give the mask overlay a predictable box). A `<div>` has no genuine intrinsic aspect ratio the way a `<canvas>` does, and under flexbox's default `align-items: stretch` (never overridden on `.canvas-wrap`), the frame's height could resolve independently of the `aspect-ratio` hint — non-uniformly stretching the canvas rendered inside it. **Fixed by reverting canvas to being the directly-sized flex item** (restoring its pre-Slice-5, already-proven-correct `max-width/max-height/margin:auto` behavior — a canvas is a genuine replaced element with real intrinsic sizing) and instead syncing the mask overlay's position/size to canvas's own rendered box via `ResizeObserver` (fires on every resize reason — image load, zoom toggle, window resize — with ratioDiff `0` confirmed numerically in Fit, 100%, and back-to-Fit, not just visually eyeballed after the fix). Named here as a real lesson: this class of layout bug is exactly what a screenshot would have caught immediately and no automated check was checking for — added a precise `getBoundingClientRect()`-vs-`canvas.width/height` ratio assertion to the empirical pass specifically because of this.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app** (throwaway query-param-gated test driver temporarily added to the real `+page.svelte`, removed before commit — real dispatched `Pointer`/`MouseEvent`s against real rendered DOM): placing a gradient lands a correctly-shaped op in the real catalog; dragging a handle updates its stored coordinates; multiple masks compose (up to the 8-mask cap, confirmed the tool button disables exactly at the cap); pan/zoom is fully disabled while a tool is active and fully restored once deactivated; handles track panning correctly per-axis (confirmed via real `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` diagnostics — one axis legitimately had no overflow for the specific test image, not a bug); **export was called directly against the real backend** with a controlled gradient (full +2EV boost on one side) and the output JPEG's actual pixels inspected via ImageMagick — `srgb(154,144,135)` before the gradient's start point, `srgb(255,255,255)` (correctly clipped-white from the boost) after its end point, `srgb(79,62,42)` at the partial-blend midpoint — proving the mask is correctly interpreted end-to-end through the real IPC/export pipeline, not just that the UI drew lines on screen.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings. `cargo test --lib`: 70/70 passing (4 new: `develop_engine.rs`'s mask parity cases; existing suite unchanged).

## M3 Slice 4 — Extract the develop engine (2026-07-30)

## M3 Slice 4 — Extract the develop engine (2026-07-30)

Last of M3's four front-loaded scope items. `export.rs` — a module about JPEG file export — also owned `op_value`/`apply_edit_stack`, the real per-pixel exposure/contrast/saturation math shared with `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s WGSL shader, `pub(crate)` specifically because `import.rs` independently called it too (post-edit thumbnail regeneration). That ad hoc reuse across an unrelated module was the actual smell.

- **A real scope decision, made explicitly and reviewed before implementation, not assumed**: "consolidate into a single module boundary" had two readings — (A) introduce native `wgpu` and headless-render the export path off the *exact same* WGSL shader source as the interactive preview (true single-execution unification), or (B) consolidate what's actually consolidatable today — extract the CPU-side canonical logic into its own module, strengthen its tests, and document the parity obligation with the WGSL shader explicitly. **Chose (B)**, with (A) named and deliberately deferred to M5 (which already owns "GPU pipeline... with correct, seamless CPU fallback" as dedicated scope) rather than folded in here. Reasoning, confirmed independently by a design review: MILESTONES.md's own wording already frames this item as setting up M5's later work "rather than competing with it"; (A) would relitigate ADR-0004's own already-made "CPU is fine for export" call as a side effect of a basic-infra item; and (A) would introduce this codebase's first async-by-design native dependency (`wgpu`'s device/adapter/readback API is async even for headless rendering) purely to save hand-syncing three simple formulas — a poor trade for a project already burned once on unverified cross-platform GPU/build assumptions (ADR-0003's Windows vcpkg saga). Documented as a dated update in `docs/adr/ADR-0004-rendering-and-color-management.md`, not just here — this is an architecture decision, not just a progress note.
- **New `src-tauri/src/develop_engine.rs`**: pure code motion, `op_value`/`apply_edit_stack` moved out of `export.rs` verbatim (no behavior change), with a module doc comment naming it the canonical CPU-side reference and explicitly cross-referencing `DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s WGSL `fs_main` as the hand-synced parity counterpart (and the reverse pointer added to the WGSL comment too — either file now leads a reader to the other). `export.rs` and `import.rs`'s thumbnail-regeneration call site both updated to the new path; `lib.rs` gains `mod develop_engine;`.
- **Expanded the parity test table** from one hand-derived combined-ops case to seven: the original combined case, a clean passthrough, pure exposure (+1EV doubling), pure negative exposure (-1EV halving), pure contrast (a bright pixel pushed brighter), full desaturation (collapses to `(luma,luma,luma)` — verified the formula's own algebraic identity, not just spot-checked), and a clamp-past-white edge case. Each expected value hand-computed precisely (via a small Python script, not eyeballed) before writing the assertion, same `±2/255` tolerance the shader's own numeric smoke test already established. This broader table — not just the code move — is the real safety net for future drift, since there's no way to automatically cross-check the CPU implementation against the WGSL shader without the deferred (A) path.
- No behavior change anywhere: `cargo test --lib` confirms every existing test still passes unmodified (66/66, up from 61 — 7 new parity cases in `develop_engine.rs`, minus the 2 that moved out of `export.rs`), and no empirical running-app pass was needed for this slice (pure internal refactor, zero new UI surface, zero runtime behavior change) — a deviation from every prior slice's smoke-test verification, deliberate and named rather than skipped by oversight.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings (comment-only Svelte change).

## M3 Slice 3 — Develop pan/zoom & basic view controls (2026-07-30)

Third of M3's front-loaded scope additions. Develop's canvas was fit-to-window only — the entire "fit" mechanism was a bare `max-width/max-height:100%` CSS rule on the `<canvas>` in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`, no zoom-level state, no pan anywhere. Added Fit, 100% (true 1:1 canvas-backing-store pixels), zoom-to-point (click the image to toggle, centered on the click), and click-drag pan while zoomed — all self-contained in `DevelopCanvas.svelte`, no new props, no backend changes.

- **Real bug found and fixed by design review before implementation, not by testing**: the existing `.canvas-wrap`'s `align-items:center; justify-content:center` flex centering is a classic "scroll trap" once the canvas overflows in 100% mode — centering an overflowing flex item computes a negative starting scroll offset that clamps to 0, permanently hiding the "before center" portion of the image on *both* axes (no explicit `flex-direction` means `row`, so both centering properties are affected). Fixed with `margin: auto` on the canvas itself instead of centering the flex container — per the flexbox spec, auto margins absorb free space when the item fits (identical look to the old Fit behavior) and resolve to `0` when it overflows (normal, fully scrollable, no trap). One CSS rule correctly handles both modes.
- **Pan reuses native browser scroll clamping instead of hand-rolled math**: in 100% mode, `.canvas-wrap` becomes a real `overflow:auto` scroll container and the canvas renders at its true intrinsic size; drag-to-pan just writes `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop` directly from pointer deltas — the browser clamps to `[0, scrollWidth - clientWidth]` automatically, no manual bounds-checking needed.
- Click vs. drag disambiguation via a 4px movement threshold on `pointerup`; `canvasEl.setPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture` so a fast drag exiting the canvas near an edge doesn't stop delivering `pointermove`; `touch-action:none` on the canvas so native touch-scrolling doesn't fight the JS-driven scroll writes.
- Zoom-to-point converts the click's CSS-pixel position to a native canvas-backing-store coordinate using **per-axis** scale factors (`getBoundingClientRect()` width/height ratios computed separately, not one shared ratio — fractional CSS-pixel rounding can differ slightly per axis even though aspect ratio is preserved), then centers the viewport on it after `await tick()` (required: Svelte 5 runes still batch `$state`-triggered DOM patches to a microtask, so reading post-zoom layout synchronously would see stale dimensions).
- Small "Fit"/"100%" badge in the canvas corner, also clickable — discoverability beyond click-on-image.
- Zoom state resets to `"fit"` whenever the open image changes (a pure view concern, not persisted).
- Named, accepted limitation: "100%" is one canvas backing-store pixel per CSS pixel, not per physical device pixel — on HiDPI this looks ~2x more zoomed than true 1:1 physical pixels. Matches the component's existing intrinsic-sizing behavior already in place for Fit, not a new inconsistency introduced here.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app** (throwaway query-param-gated test driver temporarily added to the real `+page.svelte`, removed before commit — real dispatched `Pointer`/`MouseEvent`s against the real rendered canvas, not handler functions called directly): confirmed Fit renders at the expected downscaled size (811×608 against a 2048×1536 native preview) with the "zoomed" class absent; clicking the zoom badge engages 100% mode with the canvas's rendered CSS size exactly equal to its native backing-store size; **the scroll-trap fix specifically verified** by forcing scroll to both `(0,0)` and `(scrollWidth-clientWidth, scrollHeight-clientHeight)` and confirming both are reachable (the axis the review flagged as previously unreachable under naive flex centering); overshoot past either edge (both positive and negative) confirmed clamped by the browser, not the app; a real drag-panning sequence via dispatched `PointerEvent`s moved `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop` by exactly the expected delta; clicking the badge again returned to Fit with the display size restored exactly to its original value.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings. `cargo test --lib`: 61/61 passing (unchanged — this slice touched only one Svelte component).

## M3 Slice 2 — Quick-action keyboard shortcuts (2026-07-30)

Second of M3's front-loaded scope additions. Library's flag/star/color-label controls were click-only per-cell badge-row buttons (`GridCell.svelte`) — no keyboard-shortcut layer existed anywhere in this codebase. Added the standard Lightroom Classic Library scheme: `0`-`5` directly set star rating (0 clears, not a toggle — matches real Lightroom, unlike this app's own mouse click-same-star-to-clear behavior, which stays as-is); `P`/`X` toggle Pick/Reject on and off; `U` always hard-clears to unflagged regardless of prior state (a third, distinct key); `6`/`7`/`8`/`9` toggle Red/Yellow/Green/Blue on and off. Purple has no default key in real Lightroom, so none is bound here either.

- Extended the existing `handleLibraryKeydown` window-level handler in place (not a second listener) — it already existed for Delete/Backspace-to-remove. Reused `handleRatingChange`/`handleFlagChange`/`handleColorLabelChange(selectedId, value)` directly with zero new batching logic: `selectedId` (the anchor) is always a member of `selectedIds` whenever there's a real multi-selection, so those handlers' existing `targetVersionIds` resolver already batches to the whole selection "for free."
- Guard list (confirmed necessary, not defensive bloat, by a design review before implementation: none of this app's dialogs autofocus their own input or stop-propagate non-Escape keys, so without it a stray keypress could silently act on a photo through an open dialog): `activeModule === "library"`; none of `confirmingRemoval`/`exportItems`/`creatingCollection`/`creatingSmartCollection`/`creatingCollectionWithImages`/`settingsOpen`/`backupPromptOpen` are active; event target is not an `HTMLInputElement`/`HTMLTextAreaElement`/**`HTMLSelectElement`** (the select case is new — two real `<select>`s exist now, the toolbar "Add to Collection…" and Settings' frequency select, neither a concern when this guard was first written); no `metaKey`/`ctrlKey`/`altKey` held.
- `GridCell.svelte`: appended the shortcut to each control's existing `title` tooltip (`"Rate 3 stars (3)"`, `"Pick (P)"`, `"Reject (X)"`, `"Color label (6-9 red/yellow/green/blue)"`) — discoverability only, no behavior change.
- No backend changes — `setRating`/`setFlag`/`setColorLabel` and their Tauri commands are unchanged.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app** (throwaway query-param-gated test driver temporarily added to the real `+page.svelte`, removed before commit — script-dispatched real `KeyboardEvent`/`MouseEvent`s against real rendered DOM, not handler functions called directly): confirmed via precise before/after snapshot diffs against the real dev catalog (not aggregate "does any image have X" checks, which turned out unreliable given this catalog's pre-existing rated/flagged images from real prior sessions) — pressing `3` twice on a freshly-selected image leaves it at rating 3 (direct-set, not toggle); `P`/`P` toggles Pick on then off; `X` then `U` hard-clears regardless of the prior Reject; `6`/`6` toggles Red on then off; a Shift-click multi-selection followed by a rating key changes *exactly* the selected images, all to the same value; a keypress while "New Collection" is open makes zero change to any image; a keypress dispatched from the focused "Add to Collection…" `<select>` also makes zero change. Dev catalog's test-mutated rows were reset to their pre-test values afterward.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings. `cargo test --lib`: 61/61 passing (unchanged — no Rust logic touched).

## M3 Slice 1 — Settings/Preferences dialog (2026-07-30)

First of M3's four front-loaded scope additions. Before this, the only settings-editing UI anywhere in the app was `BackupPromptDialog`, the M2 close-prompt dialog — deliberately scoped at the time as "the only settings surface, a future settings surface can add a manual trigger later." This slice builds that surface and establishes the pattern: a future configurable feature adds a `<section>` to `SettingsDialog.svelte`, not a bespoke one-off dialog.

- **New `SettingsDialog.svelte`**: self-contained (fetches its own data on open, matching `MetadataPanel`'s pattern), one `<section>` per settings group — only "Backup" exists today, no sidebar/tab navigation (would be over-building for one section). Reuses `getBackupSettings`/`updateBackupSettings`/`performCatalogBackup` from `backup.js` — **zero backend changes**, since the `settings` table was already a generic key/value store from the M2 backup slice.
- Every field persists immediately on change (frequency select, folder picker, both checkboxes) — no Save/Cancel step, matching how `MetadataPanel`'s IPTC fields already save on blur/change; each write sends the full settings object (`update_backup_settings` upserts all 5 keys unconditionally, no partial-update path).
- **Also added a "Back Up Now" button** in the Backup section, reusing `performCatalogBackup` exactly like `BackupPromptDialog.handleBackUpNow` does. This directly resolves a previously named-and-deferred item ("no manual Back Up Now trigger outside the close prompt — no settings surface exists yet") now that the blocking reason is gone.
- **Real gap found by design review before implementation, not by testing**: `SettingsDialog` and `BackupPromptDialog` share the same `.overlay{position:fixed;inset:0;z-index:100}` shell. If a user opened Settings and then triggered an OS close while it was still open, the close handler would still try to show `BackupPromptDialog` underneath — whichever dialog is later in DOM order silently swallows all clicks, which could leave the app stuck mid-close (`event.preventDefault()` already called, `resolveBackupPrompt` never firing). Fixed by force-closing Settings (`settingsOpen = false`) as the literal first statement in `onCloseRequested`, before any `await` — a deterministic ordering guarantee from JS's single-threaded execution, not a timing-dependent fix, so this was verified by direct source inspection rather than a black-box close-flow run (see verification note below).
- New titlebar "⚙" button, always visible (not module-scoped, unlike Export/Remove) — placed at the far right after Import Folder, matching the existing precedent that Import Files/Import Folder are already the app-level, non-module-scoped actions living at that end of the titlebar.
- One stale doc-comment fixed in `catalog.rs`'s `migrate()`: the note that the close-prompt was "the *only* place backup settings can ever be changed" is no longer true.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app** (throwaway smoke route mounting `SettingsDialog` directly, script-dispatching real `change`/`click` events on the real rendered controls — same technique used throughout this project): confirmed the dialog opens with real persisted settings, a frequency change and a checkbox toggle each persist correctly via `sqlite3` on the real catalog *and* correctly compose with each other (an earlier field's change survives a later one, since each write's local snapshot already includes prior local edits) — one early run showed `folder` regressing to `null` after several writes, traced to a test-harness race (the dialog's own on-open fetch firing before an out-of-band seed script's write had committed, not a reachable production scenario since real users only ever set the folder through the dialog's own picker) and confirmed fixed by re-sequencing the test, not the app. "Back Up Now" produced a real, valid backup file at the chosen path, with `last_backup_at` updated to match in both the file's timestamp and the catalog row. The dialog-stacking fix itself (`settingsOpen = false` at the top of `onCloseRequested`) was checked by direct source-level reasoning rather than a live close-triggered run — it's a synchronous assignment guaranteed to execute before the function's first `await`, not contingent on scheduling — stated here plainly rather than claimed as something it wasn't.
- `npm run check`: 0 errors/warnings. `cargo test --lib`: 61/61 passing (unchanged — this slice touched no Rust logic, only a doc comment).

## M2 final slice — Catalog backup (2026-07-29)

Last remaining item on M2's scope list, resumed after being deliberately reprioritized behind the thumbnail-refresh/Filmstrip work above. Per PRD §7.6: prompt on app close with a configurable frequency (every time/daily/weekly/monthly/never), write a timestamped copy of the catalog file to a user-chosen folder kept separate from the working catalog, with an optional integrity check and optional vacuum/compact. Catalog file only — photos are never touched.

Planned via a reviewed design doc. The review corrected the design's central architectural premise: reading Tauri's actual shipped `onCloseRequested` implementation (`node_modules/@tauri-apps/api/window.js`) showed `event.preventDefault()` calling *after* an `await` is Tauri's own documented pattern, not something to engineer around — this eliminated an entire layer of planned startup-snapshot machinery in favor of just re-fetching settings fresh at the top of the close handler. It also flagged a real, deliberately-accepted mutex-hold-duration tradeoff, corrected the first-run default (a `never`-by-default would have made the feature permanently unreachable, since the close-prompt dialog is the *only* place backup settings can ever be changed), and confirmed `rusqlite::backup::Backup` against a WAL-mode source needs no manual checkpoint.

- **New `settings` key/value table**, greenfield — no settings/preferences storage existed anywhere in this codebase before this. `migrate()` inserts idempotent defaults (`backup_frequency: "weekly"`, not `"never"` — see above) on every `open()`, matching this project's existing "not a real migration system, but real enough" precedent.
- **`Catalog::perform_backup`**: canonicalizes and rejects a destination that's the same as or nested inside the app's own data folder (PRD explicitly requires "kept separate," not just a UI hint); runs `PRAGMA integrity_check` first if requested and refuses to write a backup of data already known to be corrupt; runs `VACUUM` if requested; copies via `rusqlite::backup::Backup` — SQLite's real Online Backup API, walking the source's pager rather than raw file bytes, the WAL-safe way to copy a live database without a manual checkpoint step.
- **Real bug found and fixed during empirical verification, not caught by any unit test**: the Online Backup API copies the source's pages verbatim, *including the header byte that records journal mode* — so a backup taken from this app's live WAL-mode catalog came out flagged as WAL-mode too. Confirmed on disk: opening the freshly-written backup file (even just to inspect it with the `sqlite3` CLI) spawned its own `-shm`/`-wal` sidecar files next to it, which is exactly wrong for what's supposed to be one portable, restorable artifact. Fixed by converting the destination connection to `journal_mode=DELETE` immediately after the backup completes — this checkpoints any residual WAL content into the main file and flips the header, confirmed by both a new unit test asserting `journal_mode` is `"delete"` on the finished copy, and by re-running the real smoke test and confirming no sidecar files appeared even after querying the file directly.
- **Deliberate, named exception to this codebase's own "release the mutex before slow work" convention** (`export_images`'s existing precedent): `perform_backup` holds `AppState.catalog`'s lock for its entire duration (integrity check + optional VACUUM + file copy), rather than releasing it before the slow part. Accepted because this is only reachable once, from the close-prompt dialog, immediately before the window is destroyed — documented in a comment matching `export_images`'s own style rather than silently deviating.
- **New `BackupPromptDialog.svelte`** serves both "configure" and "perform" at once, matching real Lightroom's own on-close-only prompt (no persistent settings screen exists anywhere in this app to host anything else). The close handler needed a genuinely new pattern for this codebase: every prior dialog is fire-and-forget from its caller's perspective, but this one needs the close handler to actually *wait* for the user's choice before destroying the window — a small promise-bridge (`showBackupPromptAndWait`/`resolveBackupPrompt`) does that, and "Skip This Time" is always available even if "Back Up Now" fails, so the wait can never trap the user unable to quit.
- **A real bug in my own throwaway verification script, not the shipped code**: the first empirical-verification attempt silently produced no result at all — the smoke test's close sequence called `unlisten()` (copied from the M1 Slice 6 smoke test's precedent) immediately before `getCurrentWindow().close()`, which unregistered the close handler *before* the close event fired, so the window just destroyed itself with zero interception. Root-caused by checking `ps aux` (the process had already exited with no report ever printed) rather than assuming the marker-wait command was still running; fixed by not unlistening before the actual close call.
- **Verified empirically through the real running app**, both branches: forced a stale `last_backup_at` via `sqlite3` on the real dev catalog, drove a real close through the actual `+page.svelte` close handler and the actual `BackupPromptDialog` component (via a throwaway smoke route, script-dispatching a real `MouseEvent` click on the real rendered button, same technique used for the Filmstrip slice above), and confirmed for **Back Up Now**: a real, valid, independently-openable backup file landed at the chosen folder (`PRAGMA integrity_check` → `ok`, correct row data, `journal_mode` → `delete`), and `last_backup_at` updated to the real completion time in the actual catalog; and for **Skip This Time**: no backup file was written, but `last_backup_at` still advanced (so skipping doesn't re-prompt on literally the next close). Smoke route and `tauri.conf.json`'s temporary window-`url` override were removed afterward; the real dev catalog's `settings` rows were reset to fresh defaults rather than left with test-session values.
- `cargo test --lib`: 61/61 passing (5 new: settings round-trip + idempotent defaults, a real on-disk backup that stays independently-openable and keeps `journal_mode=wal` on the *source* while landing `journal_mode=delete` on the *copy*, the destination-not-separate guard, and integrity-check blocking a deliberately corrupted catalog). `npm run check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings.
- **Deliberately deferred, named not silently dropped**: a manual "Back Up Now" trigger outside the close prompt, and any in-app restore UI — no menu/settings surface exists anywhere in this app yet to host either, and PRD's literal ask is prompt-on-close; recovery means pointing the app at a different catalog file, which needs no new UI (matches how real Lightroom itself handles this too).

## Thumbnail refresh after edit + persistent Filmstrip (2026-07-29)

User-requested, reprioritized ahead of catalog backup (the last M2 item): (1) the Library thumbnail never reflected a Develop edit — thumbnails were generated once at import from the "clean" decode and never touched again; (2) `docs/ux/UX-DESIGN.md` specifies a "persistent filmstrip along the bottom of both modules... so context is never lost switching between culling and editing," with a matching (non-interactive) mockup, but it was never actually built. Building the filmstrip also closed a real, separate functional gap: there was previously no way to move between images while already in Develop at all — the only entry point was double-clicking a Library grid cell.

Planned via a reviewed design doc. The review found **one real bug the original design would have shipped**: chaining thumbnail regeneration onto the same promise `pendingSave` tracks would have silently reintroduced the exact "app hangs unable to quit" class of bug M1 Slice 6 already found and fixed once, for the edit-stack flush itself. It also corrected the plan's own cost model (a Develop-preview-cache-hit reuse is a real decode+edit+resize+encode, not free — narrowed the regen hook to real transitions, not every 250ms debounce settle), found a genuine determinism gap in the proposed content-hashing scheme, corrected the exact SQL JOIN needed for a new catalog lookup, and found two real structural gaps in the filmstrip layout the first sketch missed.

- **Thumbnail regeneration reuses the Develop preview cache** (`preview_cache::ensure_develop_preview_for_hash`) rather than a fresh RAW/JPEG decode — cheaper, and the image has already been decoded once anyway by the time Develop is open. `export.rs`'s `apply_edit_stack` (the exact formula the WGSL shader uses) is now `pub(crate)` and reused directly, rather than duplicated.
- **Content-hash-keyed filename, not an overwrite-in-place**: writes to `{image_id}-{blake3(edit_stack_json)[..8]}.jpg`, never touching `{image_id}.jpg`. This codebase has zero cache-busting precedent anywhere (no query params, nothing) — overwriting the same path risked the webview's asset-protocol fetch showing stale cached bytes forever, the same class of bug `preview_cache.rs`'s own module doc already named and solved once for Develop previews. Old thumbnail files are left as accepted orphans, matching that module's already-documented tradeoff. **Corrected framing, not silently assumed**: `develop.js`'s `upsertOp` re-appends the touched op at the end of the array on every change, so reaching identical final values via a different edit order serializes and hashes differently — "bounded pileup, not none," not full dedup idempotency (verified directly: re-regenerating the *same* stack does resolve to the same file, confirmed by test).
- **The real bug, fixed before it shipped**: thumbnail regeneration is fired from a structurally separate promise chain (`regenerateThumbnailFor`), never chained onto `pendingSave`/`pendingIptcSave` — the exact mechanism M1 Slice 6 already fixed once for the edit-stack flush's own hang risk. Never awaited anywhere, including the window-close handler (direct precedent: `generate_missing_thumbnails`' own already-accepted "abandoned on force-quit is fine" posture) — a stale grid thumbnail after a crash is strictly lower stakes than a lost edit.
- **Hook point narrowed to real transitions** (leaving Develop via `switchModule`, switching images via `openDevelop` — captures the OLD `developVersionId` before it's reassigned, mirroring `flushEditStack`'s own already-correct capture-before-reassignment shape — plus `handleExportClick` and the close handler), not the bare 250ms debounce settle, since a user still actively dragging a slider would otherwise trigger a real decode+edit+resize+encode immediately superseded by the next tick.
- **New `Filmstrip.svelte`**, one shared component (matching UX-DESIGN.md's explicit wording) — thumbnail-only cells, no flag/star/color-label badges, matching the reviewed mockup's own explicit simplification for filmstrip cells specifically. **The review's structural fix**: hoisted as a single instance after the whole three-way `{#if library}{:else if developImagePath}{:else}{/if}` block (the first sketch only accounted for two of the three branches, which would have hidden the filmstrip in Develop before any image was ever opened that session — contradicting "persistent"). Clicking a cell in Library selects it (reusing `handleSelect`); clicking a cell in Develop switches the open image via the existing `openDevelop`.
- **The review's second structural fix**: `filteredImages` (driven by the active collection filter) is independent of `developVersionId` — if the open Develop image falls outside the current filter, a naive filmstrip would show no highlighted cell or render empty. `developFilmstripImages` falls back to the unfiltered catalog only in that specific case.
- **Verified empirically through the real app, not just unit tests**: opened a real cataloged RAW file in Develop via the actual `openDevelop`/`handleAdjustmentChange`/`switchModule` code path, confirmed via the filesystem that a new content-hashed thumbnail file appeared, the original import-time thumbnail was left untouched (accepted orphan), and the catalog's `thumbnail_path` pointed at the new file. Separately, since there's no way to screenshot the native window, drove and inspected the **real DOM**: confirmed `Filmstrip` renders the correct cell count in both modules, and dispatched a real `MouseEvent` click on a filmstrip cell in Develop mode, confirming it actually switched `developVersionId` to the clicked image.
- `cargo test --lib`: 56/56 passing (2 new: `get_version_source`'s JOIN, and a real-JPEG test confirming a regenerated thumbnail both reflects the edit and is correctly content-addressed/idempotent). `npm run check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings.

## M2 Slice 5 — Collections (manual) and Smart Collections (rule-based) (2026-07-28)

Last item on MILESTONES.md's M2 scope list requiring new UI (catalog backup is next, and doesn't touch the Library UI at all). The largest slice since Slice 3: the first time the Library rail becomes real/dynamic (a static "Folders > All Photos" placeholder since M1) and the first time this codebase needed a rule/predicate schema (confirmed via grep beforehand: zero prior art for any filter concept anywhere). Planned via a reviewed design doc that found one real, confirmed bug before any code shipped, corrected a factually wrong analogy in the plan's own reasoning, and found a genuinely cheap way to widen scope rather than just possible ones.

- **New tables**: `collections` (id/name/is_smart/rules_json, with a `CHECK` enforcing `is_smart` and `rules_json` stay consistent — a real, correctly-enforced constraint, unlike `keywords`' NULL-parent_id situation from last slice where the equivalent constraint would have silently done nothing) and `collection_images` (composite-PK join, manual collections only — smart collections never get rows there, their membership is always computed, never stored).
- **Rule evaluation stays entirely client-side, but for the right reason this time.** The plan's first draft justified this by claiming "Rust never interprets JSON blobs either, see `EditStack.ops`" — the review caught that this is factually wrong: `export.rs`'s `apply_edit_stack` does interpret `EditStack.ops`, to run the CPU-side export render. The actual, correct reason a JS-only evaluator is fine here: rule evaluation needs no pixel access, and every field it touches (`rating`/`flag`/`color_label`) is already present on every already-loaded `ImageSummary` row. Documented as an explicit schema comment so a future feature needing Rust-side action on "images in a smart collection" reaches for computing the id list in JS and passing it through the existing id-list commands, not a second Rust rule-interpreter.
- **"Keyword" included as a smart-collection field because it turned out to be genuinely cheap, not just possible** — the review found a one-query, no-joins `list_all_image_keywords()` that doesn't touch `list_images()` at all, fetched once. This is real design provenance, not opportunism: the reviewed mockup's own flagship smart-collection example is literally "Smart: 5★ + Untagged," and "Untagged" (has zero keywords) is now supported alongside "has a specific keyword," rating (>=/<=/==), flag, and color_label — all ANDed together, no OR/nesting for V1.
- **Real, confirmed bug caught by the review before it ever shipped**: once the Library grid starts showing a filtered subset (`filteredImages`) instead of the full catalog, `handleSelect`'s Shift-click range-selection logic — which computed range boundaries by indexing into the full `images` array — would have silently pulled hidden, filtered-out images into the selection, which then flows into remove/batch-culling/keyword-assignment against images the user never saw. Fixed by indexing into `filteredImages` instead. **Verified with a smoke test specifically designed to discriminate the bug, not just exercise the happy path**: a 3-image test put two non-adjacent images (a, c) into a collection, deliberately excluding the one between them (b) in catalog order, then Shift-clicked across the filtered pair — confirming `shiftSelectedCount === 2` (not 3) and that no selected id fell outside `filteredImages`. A naive test using adjacent images wouldn't have caught this at all.
- **Another real SQL-correctness catch, also verified rather than assumed**: a smart collection's `count` must come back `null`/`None`, not `0` — `COUNT()` over a `LEFT JOIN` with zero matched rows returns `0`, which would be silently indistinguishable from "not applicable" and invisible in testing (a brand-new smart collection legitimately has 0 real matches too). Explicitly overridden in Rust before serializing; confirmed via both a unit test and the real-app smoke test (`smartCountFromBackend: null`, not `0`).
- **Cache-invalidation discipline**: manual-collection membership is cached client-side per collection id, invalidated by whichever collection id was actually just mutated (add/remove-from-collection), never by whatever collection happens to currently be filtering the grid — those two differ exactly when the toolbar's "Add to Collection…" targets a different collection than the one being viewed, and invalidating the wrong one would silently leave the real target's cache stale.
- **Atomicity precedent followed, not reinvented**: the toolbar's "Add to Collection… → New Collection…" flow uses a new `create_collection_with_images` (one transaction, create + populate together) rather than two separate IPC calls — matching `assign_keyword_path`'s existing create-then-assign shape from the keywording slice, and avoiding the same failure mode (an error between two separate calls leaving an orphaned, empty, confusingly-named collection).
- **Rail becomes real for the first time**: Folders stays exactly as it was (a real disk-folder tree isn't a listed M2 scope bullet anywhere, despite UX-DESIGN.md's aspirational rail description — left untouched, not attempted this slice); a new Collections section lists manual and smart collections with live counts, click-to-filter, hover-reveal delete, and "+"/"+⚡" creation affordances. `.rail` gained `overflow-y: auto` — the review caught that today's rail had no scroll behavior at all (invisible with 2 static lines, a real visual defect the moment a variable-length list was added).
- **Deliberately deferred, matching this codebase's existing precedent for `add_image_with_metadata`/`add_edit_stack`** (lower-level building blocks kept ready without a UI trigger): collection rename, and editing an existing smart collection's rules (the backend `update_smart_collection_rules` command exists and is tested, but has no UI trigger yet — delete-and-recreate is the only way to change a smart collection's rules for now).
- **Verified empirically through the real app**, not just unit tests: created a manual collection with 2 images via the atomic path, confirmed the grid filtered to exactly those 2; ran the Shift-range regression test described above against real data; removed one image from the collection and confirmed membership updated; created a smart collection (rating ≥ 4) and confirmed both the client-side count and the backend's `null` count matched expectations; deleted both collections and confirmed via `sqlite3` that `collections` and `collection_images` were both empty afterward.
- `cargo test --lib`: 54/54 passing (6 new: atomic create-with-images, add/remove round-trip with idempotency, smart-collection rules round-trip + the count-is-null-not-zero assertion, delete-collection cleanup for both manual and smart, `remove_images`'s new orphaned-membership check, the flat keyword-assignment query). `npm run check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings.

## M2 Slice 4 — Keywording (hierarchical): data model + assignment UI (2026-07-28)

Next roadmap item per MILESTONES.md's M2 scope list — a separate bullet from Collections/Smart Collections and from search/filter (the exit criteria literally couple keyword-based search with smart collections as one later criterion, both still fully unbuilt). Scoped to keyword data model + per-image assignment UI only, matching this project's one-bullet-per-slice discipline. Deliberately deferred: keyword management (rename/delete/reparent an existing keyword — no rail/tree-browser UI exists yet to host it), keyword-based search/filter (confirmed genuinely not cheap to fold in — no keyword data flows into `list_images()`, no filter mechanism exists over the Library grid at all today), and the Folders/Collections rail (UX-DESIGN.md and the reviewed mockup both place Keywords in the right panel instead, as its own concern).

Planned via a reviewed design doc that included direct SQLite experiments against the pinned engine (not just reading docs) to settle two real correctness questions before writing code: **`UNIQUE(parent_id, name)` would NOT actually have prevented duplicate top-level keyword names** — SQLite treats every `NULL` as distinct in a UNIQUE check, verified by inserting two `(NULL, 'x')` rows and watching both succeed — so uniqueness is enforced at the application layer instead (find-or-create inside one transaction, safe because `AppState.catalog` is one `Arc<Mutex<Catalog>>`, no concurrent-writer race possible); and **multi-level self-referencing `ON DELETE CASCADE` was confirmed to actually cascade correctly** (deleting a grandparent keyword correctly removed a grandchild, verified directly) now that `foreign_keys` is enabled (M2 Slice 3's fix).

- **New tables** (`keywords`: id/name/parent_id self-referencing; `image_keywords`: composite-PK join table) — no `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` dance needed, since these are brand-new tables, not new columns on existing ones.
- **`Catalog::assign_keyword_path(image_ids, path_segments)`**: one transaction, walks a path like `["nature","birds","owl"]`, finds-or-creates each level, then `INSERT OR IGNORE`s the assignment for every image in the batch — this is what lets typing one keyword into the panel apply it to an entire multi-selection in one round trip, and lets re-typing the same path from a different image reuse the existing hierarchy instead of duplicating it (verified: re-resolving `["nature","birds","owl"]` for a second image returned the identical leaf id, and the keyword table still had exactly 3 rows, not 6).
- **Removal stays anchor-only** (`remove_keyword_from_image`), matching the existing IPTC caption/copyright/contact precedent — only *assignment* batches across a selection, not display or removal. `remove_images` (M2 Slice 3) gained one more explicit `DELETE FROM image_keywords` in its existing transaction, same "don't rely on CASCADE even with the pragma on" discipline as its `image_versions` delete.
- **Real async race caught and fixed before it shipped, not discovered by precedent**: the design review found that this codebase's one existing prop-triggered-`$effect` pattern (`DevelopCanvas.svelte`'s image loader) has no guard against a stale response overwriting newer state if triggered rapidly — but concluded that's tolerable there because switching Develop images is a rare, deliberate action, whereas rapid Library arrow-key/click navigation firing several keyword fetches in flight is a real, likely scenario. `MetadataPanel`'s keyword fetch is guarded: the target `image_id` is captured in the effect closure, and a response is only committed if that id still matches the currently-displayed image when it arrives.
- **Real UX gap caught by the same review**: batch-assigning a keyword to a multi-selection needs visible confirmation beyond the chip list, since the chips only ever reflect the anchor image (same as IPTC) — a newly-assigned keyword landing on N-1 *other* images would otherwise be invisible. Reused this project's existing `statusMessage` pattern (`runImport`/`handleRemoveConfirmed`'s precedent) via a new `onKeywordAssigned` callback: `"Added \"owl\" to 3 photos"`.
- **A planned verification step turned into a design decision instead, because it structurally couldn't be performed**: the plan called for visually checking an HTML `<datalist>`'s rendering in the real WKWebView dev window before committing to it as the suggestion-picker UI (WebKit's native datalist is well-documented to ignore all page theming). This app has no way to screenshot its own native window — a constraint that's held throughout this whole project — so that check was never actually possible. Rather than ship something unverifiable with a known, real theming problem against this panel's otherwise fully dark-themed inputs, swapped to a small manually-rendered, app-themed suggestion dropdown before ever running it, instead of shipping-then-maybe-fixing.
- **Verified empirically through the real app** (script-driven, calling the API functions directly the same way M2 Slice 2 verified IPTC): assigned `nature/birds/owl` to one real cataloged image, confirmed via `sqlite3` exactly 3 keyword rows were created with the correct parent chain; batch-assigned `kyoto` to two images at once, confirmed both got it; removed `kyoto` from just the first image and confirmed the second still had it (anchor-only removal, batch assignment — both directions of the asymmetry actually holding); `get_image_keywords`' returned `path` strings matched the real tree exactly (`"nature / birds / owl"`).
- `cargo test --lib`: 48/48 passing (5 new: nested-path creation/reuse, batch-assign idempotency, anchor-only removal, and `remove_images`'s new orphaned-keyword-assignment check). `npm run check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings.

## Follow-up: batch rate/flag/color-label on multi-select (2026-07-28)

MILESTONES.md's M2 scope bullet for "basic photo management, completed" explicitly covers "rate/flag/color-label... many at once," not just multi-select-then-remove — Slice 3's plan deliberately deferred this piece to keep that slice's scope contained. Small, frontend-only, no schema/backend change: `handleRatingChange`/`handleFlagChange`/`handleColorLabelChange` in `+page.svelte` now fan out to the whole `selectedIds` set (via a shared `targetVersionIds` helper) whenever the clicked cell is part of an active multi-selection (`selectedIds.size > 1 && selectedIds.has(versionId)`) — matching Lightroom's own behavior. Acting on a cell outside the current selection, or when only one image is selected, stays single-target, so an unrelated existing selection elsewhere in the grid can't leak into an unrelated click. Verified empirically: multi-selected 2 real images (plain click + Cmd-click), set a rating on one and a flag on the other, confirmed via a real `list_images()` round trip that **both** images ended up with both values applied. `cargo test --lib`: 44/44 unchanged (no backend surface touched). `npm run check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings.

## M2 Slice 3 — Library multi-select + remove from catalog + batch export: DONE (2026-07-28)

Closes the last piece of the user's original M2 ask ("multi-select, delete from catelogs"). Batch export rode along nearly free: M1 Slice 5 deliberately built `export_batch` to take a `Vec` specifically so this would be "a frontend-only follow-up later," not a backend rewrite. Planned via a reviewed design doc — the review found two real pre-existing bugs before any code was written and one real type-contract break the design itself would have introduced.

- **Real pre-existing bug found while designing removal, not by accident**: `PRAGMA foreign_keys` was never enabled anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by grep) — meaning the schema's `image_versions.image_id ... ON DELETE CASCADE` has been **inert since M1 Slice 1**. A naive `DELETE FROM images` would have silently orphaned `image_versions` rows (invisible garbage `list_images()`'s JOIN would just stop returning, not a visible error). Fixed with a small `enable_foreign_keys()` helper called from both `Catalog::open()` and `open_in_memory()` — but **not** by calling full `harden()` from the in-memory path, which the review caught as a real landmine: `harden()`'s WAL `debug_assert_eq!` would panic every debug-build test, since an in-memory database reports journal_mode `"memory"`, never `"wal"`. `Catalog::remove_images` still deletes child rows **explicitly**, not relying on CASCADE even now that the pragma is live — correctness shouldn't hinge on a per-connection pragma succeeding.
- **New `Catalog::remove_images`**: one transaction per batch, unknown ids are a silent no-op (not an error — a double-fired removal or one racing a refresh should converge on "row is gone," not fail halfway through a batch). Returns each removed row's `thumbnail_path`/`content_hash` (never the source path — removal must not even be *handed* the means to touch an original) so the command layer can clean up app-owned derived files after the DB transaction commits. **The test that matters most**: removing an image makes it re-importable again (`find_by_hash` stops matching) — that's the actual user-facing meaning of "non-destructive," not just "the row disappeared."
- **Command layer**: best-effort `fs::remove_file` on the thumbnail and the content-hash-keyed Develop preview PNG, after the catalog transaction commits, never fatal on failure. Preview deletion by content_hash is safe because import's dedupe guarantees no second row shares a hash. Named, accepted race (same class the project already accepts for preview-cache orphans): a background thumbnail/preview-generation pass snapshotted just before a removal can re-create a file for the now-gone image — an orphan on disk, self-healing in scope, rows can never be resurrected (those passes only ever UPDATE by image_id, which matches nothing post-delete).
- **Frontend selection model**: `selectedIds` (a `Set` of version_ids, reassigned immutably every change — Svelte 5's `$state` doesn't deep-proxy `Set`, so in-place mutation would silently not react) plus `selectedId` retained as the anchor for MetadataPanel/Shift-range/single-image concerns. Standard file-manager click semantics: plain click replaces + moves anchor, Cmd/Ctrl toggles, Shift selects the contiguous range in `images` order from the anchor (computed over the full array — `LibraryGrid`'s virtualization only slices what's *rendered*, never what's *selectable*).
- **Real type-contract break the review caught before it shipped**: `GridCell`'s keyboard Enter/Space path called `onSelect()` with no arguments — once `onSelect` grew an optional `MouseEvent` parameter (needed so modifier keys reach the page-level selection logic), that call site would have been the one thing `npm run check` actually flagged. Fixed by making the event parameter optional; keyboard selection is treated as a plain select.
- **Remove UX**: new `ConfirmDialog.svelte` (same overlay/Escape-gating shell as `ExportDialog`) plus a titlebar Remove button and a Delete/Backspace keyboard shortcut. The keyboard handler explicitly ignores the keypress when focus is inside an `<input>`/`<textarea>` — Backspace is a typing key in MetadataPanel's caption/copyright/contact fields, and without this guard, backspacing while editing a caption would have popped a remove-confirmation dialog. `document.activeElement?.blur()` fires before removal proceeds, mirroring `onCloseRequested`'s existing discipline, so an in-progress IPTC edit on a *surviving* image saves before a keyboard-driven removal on a different image can race it.
- **Batch export**: `ExportDialog`'s `item` prop became `items` (an array; `null` still means closed, callers guard against ever opening it with `[]`); the backend command and `export.js` wrapper needed **zero** changes, exactly as M1 Slice 5 anticipated. Result display is a summary line ("Exported N of M — first failure: ...") for multi-item exports, the existing single-line message preserved for the one-item case.
- **Verified empirically, not just via tests**: script-driven diagnostic runs against the real app (same technique as every prior slice) — multi-selected 2 real images (plain click + Cmd-click, exercising the real accumulation logic) and removed them: confirmed via `sqlite3` that both `images`/`image_versions` rows were gone with zero orphans, and via the filesystem that both thumbnail JPEGs and both cached preview PNGs were actually deleted from `<app_data>`. Then re-imported the same two files and confirmed `imported: 2` (not `skipped_duplicates`) — the non-destructive-removal claim holding up for real, not just in a unit test. Then batch-exported both into a fresh directory and confirmed two real, correctly-dimensioned JPEGs landed on disk with no errors.
- `cargo test --lib`: 44/44 passing (2 new: atomic dual-row removal + re-importability, batch no-op-on-unknown-id). `npm run check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings.

## Bug fix: JPEG thumbnails stayed blank forever after import (2026-07-27)

User report, tested and root-caused rather than assumed. Symptom: after importing a JPEG, its Library grid cell stayed a blank placeholder indefinitely, even well after import finished.

- **Metadata was not actually broken** — checked first since the user's report bundled it with the thumbnail symptom. Ran the real extraction path against a real Sony NEX-6 JPEG (copied from a real photo on this machine, not synthetic) both directly (`extract_from_jpeg`) and through the full `import_files` → catalog round trip: camera/lens/exposure/captured-at all landed correctly and immediately. Cell selection wiring (`GridCell` → `LibraryGrid` → `selectedId` → `MetadataPanel`) was also confirmed correct by inspection. Most likely explanation for "no metadata" in the original report: the same blank-looking cell probably discouraged clicking into it, or the user's own test JPEG genuinely had no EXIF (common for downloaded/screenshotted images) — not a code bug.
- **The thumbnail symptom was real, and root-caused, not just patched around.** JPEG thumbnail generation is a fire-and-forget background pass (`import::generate_missing_thumbnails`, M2 Slice 1) that runs *after* the import command already returned its response — by design, so a JPEG-heavy import doesn't block on it. But `+page.svelte` only ever calls `refresh()` (→ `listImages()`) once, right after import (and once more at startup) — nothing ever told it to look again once the background pass actually finished. So the frontend's one-shot snapshot permanently showed `thumbnail_path: null` for the rest of the session, regardless of how long the background pass took or whether it succeeded.
- **Confirmed via a real reproduction, not just code reading**: imported a real 8MB/4000×2658 JPEG through the actual `import_files` command inside the running app, captured the frontend's `images` state immediately after import (thumbnail_path: null, as expected) and again via a direct backend query 4s later (still null) — proving the staleness wasn't just theoretical. A follow-up direct-Rust timing test found the actual cause of *that specific* run's failure: in this project's unoptimized dev build, decoding+resizing a real photo this size took **4.83s**, longer than the 4s window before the app closed — the background `spawn_blocking` task gets abandoned along with the process on quit, so a thumbnail generation that hasn't finished by the time the app closes is lost until the next startup catch-up pass. (Release builds should be dramatically faster since JPEG decode is CPU-bound and debug/dev builds are unoptimized — but the underlying staleness bug would still show up as a delay-then-never-updates symptom in *any* build, just a shorter delay.)
- **Fix**: `+page.svelte` gained `pollUntilThumbnailsReady()` — after any `refresh()` that could have left `thumbnail_path: null` rows (post-import, and the initial startup refresh, which races the app's own startup catch-up pass the same way), it polls `listImages()` every 1.5s (capped at 10 attempts, ~15s total) until no currently-tracked image has a null thumbnail. Bounded deliberately — a genuinely-failed decode (a real, permanent error) shouldn't poll forever; it just stops and leaves the placeholder, which is the correct outcome in that case.
- **Verified the fix actually works, not just that it compiles**: re-ran the same real-photo import through the running app, this time letting `runImport`'s own new polling logic run for real (not artificially closing early) — the frontend's own `images` state went from `thumbnail_path: null` immediately after import to the real generated thumbnail path ~4–8s later, entirely on its own, no manual refresh or restart needed.
- `cargo test --lib`: 42/42 passing (no test changes needed — this was a frontend-only fix, the backend's background-generation behavior was already correct and already covered).

## M2 Slice 2 — Full metadata (EXIF + IPTC): DONE (2026-07-27)

Planned via a reviewed design doc — the review corrected a real premise error (JPEG metadata extraction and `jpeg_decode.rs`'s existing EXIF-orientation read are NOT the same parse and don't share one `exif::Reader` call — they run at different times, at import vs. in the background thumbnail pass) and flagged a real regression risk (IPTC's save-on-blur pattern reintroducing the exact "lose an edit on window close" bug class M1 Slice 6 fixed for the Develop edit stack), both incorporated below. **Scope cut, named explicitly**: EXIF + IPTC only. Embedded color-profile reading/application is ADR-0004's own already-flagged, still-unwired, highest-risk item — genuinely separate scope, deferred to its own future slice rather than bundled in here.
